@article{akkepalliCopulaEntropyRegularization2025,
  title = {Copula Entropy Regularization Transformer with {{C}} 2 Variational Autoencoder and Fine-Tuned Hybrid {{DL}} Model for Network Intrusion Detection},
  author = {Akkepalli, Srinivas and K, Sagar},
  year = 2025,
  month = mar,
  journal = {Telematics and Informatics Reports},
  volume = {17},
  pages = {100182},
  issn = {27725030},
  doi = {10.1016/j.teler.2024.100182},
  urldate = {2026-02-17},
  abstract = {In cyber security, Intrusion Detection Systems (IDS) act as a network security tool, in which computational complexity and dynamic IDS detection issues are observed by conventional studies. In this paper, a novel Copula Entropy Regularization Transformer with variational autoencoder and Fine-tuned Hybrid Deep Learning (DL) model is introduced for Network Intrusion Detection. In previous IDSs studies, flow-based feature extraction techniques are concentrated, which is ineffective for detecting high-dimensional anomaly data. This work proposes a novel Copula Entropy Regularization Transformer with variational autoencoder for regularizing the feature extraction and feature selection, using a self-paced regularization mechanism. Recently, the pull towards IDS with Zero-Day (ZD) attacks gets increased, and the existing studies over it, possess high False-Negative Rates (FNR), leading to limited practical usage. For reducing the FNR and to improve the identification of ZD, a Fine-tuned Hybrid DL attack prediction model with deep Transudative Federated Transfer Learning (TFTL) is proposed. This gives out a map connection between known and zero-day attacks, data points for different dynamic network traffic, and classification of known and unknown network attacks. To investigate the ZD attack detection with the proposed model, it is validated in Python platform with Network Intrusion Detection dataset and the performance results show that the work gives better accuracy (98.54\%), F1-score (97.5\%), recall (97.302\%), precision (98.2\%) and detection rate of about 0.975\%, while the comparative results show that this approach achieves a comparatively low false positive rate of 0.1, yielding high detection rate, and high accuracy in predicting attacks.},
  langid = {english},
  note = {Based on the provided excerpts from the article, here are the answers to your questions:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Is the solution suitable for detecting zero-day attacks?} \textbf{Yes.} The article explicitly states that the proposed model is designed to improve the identification of \textbf{zero-day (ZD) attacks} and unknown network attacks,. It aims to reduce the False-Negative Rate (FNR) specifically associated with ZD attacks by tracing relationships between known and unknown attacks,.
\item \textbf{What training paradigm does it use and how can it be categorized?} The model uses a \textbf{hybrid} approach. It incorporates \textbf{unsupervised learning} through the Variational Autoencoder (VAE) for feature extraction,. For classification and adaptation to new threats, it utilizes \textbf{Deep Transductive Federated Transfer Learning (TFTL)},. The authors categorize their transfer learning specifically as \textbf{transductive}.
\item \textbf{Does the article address the handling of imbalanced datasets?} The article acknowledges the \textbf{class imbalance problem} in its literature review,. For the proposed model, it addresses this by utilizing \textbf{Randomized search CV} to reduce false positives and stabilize error estimation in cross-validation, which helps maintain accuracy across different attack types,. It also uses \textbf{Self-paced regularization} to select reliable samples and mitigate the impact of outliers.
\item \textbf{Does the article deal with the concept drift phenomenon?} The specific term "concept drift" is not used, but the authors address the concept by designing the system to handle \textbf{dynamic network traffic}, \textbf{dynamic attacks}, and \textbf{changing attack patterns},,. The model is intended to be \textbf{self-adaptive} to new network settings.
\item \textbf{Is interpretability considered an important aspect?} \textbf{No}, the article does not present interpretability as a central feature of the \emph{proposed} model. It mentions interpretability as a challenge only in the context of other models like Graph Neural Networks (GNN). Techniques like LIME or SHAP are not mentioned for the proposed architecture.
\item 
\par
\textbf{What data preprocessing steps are used?} The preprocessing steps mentioned include:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Windowing/Segmentation:} Splitting time-domain data into small segments using a fixed-length window.
\item \textbf{Shuffling:} Randomizing data items to create the training set.
\item \textbf{Matrix Transformation:} Transforming operation data into three \textbf{32 \texttimes{} 32-dimensional matrices}.
\item \textbf{Onehot Encoding:} Used for categorical data representation.
\item \textbf{Batch Normalization:} Used to stabilize the network and speed up learning,.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Which datasets were used or tested in the study?} The primary dataset used is the \textbf{Network Intrusion Detection (NID)} dataset (simulated military network environment),. The study also tests and compares the model against \textbf{CSE-CK-IDS2018}, \textbf{NSL-KDD}, and \textbf{UNSW-NB-15},.
\item \textbf{Is there any special feature engineering method applied?} \textbf{Yes.} The model uses a \textbf{C2 Variational Autoencoder} to extract flow-based, low-level spatial, and high-level temporal features,. Additionally, it applies \textbf{Copula Entropy with self-paced regularization} for feature selection and redundancy removal,. It also mentions \textbf{Deep packet inspection} features.
\item 
\par
\textbf{What type of model is presented?} It is a \textbf{Fine-tuned Hybrid Deep Learning (DL)} model. It combines several architectures:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Transformer} (specifically a Copula Entropy Regularization Transformer with strided multi-head attention),.
\item \textbf{Variational Autoencoder} (C2 VAE),.
\item \textbf{CNN} (Deep Euclidian Convolutional Neural Network),.
\item \textbf{Transfer Learning} (Deep Transductive Federated Transfer Learning),.

\end{itemize}

\item 
\par
\textbf{What loss function is used?} Several loss components are mentioned:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{VAE Loss:} A combination of reconstruction error and \textbf{Kullback--Leibler (KL) divergence},.
\item \textbf{Consensus Graph Loss (\$L\_z\$):} Used in the contractive autoencoder.
\item \textbf{Distortion and Discrepancy functions:} Used for the transfer learning optimization,.

\end{itemize}

\item \textbf{What training algorithm is applied?} The training involves \textbf{Deep Transductive Federated Transfer Learning} to map known attacks to zero-day attacks,. It also uses an \textbf{iterative self-paced learning algorithm} to manage outliers and \textbf{Randomized Search CV} for hyper-parameter tuning.

\end{itemize}

\par
\textbf{Note on accessibility:} This information is derived from the provided excerpts of the article; the full text (all chapters and appendices) was not provided beyond these specific sections, but the excerpts contained detailed technical information regarding the methodology and results.},

@article{aktarDDoSAttackDetection2023,
  title = {Towards {{DDoS}} Attack Detection Using Deep Learning Approach},
  author = {Aktar, Sharmin and Yasin Nur, Abdullah},

  journal = {Computers \& Security},
  volume = {129},
  pages = {103251},
  issn = {01674048},

  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {Due to the extensive use and evolution in the cyber world, different network attacks have recently increased significantly. Distributed Denial-of-Service (DDoS) attack has become one of the fatal threats to the Internet, where attackers send massive amounts of packets to the target system to make online systems unavailable to legitimate users. Proper attack detection measurement is crucial to defend against these attacks. This paper proposes a deep learning-based model using a contractive autoencoder to detect anomalies. We train our model to learn the normal traffic pattern from the compacted representation of the input data, and then apply a stochastic threshold method to detect the attack. Three renowned Intrusion Detection System datasets have been used for evaluationCIC-IDS2017, NSL-KDD, and CIC-DDoS2019. We have assessed the results against a basic autoencoder and other deep learning approaches to show our model efficacy. Our results indicate a successful intrusion detection of the proposed method with an accuracy ranging between 93.41\% and 97.58\% on the CIC-DDoS2019 dataset. Moreover, it achieved an accuracy of 96.08\% and 92.45\% on NSL-KDD and CIC-IDS2017 datasets, respectively.},

\item \textbf{Milyen tanul\'asi paradigm\'at haszn\'al?} A modell \textbf{f\'elig fel\"ugyelt (semi-supervised) tanul\'asi paradigm\'at} haszn\'al. Ez azt jelenti, hogy a tan\'it\'as sor\'an kiz\'ar\'olag norm\'al (nem t\'amad\'asi) mint\'akat haszn\'alnak fel a h\'al\'ozati forgalom mint\'azat\'anak elsaj\'at\'it\'as\'ahoz.

\begin{enumerate}

\item A kateg\'oriav\'altoz\'ok (pl. protokoll t\'ipus) \textbf{one-hot k\'odol\'asa}.
\item A bin\'aris oszt\'alyc\'imk\'ek numerikus k\'odol\'asa (0 \'es 1).
\item A \textbf{duplik\'alt rekordok} elt\'avol\'it\'asa a torz\'it\'as megel\H oz\'es\'ere.

A b\"untet\H o tag a k\'odol\'o aktiv\'aci\'oinak a bemenetre vonatkoz\'o \textbf{Jacobian-m\'atrix\'anak Frobenius-norm\'aja}.

@article{alamDataDrivenNetworkAnalysis2023,
  title = {Data-{{Driven Network Analysis}} for {{Anomaly Traffic Detection}}},
  author = {Alam, Shumon and Alam, Yasin and Cui, Suxia and Akujuobi, Cajetan},
  year = 2023,
  month = sep,
  journal = {Sensors},
  volume = {23},
  number = {19},
  pages = {8174},
  issn = {1424-8220},
  doi = {10.3390/s23198174},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {Cybersecurity is a critical issue in today's internet world. Classical security systems, such as firewalls based on signature detection, cannot detect today's sophisticated zero-day attacks. Machine learning (ML) based solutions are more attractive for their capabilities of detecting anomaly traffic from benign traffic, but to develop an ML-based anomaly detection system, we need meaningful or realistic network datasets to train the detection engine. There are many public network datasets for ML applications. Still, they have limitations, such as the data creation process and the lack of diverse attack scenarios or background traffic. To create a good detection engine, we need a realistic dataset with various attack scenarios and various types of background traffic, such as HTTPs, streaming, and SMTP traffic. In this work, we have developed realistic network data or datasets considering various attack scenarios and diverse background/benign traffic. Furthermore, considering the importance of distributed denial of service (DDoS) attacks, we have compared the performance of detecting anomaly traffic of some classical supervised and our prior developed unsupervised ML algorithms based on the convolutional neural network (CNN) and pseudo auto-encoder (AE) architecture based on the created datasets. The results show that the performance of the CNN-Pseudo-AE is comparable to that of many classical supervised algorithms. Hence, the CNN-Pseudo-AE algorithm is promising in actual implementation.},
  langid = {english},
  note = {Az al\'abbi v\'alaszok az "Alam \'es mtsai. - 2023 - Data-Driven Network Analysis for Anomaly Traffic Detection" c\'im\H u cikk tartalma \'es metaadatai alapj\'an k\'esz\"ultek:

Az \'altaluk javasolt \textbf{fel\"ugyelet n\'elk\"uli (unsupervised)} modell k\"ul\"on\"osen alkalmas erre, mivel nem ig\'enyel el\H ore meghat\'arozott c\'imk\'eket az ismeretlen mint\'ak felismer\'es\'ehez.
\item \textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'asi paradigm\'at haszn\'al, \'es hogyan kategoriz\'alhat\'o?} A cikk mind a \textbf{fel\"ugyelt (supervised)}, mind a \textbf{fel\"ugyelet n\'elk\"uli (unsupervised)} tanul\'asi paradigm\'akkal foglalkozik. A szerz\H ok \"osszehasonl\'itj\'ak a klasszikus fel\"ugyelt algoritmusokat (pl. GNB, RF, MLP) az \'altaluk fejlesztett \textbf{CNN-Pseudo-AE} modellel, amely egy \textbf{fel\"ugyelet n\'elk\"uli m\'elytanul\'asi} architekt\'ura.

B\'ar a saj\'at \textbf{PVAMU-DDoS2020} adathalmazuk eloszl\'as\'at (67,33\% t\'amad\'as, 32,67\% benignus) r\"ogz\'itik, a sz\"oveg nem r\'eszletez specifikus algoritmus-szint\H u technik\'at (mint a SMOTE) az egyens\'ulyhi\'any kezel\'es\'ere.

\par
\textbf{Milyen adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi (preprocessing) l\'ep\'eseket haszn\'alnak?} A cikk a k\"ovetkez\H o l\'ep\'eseket sorolja fel:

@article{alkasassbehSelfAdaptiveIntrusionDetection2025,
  title = {A {{Self-Adaptive Intrusion Detection System}} for {{Zero-Day Attacks Using Deep Q-Networks}}},
  author = {Alkasassbeh, Mouhammd and Omoush, Ebtehal H. and Almseidin, Mohammad and Aldweesh, Amjad},
  year = 2025,
  journal = {IEEE Access},
  volume = {13},
  pages = {174280--174296},
  issn = {2169-3536},
  doi = {10.1109/ACCESS.2025.3617792},
  urldate = {2026-03-04},
  abstract = {Zero-day attacks remain among the most formidable threats to modern cybersecurity, exploiting undisclosed vulnerabilities that bypass conventional detection mechanisms. In this paper, we propose a self-learning intrusion detection system (IDS) based on Deep Q-Networks (DQNs), a reinforcement learning approach capable of autonomously classifying network traffic without dependence on predefined signatures or labeled training data. Leveraging the UGRansome dataset, the model is evaluated under two experimental scenarios: a conventional random split and a more rigorous zero-day split, where ransomware families in the test set are entirely excluded from training. In binary classification, the proposed system achieves 97.6\% accuracy (F1-score: 0.98) in the random split and 95.9\% accuracy (F1-score: 0.96) in the zero-day setup, consistently prioritizing high recall for ransomware detection. In multiclass classification, it attains 97.0\% and 92.0\% accuracy in the random and zero-day splits, respectively. These results underscore the potential of reinforcement learning as a robust and adaptive foundation for real-time intrusion detection in evolving threat landscapes.},
  copyright = {https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/legalcode},
  note = {Az al\'abbi v\'alaszok a forr\'ask\'ent megadott cikk tartalm\'an alapulnak:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Alkalmass\'ag zero-day t\'amad\'asok detekt\'al\'as\'ara:} Igen, a bemutatott megold\'as alkalmas zero-day t\'amad\'asok \'eszlel\'es\'ere. A rendszer k\'epes auton\'om m\'odon oszt\'alyozni a h\'al\'ozati forgalmat an\'elk\"ul, hogy el\H ore meghat\'arozott szignat\'ur\'akra vagy c\'imk\'ezett tan\'it\'oadatokra t\'amaszkodna. A k\'is\'erletek sor\'an egy speci\'alis ,,zero-day split'' be\'all\'it\'ast haszn\'altak, ahol a tesztk\'eszletben szerepl\H o zsarol\'oprogram-csal\'adokat teljesen kihagyt\'ak a tan\'it\'asb\'ol, \'es a modell \'igy is 95,9\%-os pontoss\'agot \'ert el.
\item \textbf{Tan\'it\'asi paradigma \'es kateg\'oria:} A cikk a \textbf{meger\H os\'it\'eses tanul\'ast (reinforcement learning - RL)} alkalmazza, azon bel\"ul is a \textbf{Deep Q-Networks (DQN)} algoritmust. A rendszert ,,\"ontanul\'o'' (self-learning) \'es ,,\"onadapt\'iv'' (self-adaptive) IDS-k\'ent kategoriz\'alj\'ak.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Kiegyens\'ulyozatlan adathalmazok kezel\'ese:} Igen, a cikk foglalkozik ezzel a k\'erd\'essel. A felhaszn\'alt UGRansome adathalmaz eleve kiegyens\'ulyozatlan. A kezel\'es\'ere k\'et f\H o m\'odszert eml\'itenek:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item Az adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'es sor\'an \textbf{r\'etegzett mintav\'etelt (stratified sampling)} alkalmaztak az oszt\'alyok egyens\'uly\'anak fenntart\'asa \'erdek\'eben a tan\'it\'o- \'es tesztk\'eszletekben.
\item A meger\H os\'it\'eses tanul\'asi k\"ornyezetben \textbf{aszimmetrikus jutalmaz\'asi f\"uggv\'enyt (asymmetric reward function)} haszn\'altak, amely kiemelten b\"unteti a t\'eves negat\'ivokat (false negatives), ami kritikus a zsarol\'oprogramok elleni v\'edekez\'esben.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Concept drift (koncepci\'ov\'alt\'as) jelens\'ege:} A forr\'asok nem eml\'itik n\'ev szerint a ,,concept drift'' kifejez\'est. Ugyanakkor hangs\'ulyozz\'ak a modell \textbf{alkalmazkod\'ok\'epess\'eg\'et} a v\'altoz\'o h\'al\'ozati viselked\'esekhez \'es a folyamatosan fejl\H od\H o fenyegetetts\'egi k\"ornyezethez.
\item \textbf{\'Ertelmezhet\H os\'eg (Interpretability):} Igen, az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eget fontos szempontnak tartj\'ak a val\'os alkalmaz\'asokn\'al. A szerz\H ok olyan ut\'olagos (post-hoc) technik\'ak alkalmaz\'as\'at javasolj\'ak, mint a \textbf{jellemz\H o-fontoss\'agi elemz\'es (feature importance analysis), a LIME vagy a SHAP}.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'esek:} A cikk az al\'abbi l\'ep\'eseket sorolja fel:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Hi\'anyos rekordok elt\'avol\'it\'asa} az adatmin\H os\'eg biztos\'it\'asa \'erdek\'eben.
\item \textbf{Magas kardinalit\'as\'u jellemz\H ok kiiktat\'asa} (pl. IP-c\'imek) a t\'ultanul\'as elker\"ul\'ese v\'egett.
\item \textbf{Kategorikus attrib\'utumok \'atalak\'it\'asa} numerikus form\'atumba \textbf{c\'imkek\'odol\'assal (label encoding)}.
\item \textbf{R\'etegzett mintav\'etel} az oszt\'alyeloszl\'as egyens\'uly\'a\'ert.
\item \textbf{Normaliz\'al\'as Min-Max sk\'al\'az\'assal} a tartom\'anyba.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Felhaszn\'alt adathalmazok:} A tanulm\'any els\H osorban az \textbf{UGRansome} adathalmazt haszn\'alta a modell \'ert\'ekel\'es\'ehez.
\item \textbf{Speci\'alis feature engineering:} A forr\'asok nem r\'eszleteznek speci\'alis, manu\'alis jellemz\H o-kinyer\'esi m\'odszert a fent eml\'itett el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'eseken (magas kardinalit\'as\'u elemek t\"orl\'ese, k\'odol\'as) k\'iv\"ul. A DQN modell m\'ely neur\'alis h\'al\'ozata maga v\'egzi a jellemz\H ok kinyer\'es\'et.
\item \textbf{Modellt\'ipus:} A bemutatott modell egy \textbf{Deep Q-Network (DQN)}, amely a \textbf{m\'ely tanul\'ast (deep learning)} \'es a meger\H os\'it\'eses tanul\'ast \"otv\"ozi. Architekt\'ur\'aj\'at tekintve egy t\"obbr\'eteg\H u, teljes m\'ert\'ekben \"osszekapcsolt neur\'alis h\'al\'ozatr\'ol (MLP) van sz\'o.
\item \textbf{Vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'eny (Loss function):} A tan\'it\'as sor\'an az el\H orejelzett \'es a c\'el Q-\'ert\'ekek k\"oz\"otti \textbf{\'atlagos n\'egyzetes hib\'at (Mean Squared Error - MSE)} haszn\'alt\'ak vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyk\'ent.
\item \textbf{Tan\'it\'asi algoritmus:} A specifikus \textbf{DQN tan\'it\'asi algoritmust} alkalmazt\'ak (Algorithm 1), amely olyan technik\'akat tartalmaz, mint a \textbf{tapasztalat-\'ujraj\'atsz\'as (experience replay)} egy puffer seg\'its\'eg\'evel, a \textbf{c\'elh\'al\'ozat (target network)} haszn\'alata a stabilit\'as\'ert, valamint az \textbf{\$\textbackslash epsilon\$-greedy felfedez\'esi strat\'egia}.

\end{itemize}

This paper explores the integration of sophisticated machine learning techniques and feature selection methods to enhance the performance of Network Intrusion Detection Systems. Focusing on the complex landscape of cyber threats, accentuated by the proliferation of technologies such as Internet of Things, 5G, and cloud computing, the proposed study evaluates the application of three advanced feature selection algorithms---Grey Wolf Optimizer, Bat Algorithm, and Pigeon-inspired Optimization---to identify an optimal subset of features that accurately differentiate between diverse cyberattacks and normal network traffic. Employing the comprehensive CSE-CIC-IDS2018 dataset, the experimental results demonstrate that the feature set was successfully reduced from 80 to subsets of 10, 6, and 7 features while maintaining a high detection accuracy close to 99\%. This reduction in feature space significantly decreases computational overhead without compromising detection capability. This research contributes to the cybersecurity domain by presenting a scalable, efficient, and highly accurate model for intrusion detection, setting a foundation for future advancements in Network Intrusion Detection Systems optimization and the broader field of cyber defense mechanisms.},

@article{arafahAnomalybasedNetworkIntrusion2025,
  title = {Anomaly-Based Network Intrusion Detection Using Denoising Autoencoder and {{Wasserstein GAN}} Synthetic Attacks},
  author = {Arafah, Mohammad and Phillips, Iain and Adnane, Asma and Hadi, Wael and Alauthman, Mohammad and {Al-Banna}, Abedal-Kareem},
  year = 2025,
  month = jan,
  journal = {Applied Soft Computing},
  volume = {168},
  pages = {112455},
  issn = {15684946},

  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {Intrusion detection systems face challenges in handling high-dimensional, large-scale, and imbalanced network traffic data. This paper proposes a novel architecture combining a denoising autoencoder (AE) and a Wasserstein generative adversarial network (WGAN) to address these issues. The AE-WGAN model extracts high-representative features and generates realistic synthetic attacks, effectively resolving data imbalance and enhancing anomaly-based intrusion detection. Extensive experiments on NSL-KDD and CICIDS-2017 datasets, using both binary and multiclass classification scenarios with various classifier architectures, demonstrate the model's superior performance. The proposed approach outperforms state-of-the-art models in accuracy, precision, recall, and F1 score, showing excellent generalization capabilities against unseen attacks. Time complexity analysis reveals computational efficiency while maintaining high-quality synthetic attack generation. This research contributes a robust, efficient, and adaptable framework for intrusion detection, capable of handling modern network traffic complexities and evolving cyber threats.},

A szintetikus t\'amad\'asok gener\'al\'as\'a\'ert felel\H os WGAN komponens \textbf{fel\"ugyelet n\'elk\"uli (unsupervised)} tanul\'asi t\'ipusba tartozik. Olyan technik\'akat, mint a LIME, nem alkalmaztak.

\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Adattiszt\'it\'as}: A konstans \'es kv\'azi-konstans jellemz\H ok elt\'avol\'it\'asa.
\item \textbf{Ordin\'alis k\'odol\'as (Encoding)}: A nem numerikus jellemz\H ok sz\'amm\'a alak\'it\'asa.
\item \textbf{Adatsk\'al\'az\'as (Scaling)}: Min-max normaliz\'aci\'o haszn\'alata.

@article{arafahEnhancedBiGANArchitecture2025,
  title = {An Enhanced {{BiGAN}} Architecture for Network Intrusion Detection},
  author = {Arafah, Mohammad and Phillips, Iain and Adnane, Asma and Alauthman, Mohammad and Aslam, Nauman},
  year = 2025,
  month = apr,
  journal = {Knowledge-Based Systems},
  volume = {314},
  pages = {113178},
  issn = {09507051},

  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {Intrusion detection systems face significant challenges in handling high-dimensional, large-scale, and imbalanced network traffic data. This paper proposes a new architecture combining a denoising autoencoder (AE) and a Wasserstein Generative Adversarial Network (WGAN) to address these challenges. The AE-WGAN model extracts high-representative features and generates realistic synthetic attacks, effectively resolving data imbalance and enhancing anomaly-based intrusion detection. Our extensive experiments on NSL-KDD and CICIDS-2017 datasets demonstrate superior performance, achieving 98\% accuracy and 99\% F1-score in binary classification, surpassing recent approaches by 7\%--15\%. In multiclass cases, the model achieves 89\% precision for DoS attacks and 84\% for Probe attacks, while maintaining 79\% precision for rare U2R attacks. Time complexity analysis reveals 23\% reduced training time while maintaining high-quality synthetic attack generation, contributing a robust framework capable of handling modern network traffic complexities and evolving cyber threats.},

\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Is the solution suitable for detecting zero-day attacks?} \textbf{Yes.} The article describes an Anomaly-based Intrusion Detection System (AIDS) designed to identify deviations from normal behavior, which makes it highly effective at detecting \textbf{unknown threats}, including emerging attacks that lack predefined signatures. Furthermore, the authors note that investigating its adaptability to zero-day detection specifically is a promising direction for future work.
\item \textbf{What training paradigm does it use, and how can it be categorized?} The core \textbf{E-BiGAN model uses an unsupervised learning paradigm} to learn the distribution of network traffic and generate synthetic samples. However, the overall framework is a hybrid; the \textbf{final Intrusion Detection System (AIDS) uses a supervised learning approach}, as it is trained on a balanced dataset of labeled original and synthetic samples to perform classification.
\item \textbf{Does the article address the handling of imbalanced datasets? If yes, how?} \textbf{Yes.} The article specifically addresses data imbalance by using the \textbf{E-BiGAN to generate high-quality synthetic attack samples}. These synthetic samples are added to the original dataset to create a balanced set for training the detection model, thereby reducing bias toward majority classes.
\item \textbf{Does the article deal with the concept drift phenomenon?} The provided excerpts \textbf{do not explicitly mention the term "concept drift."} However, the authors state that the system is designed to handle "evolving cyber threats" and the "complexities of modern network traffic".
\item \textbf{Is interpretability considered an important aspect?} Interpretability is mentioned only as a \textbf{general challenge and limitation} of deep learning models in the field of cybersecurity. The sources \textbf{do not mention the application of specific interpretability techniques} like LIME or SHAP to the proposed model.
\item 
\par
\textbf{What data preprocessing steps are used?} The preprocessing involves the following steps:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Labeling}: Assigning "1" to normal traffic and "0" to attack classes.
\item \textbf{Feature Removal}: Eliminating constant features to reduce computation and avoid overfitting.
\item \textbf{Scaling and Encoding}: Applying a \textbf{Min-Max scalar} for numeric features and an \textbf{ordinal encoder} for categorical features.
\item \textbf{Feature Selection}: Using the \textbf{Analysis of Variance (ANOVA)} method to select the most relevant features.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Which datasets were used or tested in the study?} The study utilized the \textbf{NSL-KDD} and \textbf{CICIDS-2017} datasets.
\item \textbf{Is there any special feature engineering method applied?} \textbf{Yes.} The architecture uses a \textbf{dual encoder design} for parallel feature extraction to capture richer representations in a lower-dimensional latent space. Additionally, \textbf{ANOVA} is used as a specific statistical method for feature selection.
\item \textbf{What type of model is presented?} The article presents a \textbf{deep learning} model called \textbf{Enhanced BiGAN (E-BiGAN)}. It features parallel \textbf{autoencoders} and a weight-sharing mechanism. The resulting data is then used to train classifiers based on \textbf{RNN, LSTM, and GRU} architectures.
\item \textbf{What loss function is used?} The E-BiGAN uses an \textbf{enhanced minimax loss function}. This objective includes components for the dual generators, discriminators, and encoders, integrating parallel autoencoder loss to facilitate better pattern representation.
\item \textbf{What training algorithm is applied?} The model is trained using \textbf{backpropagation}. The weights are optimized using the \textbf{Adam optimizer} (with specific parameters like a learning rate of 0.0009) for the E-BiGAN.

\end{itemize}

@article{ballaEnhancedCNNLSTMDeep2024,
  title = {Enhanced {{CNN-LSTM Deep Learning}} for {{SCADA IDS Featuring Hurst Parameter Self-Similarity}}},
  author = {Balla, Asaad and Habaebi, Mohamed Hadi and Elsheikh, Elfatih A. A. and Islam, Md. Rafiqul and Suliman, Fakher Eldin Mohamed and Mubarak, Sinil},
  year = 2024,
  journal = {IEEE Access},
  volume = {12},
  pages = {6100--6116},
  issn = {2169-3536},
  doi = {10.1109/ACCESS.2024.3350978},
  urldate = {2025-06-13},
  abstract = {Supervisory Control and Data Acquisition (SCADA) systems are crucial for modern industrial processes and securing them against increasing cyber threats is a significant challenge. This study presents an advanced method for bolstering SCADA security by employing a modified hybrid deep learning model. A key innovation in this work is integrating the Self-similarity Hurst parameter into the dataset alongside a CNN-LSTM model, significantly boosting the Intrusion Detection System's (IDS) capabilities. The Hurst parameter, which quantifies the self-similarity in a dataset, is instrumental in detecting anomalies. Our in-depth analysis of the CICIDS2017 dataset sheds light on contemporary attack patterns and network traffic behaviors. The CNN-LSTM architecture was substantially altered by adding multiple convolutional layers with progressively increasing filters, batch normalization for stable training, and dropout layers for regularization. Principal Component Analysis (PCA) was applied for dimensionality reduction, thereby optimizing the dataset. Test results demonstrate the superior performance of the model incorporating the Hurst parameter, achieving 95.21\% accuracy and 82.59\% recall, significantly surpassing the standard model. The inclusion of the Hurst parameter marks a substantial advancement in identifying emerging threats, while architectural improvements to the CNN-LSTM model led to more robust and accurate intrusion detection in industrial control settings.},
  keywords = {Anomaly detection,binary classification,Convolutional neural networks,Data models,Deep learning,Hurst parameter,Intrusion detection,intrusion detection system,SCADA systems,Security,self-similarity,supervisory control and data acquisition},

B\'ar a "zero-day" kifejez\'es nem szerepel explicit m\'odon, a sz\"oveg hangs\'ulyozza, hogy a modell k\'epes azonos\'itani azokat az anom\'ali\'akat \'es elt\'er\'eseket, amelyek szokatlan h\'al\'ozati esem\'enyekre utalnak, \'es amelyek az ismert szignat\'ur\'akkal nem rendelkez\H o t\'amad\'asok jellemz\H oi,,.
\item \textbf{Milyen tanul\'asi paradigm\'at haszn\'al, \'es hogyan kategoriz\'alhat\'o?} A kutat\'as \textbf{fel\"ugyelt tanul\'asi (supervised learning)} paradigm\'at alkalmaz. A modellt a \textbf{CICIDS2017} adatk\'eszleten tan\'itj\'ak, amely c\'imk\'ezett adatokat tartalmaz (pl. 'Benign' \'es k\"ul\"onb\"oz\H o t\'amad\'asi kateg\'ori\'ak),. A folyamat \textbf{bin\'aris oszt\'alyoz\'ask\'ent} van meghat\'arozva, ahol a h\'al\'ozati esem\'enyeket norm\'al vagy anom\'alis kateg\'ori\'akba sorolj\'ak,.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk a kiegyens\'ulyozatlan adatk\'eszletek (imbalanced datasets) kezel\'es\'evel?} \textbf{Nem}, a szerz\H ok tudatosan \'ugy d\"ont\"ottek, hogy ebben a tanulm\'anyban \textbf{nem foglalkoznak az adatok egyens\'ulyhi\'any\'aval}. Ennek oka, hogy el akart\'ak k\"ul\"on\'iteni a Hurst-param\'eter (Self-similarity feature) hozz\'aj\'arul\'as\'at a modell teljes\'itm\'eny\'ehez. Megjegyzik ugyanakkor, hogy a j\"ov\H obeni kutat\'asaikban tervezik ennek a hat\'asnak a vizsg\'alat\'at.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk a concept drift jelens\'eggel?} A cikk \textbf{nem haszn\'alja a "concept drift" kifejez\'est}, de foglalkozik a \textbf{"v\'altoz\'o anom\'ali\'ak" (evolving anomalies)} \'es a \textbf{"fejl\H od\H o cyber fenyeget\'esek"} \'eszlel\'es\'evel,. Megold\'ask\'ent a \textbf{Self-similarity Hurst-param\'eter} integr\'al\'as\'at javasolj\'ak, amely k\'epes k\"ovetni az id\H osoros adatokban bek\"ovetkez\H o v\'altoz\'asokat \'es hossz\'u t\'av\'u trendeket,.
\item \textbf{Fontos szempont-e az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg (interpretability)? Milyen technik\'akat haszn\'alnak?} \textbf{Igen}, a cikk t\'argyalja a modell \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg\'et, k\"ul\"on\"osen a \textbf{PCA (Principal Component Analysis) komponensek elemz\'es\'en kereszt\"ul},. A szerz\H ok r\'eszletezik, hogy mely h\'al\'ozati jellemz\H ok (pl. Flow IAT Max, Packet counts) befoly\'asolj\'ak legink\'abb az egyes f\H okomponenseket, \'igy t\'eve \'erthet\H obb\'e a modell d\"ont\'eseit,. Olyan technik\'akat, mint a LIME vagy a SHAP, nem eml\'itenek; a SMOTE-ot pedig csak a kapcsol\'od\'o munk\'ak (Related Work) szakaszban eml\'itik m\'as kutat\'asok kapcs\'an, de \H ok maguk nem alkalmazz\'ak.

\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Outlier (kiugr\'o \'ert\'ek) detekt\'al\'as \'es elt\'avol\'it\'as}: A \textbf{Z-score m\'odszer} alkalmaz\'as\'aval,.

Az architekt\'ura Conv1D, Max-Pooling, LSTM, Dense \'es Dropout r\'etegekb\H ol \'all.

\item \textbf{Stabiliz\'aci\'o}: \textbf{Batch Normalization (BN)} a tan\'it\'as gyors\'it\'as\'ara \'es stabiliz\'al\'as\'ara,.
\item \textbf{Hiperparam\'eterek}: 128-as batch size, 10 epoch, ReLU aktiv\'aci\'os f\"uggv\'eny a rejtett r\'etegekben \'es Sigmoid a kimeneti r\'etegben,.

\end{itemize}

@article{bensaidCNNBiLSTMHybridDeep2023,
  title = {{{CNN-BiLSTM}}: {{A Hybrid Deep Learning Approach}} for {{Network Intrusion Detection System}} in {{Software-Defined Networking With Hybrid Feature Selection}}},
  shorttitle = {{{CNN-BiLSTM}}},
  author = {Ben Said, Rachid and Sabir, Zakaria and Askerzade, Iman},
  year = 2023,
  journal = {IEEE Access},
  volume = {11},
  pages = {138732--138747},
  issn = {2169-3536},
  doi = {10.1109/ACCESS.2023.3340142},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {A Software-Defined Network (SDN) was designed to simplify network management by allowing the control and management of the entire network from a single place. SDN is commonly used in today's data center network infrastructure, but new forms of threats such as Distributed Denial-of-Service (DDoS), web attacks, and the U2R (User to Root) attack are significant issues that might restrict the widespread adoption of SDNs. Intruders are attractive to SDN controllers because they are valuable targets. An SDN controller can be hijacked by an attacker and used to route traffic in accordance with its own needs, resulting in catastrophic consequences for the whole network. While the unified vision of SDN and deep learning methods opens new possibilities for the security of IDS deployment, the effectiveness of the detection models is dependent on the quality of the training datasets. Even though deep learning for NIDSs has lately shown promising results for a number of issues, the majority of the studies overlooked the impact of data redundancy and an unbalanced dataset. As a consequence, this may adversely affect the resilience of the anomaly detection system, resulting in a suboptimal model performance. In this study, we created a hybrid Convolutional Neural Network (CNN) and bidirectional long short-term memory (BiLSTM) network to enhance network intrusion detection using binary and multiclass classification. The effectiveness of the proposed model was tested and assessed using the most frequently used datasets (UNSW-NB15 and NSL-KDD). In addition, we used the InSDN dataset, which is specifically dedicated to SDN. The outcomes demonstrate the efficiency of the proposed model in achieving high accuracy and requiring less training time.},
  copyright = {https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-nc-nd/4.0/},
  note = {Based on the provided excerpts and metadata of the article, here are the answers to your questions:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Is the solution suitable for detecting zero-day attacks?} \textbf{Yes}, the authors present their solution as an \textbf{anomaly detection system}. While the term "zero-day" is explicitly highlighted in their discussion of related work, they state that their own model is designed to efficiently detect anomalies and maintains a high capacity for identifying \textbf{unseen data}.
\item \textbf{What training paradigm does it use and how can it be categorized?} The model uses a \textbf{supervised learning} paradigm. This is evidenced by the use of \textbf{labeled datasets} (where traffic is categorized into specific attack types or "normal") and the splitting of data into explicit training (70\%) and testing (30\%) sets.
\item \textbf{Does the article address the handling of imbalanced datasets?} \textbf{Yes.} The authors acknowledge that previous studies often overlooked the impact of unbalanced datasets. To address this, they used \textbf{random oversampling} to balance the classes by duplicating minority class samples.
\item \textbf{Does the article deal with the concept drift phenomenon?} \textbf{No.} The provided text \textbf{does not explicitly mention "concept drift"} or propose a specific solution for the phenomenon where the statistical properties of the target variable change over time.
\item \textbf{Is interpretability considered an important aspect?} \textbf{No}, interpretability (or "Explainable AI") is not treated as a primary goal or a highlighted feature of the proposed model in the excerpts provided. Techniques such as LIME or SHAP are not mentioned. Note that while \textbf{SMOTE} is mentioned in the "Related Work" section regarding a different study, the authors of this paper opted for random oversampling instead.
\item 
\par
\textbf{What data preprocessing steps are used?} The article lists the following steps:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Dropping Rows and Missing Values:} Specifically removing the "id" column and ensuring data integrity.
\item \textbf{Categorical Encoding:} Using the \textbf{LabelEncoder} technique to convert attributes like "proto" and "service" into numeric representations.
\item \textbf{Data Balancing:} Applying \textbf{random oversampling} to handle imbalanced classes.
\item \textbf{Normalization:} Mapping feature values to a consistent ** interval** using a min-max formula.
\item \textbf{Numerical Processing:} Using a "hot encoding" approach to align symbol features to digital vectors.
\item \textbf{Matrix Generation:} Reforming 1D feature vectors into a \textbf{2D matrix} (e.g., 10 \texttimes{} 10) to make the data compatible with the CNN input.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Which datasets were used or tested in the study?} The authors used three datasets: \textbf{InSDN} (specifically dedicated to SDN), \textbf{NSL-KDD}, and \textbf{UNSW-NB15}.
\item \textbf{Is there any special feature engineering method applied?} \textbf{Yes.} The authors used a \textbf{Hybrid Feature Selection} approach. This involved using a \textbf{Random Forest (RF) classifier} to determine feature importance and \textbf{Recursive Feature Elimination (RFE)} to select the optimal subset of 10 highly important features.
\item \textbf{What type of model is presented?} The study presents a \textbf{hybrid deep learning} model combining a \textbf{Convolutional Neural Network (CNN)} for spatial feature extraction and a \textbf{Bidirectional Long Short-Term Memory (BiLSTM)} network for temporal feature extraction.
\item \textbf{What loss function is used?} The specific name of the loss function (e.g., Cross-Entropy) is \textbf{not explicitly stated} in the text. However, the authors mention using a \textbf{sigmoid layer} at the output to represent the likelihood of each input flow for classification.
\item \textbf{What training algorithm is applied?} The training process involves \textbf{k-fold cross-validation} (specifically 2-fold) with \textbf{20 epochs} per fold. They also used \textbf{Randomized Search} to tune hyperparameters and applied \textbf{L2 Regularization} and \textbf{Dropout} (probability 0.5) to prevent overfitting.

\end{itemize}

@article{bouguessaTBACIDSEnhancingIntrusion2025,
  title = {{{TBAC-IDS}}: Enhancing Intrusion Detection with Transformer-Based Alerts Correlation},
  shorttitle = {{{TBAC-IDS}}},
  author = {Bouguessa, Abdelkader and Mostefaoui, Sid Ahmed Mokhtar and Daoud, Mohamed Amine and Alem, Abdelkader and Mekroussi, Said and Maasakri, Moustafa and Hasan, Ahmed},
  year = 2025,
  month = dec,
  journal = {Cluster Computing},
  volume = {28},
  number = {16},
  pages = {1012},
  issn = {1386-7857, 1573-7543},
  doi = {10.1007/s10586-025-05716-z},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {Intrusion Detection Systems (IDS) play a vital role in cybersecurity by identifying and mitigating malicious activities in network traffic. However, the high volume of alerts often leads to false positives, alert fatigue, and missed threats. Conventional IDS methods detect isolated anomalies primarily, but fail to capture temporal and contextual relationships between alerts. To address these limitations, we introduce an IDS model that utilizes transformer-based encoder attention mechanisms. Inspired by advances in natural language processing, our approach dynamically prioritizes alerts based on context, enhancing the detection of critical threats and uncovering hidden attack patterns. This significantly reduces false positives while improving detection accuracy. By carefully tuning the model components such as the number of encoder layers, attention heads, batch size, and feedforward layer size, our model achieved a classification accuracy of 99.34\% with an error rate of just 0.55\% on the test data. These results highlight the effectiveness of encoder attention mechanisms in optimizing IDS performance, particularly when combined with dense neural layers, demonstrating the potential of Transformer-based models in developing more adaptive and intelligent IDS.},
  langid = {english},

@article{callegariRealTimeDeep2024,
  title = {A {{Real Time Deep Learning Based Approach}} for {{Detecting Network Attacks}}},
  author = {Callegari, Christian and Giordano, Stefano and Pagano, Michele},
  year = 2024,
  month = may,
  journal = {Big Data Research},
  volume = {36},
  pages = {100446},
  issn = {2214-5796},
  doi = {10.1016/j.bdr.2024.100446},
  urldate = {2025-06-12},
  abstract = {Anomaly-based Intrusion Detection is a key research topic in network security due to its ability to face unknown attacks and new security threats. For this reason, many works on the topic have been proposed in the last decade. Nonetheless, an ultimate solution, able to provide a high detection rate with an acceptable false alarm rate, has still to be identified. In the last years big research efforts have focused on the application of Deep Learning techniques to the field, but no work has been able, so far, to propose a system achieving good detection performance, while processing raw network traffic in real time. For this reason in the paper we propose an Intrusion Detection System that, leveraging on probabilistic data structures and Deep Learning techniques, is able to process in real time the traffic collected in a backbone network, offering excellent detection performance and low false alarm rate. Indeed, the extensive experimental tests, run to validate our system and compare different Deep Learning techniques, confirm that, with a proper parameter setting, we can achieve about 92\% of detection rate, with an accuracy of 0.899. Finally, with minimal changes, the proposed system can provide some information about the kind of anomaly, although in the multi-class scenario the detection rate is slightly lower (around 86\%).},

  abstract = {Integrating physical and cyber realms, Cyber--Physical Systems (CPSs) expand the potential attack surface for intruders. Given their deployment in critical infrastructures like Industrial Control Systems (ICSs), ensuring robust security is imperative. Current research has developed various Intrusion Detection techniques to identify and counter malicious activities. However, traditional methods often encounter challenges in detecting several attack types due to reliance on a single data source such as time series data from sensors and actuators. In this study, we meticulously design advanced Deep Learning (DL) anomaly-based techniques trained on either sensor/actuator data or network traffic statistics in an unsupervised setting. We evaluate these techniques on network and physical data collected concurrently from a real-world CPS. Through meticulous hyperparameter tuning, we identify the optimal parameters for each model and compare their efficiency and effectiveness in detecting different types of attacks. In addition to demonstrating superior performance compared to various baselines, we showcase the best model for each data source. Eventually, we show how utilizing diverse data sources can enhance cyber-threat detection, recognizing different kinds of attacks.},
  keywords = {Anomaly detection,CPS,ICS,Threat detection,Unsupervised learning},
  note = {Based on the provided excerpts from the article, here are the answers to your questions:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Is the solution presented suitable for detecting zero-day attacks?} \textbf{Yes.} The article states that the unsupervised anomaly detection approach succeeds in detecting previously unknown attacks, including \textbf{zero-day attacks}, which cannot be identified by signature-based models. The chosen models (AE, AE-LSTM, VAE, and GAN) were specifically selected for their ability to detect unseen threats by identifying deviations from learned normal behavior.
\item \textbf{What training paradigm does it use, and how can it be categorized?} The models are trained in an \textbf{unsupervised setting}. The framework is categorized as \textbf{unsupervised learning} because it trains exclusively on observations collected during normal working conditions to learn the system's regular patterns without requiring labeled attack data.
\item \textbf{Does the article address the handling of imbalanced datasets? If yes, how?} \textbf{Yes.} The article acknowledges that the \textbf{dataset is imbalanced}. It addresses this by \textbf{avoiding the use of accuracy} as a performance metric, instead utilizing \textbf{Precision, Recall, and the F1 score} (specifically the F1 score for the anomalous class) to provide a more rigorous and fair evaluation of the models' effectiveness.
\item \textbf{Does the article deal with the concept drift phenomenon?} \textbf{No.} The provided excerpts \textbf{do not explicitly mention or propose a solution for the concept drift phenomenon}. While the authors mention that CPS plants are prone to reconfigurations that introduce new attacks or failures, they do not specifically address the statistical shift in data distribution over time known as concept drift.
\item \textbf{Is interpretability considered an important aspect? If yes, what techniques are used?} \textbf{No.} Interpretability is \textbf{not presented as a primary focus or requirement} for the proposed deep learning models. While the article mentions interpretability as a characteristic of some baseline methods or a limitation of others, the authors \textbf{do not apply specific interpretability techniques} such as LIME or SHAP to their own models.
\item 
\par
\textbf{What data preprocessing steps are used?} The preprocessing steps include:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Filtering}: Irrelevant network packets (e.g., ICMP) are filtered out, and physical data is filtered by subsystem.
\item \textbf{Aggregation}: Raw network packets are grouped into \textbf{biflows} (bidirectional traffic flows).
\item \textbf{Scaling and Normalization}: Physical data is transformed using a \textbf{min--max scaler} to convert raw data into a structured format.
\item \textbf{Feature Extraction}: Statistical features are derived from network biflows (e.g., duration, packet counts, interarrival times) and sensor/actuator time series.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Which datasets were used or tested in the study?} The study used a portion of the \textbf{SWaT (Secure Water Treatment)} dataset. Specifically, they utilized the \textbf{2015 dataset} for training the physical models and the \textbf{2019 dataset} for network training and testing.
\item \textbf{Is there any special feature engineering method applied?} \textbf{Yes.} The authors utilize \textbf{statistics from network traffic biflows} (such as interarrival time, mean packet length, and packet counts) as input for the neural networks. For physical data, they extract features from \textbf{multivariate time series} collected from sensors and actuators.
\item \textbf{What type of model is presented?} The article presents \textbf{Deep Learning (DL)} models, specifically four unsupervised architectures: \textbf{AutoEncoder (AE)}, \textbf{AutoEncoder based on LSTM (AE-LSTM)}, \textbf{Variational Autoencoder (VAE)}, and \textbf{Generative Adversarial Network (GAN)}.
\item \textbf{What loss function is used?} The models primarily use \textbf{Mean Square Error (MSE)} to calculate the reconstruction error between the reconstructed outputs and the input. For the \textbf{Variational Autoencoder (VAE)}, the \textbf{Kullback--Leibler (KL) divergence} is added to the reconstruction loss as a regularization term.
\item \textbf{What training algorithm is applied?} The models are trained using \textbf{Deep Learning} optimization techniques, specifically involving \textbf{backpropagation} (explicitly mentioned for the VAE) and hyperparameter tuning of the \textbf{learning rate} and number of epochs.

\end{itemize}

This material appears to cover the full scope of the research study. Acknowledging that these are excerpts, the information is sufficiently comprehensive to answer the query without relying solely on metadata.},

  title = {{{XOR-Based Detector}} of {{Different Decisions}} on {{Anomalies}} in the {{Computer Network Traffic}}},
  author = {{Center for Applied Mathematics and Electronics, Vojvode Stepe 445, 11000 Belgrade, Serbia} and Protic, Danijela and Stankovic, Miomir and {Mathemathical Institute of SASA, Knez Mihajlova 36, 11000 Belgrade, Serbia}},

  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {Anomaly-based intrusion detection systems are designed to scan computer network traffic for abnormal behavior. Binary classifiers based on supervised machine learning have proven to be highly accurate tools for classifying instances as normal or abnormal. Main disadvantages of supervised machine learning are the long processing time and large amount of training data required to ensure accurate results. Two preprocessing steps to reduce data sets are feature selection and feature scaling. In this article, we present a new hyperbolic tangent feature scaling approach based on the linearization of the tangent hyperbolic function and the damping strategy of the Levenberg-Marquardt algorithm. Experiments performed on the Kyoto 2006+ dataset used four high-precision binary classifiers: weighted k-nearest neighbors, decision tree, feedforward neural networks, and support vector machine. It is shown that hyperbolic tangent scaling reduces processing time by more than twofold. An XOR-based detector is proposed to determine conflicting decisions about anomalies. The decisions of the FNN and wk-NN models are compared. It is shown that decisions sometimes turn out differently. The percentage of the opposite decisions has been shown to vary and is not affected by dataset size.},

@article{cevallosm.HEROHighdimensionalNetwork2025,
  title = {{{HERO}}: {{From High-dimensional}} Network Traffic to {{zERO-Day}} Attack Detection},
  shorttitle = {{{HERO}}},
  author = {Cevallos M., Jes{\'u}s F. and Rizzardi, Alessandra and Sicari, Sabrina and {Coen-Porisini}, Alberto},
  year = 2025,
  month = jun,
  journal = {Computer Networks},
  volume = {265},
  pages = {111264},
  issn = {13891286},
  doi = {10.1016/j.comnet.2025.111264},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {Recent trends in zero-day attack (ZdA) detection use collective anomaly detection to give insights on out-of-distribution anomalies in a zero-shot fashion. Among these, existing frameworks propose the use of specialised labelling strategies to mimic a step-wise abstract anomaly detection algorithm that generalise ZdA-detection over low-dimensional traffic-flow statistics. To enlarge such applicative scenarios, this paper proposes hero, which is compatible with High-dimensional raw-network traffic captures when performing zERO-day attack detection. To reach convergence over such a high-dimensional and noisy input space, hero decouples the representation task and the correspondent gradient updates from the discriminative task, following the neural algorithmic reasoning blueprint. Specifically, a neural processor is first trained on the discriminative task using synthetic data, and the weights are then frozen. A second training phase successfully optimises the encoding and decoding networks using raw-traffic captures and the algorithmically-aligned processor. Experiments with well-known intrusion detection datasets demonstrate the crucial advantage of using a two-stage training framework to achieve convergence. To the best of the authors' knowledge, hero is the first deep learning-based instrument that performs collective anomaly detection and categorisation over raw network traffic on a zero-shot basis, i.e., without using labels.},
  langid = {english},
  note = {A forr\'ask\'ent megadott \textbf{"HERO: From High-dimensional network traffic to zERO-Day attack detection"} (Cevallos M. \'es mtsai., 2025) c\'im\H u tanulm\'any alapj\'an az al\'abbi v\'alaszokat adom a k\'erd\'eseire:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Alkalmas-e a megold\'as zero-day t\'amad\'asok \'eszlel\'es\'ere?} \textbf{Igen.} A HERO keretrendszert kifejezetten a zero-day t\'amad\'asok (ZdA) \'eszlel\'es\'ere \'es hierarchikus kollekt\'iv anom\'alia-\'eszlel\'es form\'aj\'aban t\"ort\'en\H o kategoriz\'al\'as\'ara tervezt\'ek.
\item \textbf{Milyen tanul\'asi paradigm\'at haszn\'al, \'es hogyan kategoriz\'alhat\'o?} A modell \textbf{zero-shot learning} (nulla-minta alap\'u tanul\'as) \'es \textbf{metrika-alap\'u meta-tanul\'as} (metric-based meta-learning) paradigm\'at alkalmaz. A keretrendszer \textbf{hibrid} megk\"ozel\'it\'est haszn\'al, amelyben a neur\'alis processzort szintetikus adatokon tan\'itj\'ak el\H o, majd a tesztel\'es sor\'an c\'imk\'ek n\'elk\"ul v\'egzi el az ismeretlen t\'amad\'asok klaszterez\'es\'et.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk az kiegyens\'ulyozatlan adathalmazok kezel\'es\'evel?} \textbf{Igen.} A szerz\H ok megjegyzik, hogy a zero-day t\'amad\'asok kiegyens\'ulyozatlan oszt\'alyt k\'epviselnek, ez\'ert az \'ert\'ekel\'eshez \textbf{kiegyens\'ulyozott pontoss\'agot (balanced accuracy)} haszn\'alnak. Emellett a tan\'it\'as sor\'an \textbf{v\'eletlenszer\H u mintav\'etelez\'est} \'es egyfajta alacsony adatmennyis\'eg\H u (low-data) rendszert alkalmaznak a heterogenit\'as kezel\'es\'ere.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk a concept drift jelens\'eg\'evel?} A tanulm\'any az \textbf{out-of-distribution (OOD) tartalomeltol\'od\'assal} foglalkozik (olyan t\'amad\'asok, amelyek tartalma elt\'er a tan\'it\'o adatok\'et\'ol). A forgalom folyamatos v\'altoz\'as\'ahoz val\'o val\'os idej\H u alkalmazkod\'ast (online tanul\'as) azonban \textbf{j\"ov\H obeli kutat\'asi ir\'anyk\'ent} jel\"olik meg.
\item \textbf{Fontos szempont-e az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg? Haszn\'alnak-e speci\'alis technik\'akat?} \textbf{Igen}, a modell az \'ujdons\'ag foka (Degree of Novelty - DoN) alapj\'an kategoriz\'alja az anom\'ali\'akat, ami \textbf{hasznos inform\'aci\'ot ny\'ujt a szak\'ert\H oknek} a t\'amad\'asok jelleg\'enek \'ertelmez\'es\'ehez. Speci\'alis technik\'akat, mint a LIME vagy a SHAP, a forr\'as nem eml\'it.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'eseket haszn\'alnak?} Az al\'abbi l\'ep\'eseket alkalmazz\'ak:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item A forgalom \textbf{kapcsolati folyamatokra (flows)} bont\'asa.
\item Az els\H o \textbf{512 csomag els\H o 512 b\'ajtj\'anak} kinyer\'ese.
\item A b\'ajtok \textbf{sz\"urke\'arnyalatos k\'epp\'e} (512x512-es m\'atrix) t\"ort\'en\H o \'atalak\'it\'asa.
\item Az IP-c\'imek \'es portok \textbf{maszkol\'asa}.
\item \textbf{Normaliz\'al\'as}: a b\'ajtok \'ert\'ekeit (0-255) leosztj\'ak 255-tel, \'igy 0 \'es 1 k\"oz\"otti lebeg\H opontos sz\'amokat kapnak.
\item \textbf{Kit\"olt\'es (padding)} vagy \textbf{lev\'ag\'as (truncation)} a fix 512x512-es m\'eret el\'er\'es\'ehez.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Mely adathalmazokat haszn\'alt\'ak vagy tesztelt\'ek?} A vizsg\'alat sor\'an a \textbf{BoT-IoT} \'es az \textbf{Edge-IIoTset} adathalmazokat haszn\'alt\'ak, tov\'abb\'a egy \textbf{szintetikus adathalmazt} a processzor tan\'it\'as\'ahoz.
\item \textbf{Alkalmas-e speci\'alis jellemz\H o-m\'ern\"oki m\'odszert?} \textbf{Igen.} A modell a \textbf{Neural Algorithmic Reasoning (NAR)} architekt\'ur\'at haszn\'alja, amely \textbf{automatikus jellemz\H o-kinyer\'est} v\'egez a nyers b\'ajtokb\'ol. Manu\'alis jellemz\H o-m\'ern\"oki statisztik\'akat (pl. csomagok k\"oz\"otti \'erkez\'esi id\H o) nem haszn\'alnak.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen t\'ipus\'u modellt mutatnak be?} Egy \textbf{hibrid m\'ely tanul\'asi (Deep Learning) pipeline-t}, amely egy \textbf{k\'odol\'o-processzor-dek\'odol\'o (encode-process-decode)} architekt\'ur\'ab\'ol \'all. A f\H obb komponensek:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{CNN k\'odol\'o} (ResNet-18 vagy MobileNetV3).
\item \textbf{Gr\'af-alap\'u (GAT-v2)} neur\'alis processzor.
\item \textbf{Prototipikus h\'al\'ozat (Prototypical Network)} alap\'u dek\'odol\'o.

\end{itemize}

\item \textbf{Milyen vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt haszn\'alnak?} \textbf{Keresztentr\'opia (Cross-Entropy)} vesztes\'eget alkalmaznak az oszt\'alyoz\'ashoz \'es az anom\'alia-\'eszlel\'eshez. Tov\'abb\'a egy \textbf{adjacencia-alap\'u kernel regulariz\'aci\'os} vesztes\'eget is haszn\'alnak a l\'atens t\'er strukt\'ur\'aj\'anak jav\'it\'as\'ara.
\item \textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'o algoritmust alkalmaznak?} Az \textbf{Adam optimaliz\'al\'ot} haszn\'alj\'ak. A tan\'it\'as k\'et szakaszb\'ol \'all: el\H osz\"or a processzort tan\'itj\'ak szintetikus adatokon, majd r\"ogz\'itett (frozen) processzor-s\'ulyok mellett a k\'odol\'ot \'es a dek\'odol\'ot tan\'itj\'ak a val\'odi nyers forgalmon.

\end{itemize}

\par
\textbf{Megjegyz\'es a hozz\'af\'erhet\H os\'eggel kapcsolatban:} A v\'alaszok a rendelkez\'esre bocs\'atott teljes dokument\'aci\'o (bevezet\'es, m\'odszertan, technikai mell\'ekletek \'es eredm\'enyek) alapj\'an k\'esz\"ultek, nem csup\'an metaadatokb\'ol.},

@article{chenFedDefDefenseGradient2023,
  title = {{{FedDef}}: {{Defense Against Gradient Leakage}} in {{Federated Learning-Based Network Intrusion Detection Systems}}},
  shorttitle = {{{FedDef}}},
  author = {Chen, Jiahui and Zhao, Yi and Li, Qi and Feng, Xuewei and Xu, Ke},
  year = 2023,
  journal = {IEEE Transactions on Information Forensics and Security},
  volume = {18},
  pages = {4561--4576},
  issn = {1556-6013, 1556-6021},
  doi = {10.1109/TIFS.2023.3297369},
  urldate = {2026-03-04},
  abstract = {Deep learning (DL) methods have been widely applied to anomaly-based network intrusion detection system (NIDS) to detect malicious traffic. To expand the usage scenarios of DL-based methods, federated learning (FL) allows multiple users to train a global model on the basis of respecting individual data privacy. However, it has not yet been systematically evaluated how robust FL-based NIDSs are against existing privacy attacks under existing defenses. To address this issue, we propose two privacy evaluation metrics designed for FL-based NIDSs, including (1) privacy score that evaluates the similarity between the original and recovered traffic features using reconstruction attacks, and (2) evasion rate against NIDSs using adversarial attack with the recovered traffic. We conduct experiments to illustrate that existing defenses provide little protection and the corresponding adversarial traffic can even evade the SOTA NIDS Kitsune. To defend against such attacks and build a more robust FL-based NIDS, we further propose FedDef, a novel optimization-based input perturbation defense strategy with theoretical guarantee. It achieves both high utility by minimizing the gradient distance and strong privacy protection by maximizing the input distance. We experimentally evaluate four existing defenses on four datasets and show that our defense outperforms all the baselines in terms of privacy protection with up to 7 times higher privacy score, while maintaining model accuracy loss within 3\% under optimal parameter combination.},
  copyright = {https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/Xplorehelp/downloads/license-information/IEEE.html},
  note = {A megadott forr\'asok alapj\'an a \textbf{FedDef} nev\H u keretrendszerr\H ol sz\'ol\'o cikk k\'erd\'eseire az al\'abbi v\'alaszok adhat\'ok:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Zero-day t\'amad\'asok \'eszlel\'ese:} A cikk nem \'all\'itja kifejezetten, hogy a FedDef megold\'ast ny\'ujtana a zero-day t\'amad\'asok \'eszlel\'es\'ere. A kutat\'as f\'okusza nem mag\'an az \'eszlel\'esi k\'epess\'egen, hanem a h\'al\'ozati behatol\'asjelz\H o rendszerek (NIDS) \textbf{adatv\'edelmi v\'edelm\'en} van a feder\'alt tanul\'as (FL) sor\'an. Ugyanakkor az \'ert\'ekel\'eshez haszn\'alt \textbf{UNSW-NB15} adatk\'eszlet k\"ozismerten tartalmaz zero-day t\'ipus\'u t\'amad\'asokat, \'es a tesztel\'eshez haszn\'alt SOTA modell, a \textbf{Kitsune}, egy anom\'alia-alap\'u online \'eszlel\H o rendszer.
\item \textbf{Tan\'it\'asi paradigma:} A modell a \textbf{feder\'alt tanul\'as (FL)} paradigm\'aj\'at alkalmazza. A besorol\'asa \textbf{fel\"ugyelt tanul\'as (supervised learning)}, mivel c\'imk\'ezett adatk\'eszleteket (pl. KDD99, UNSW-NB15) haszn\'al a tan\'it\'ashoz, \'es a modell c\'elja a t\'amad\'asi kateg\'ori\'ak vagy bin\'aris c\'imk\'ek el\H orejelz\'ese.
\item \textbf{Kiegyens\'ulyozatlan adatk\'eszletek kezel\'ese:} \textbf{Igen}, a cikk eml\'iti az adatk\'eszletek kiegyens\'ulyoz\'as\'at. A szerz\H ok \'ugy kezelt\'ek ezt a probl\'em\'at, hogy a CIC-IDS2017 adatk\'eszletb\H ol \textbf{csak a DDoS t\'amad\'asi forgalmat v\'alasztott\'ak ki}, hogy kiegyens\'ulyozottabb 2-oszt\'alyos adatk\'eszleteket hozzanak l\'etre. Tov\'abb\'a egy speci\'alis algoritmust alkalmaztak a nem-f\"uggetlen \'es azonos eloszl\'as\'u (non-iid) adatok gener\'al\'as\'ahoz, ahol a felhaszn\'al\'ok azonos sz\'azal\'ekban kaptak tiszta (benign) forgalmat.
\item \textbf{Concept drift (koncepci\'ov\'alt\'as):} A forr\'asok \textbf{nem eml\'itik} a "concept drift" kifejez\'est, \'es nem javasolnak r\'a specifikus technikai megold\'ast.
\item \textbf{\'Ertelmezhet\H os\'eg (Interpretability):} Az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg \textbf{nem k\"ozponti eleme} a javasolt FedDef m\'odszernek. A cikk megeml\'iti, hogy l\'eteznek \'ertelmezhet\H o FL-NIDS kutat\'asok, de a FedDef az \textbf{input perturb\'aci\'ora} \'es az adatv\'edelem optimaliz\'al\'as\'ara \"osszpontos\'it. Olyan technik\'ak, mint a LIME vagy a SMOTE, nem szerepelnek a le\'ir\'asban.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'esek:}
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Jellemz\H ok kinyer\'ese} a h\'al\'ozati forgalomb\'ol.
\item \textbf{Normaliz\'al\'as:} Az adatok \$\$ k\"oz\"otti tartom\'anyba val\'o sk\'al\'az\'asa a minimum/maximum \'ert\'ekek alapj\'an.
\item \textbf{Diszkr\'et jellemz\H ok kezel\'ese:} A perturb\'alt adatok diszkr\'et jellemz\H oinek k\'enyszer\'it\'ese, hogy eg\'eszek maradjanak.
\item \textbf{Adatok feloszt\'asa:} Tan\'it\'o \'es tesztel\H o k\'eszletekre val\'o bont\'as 7:3 ar\'anyban.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Haszn\'alt adatk\'eszletek:} A tanulm\'anyban n\'egy adatk\'eszletet haszn\'altak: \textbf{KDDCUP'99}, \textbf{Mirai botnet}, \textbf{CIC-IDS2017} \'es \textbf{UNSW-NB15}.
\item \textbf{Speci\'alis feature engineering:} A cikk nem mutat be \'uj \'eszlel\'esi c\'el\'u jellemz\H o-kinyer\'esi m\'odszert, viszont egy egyedi \textbf{input perturb\'aci\'os strat\'egi\'at} alkalmaz, amely az eredeti jellemz\H oket ,,pszeudo-adatokk\'a'' alak\'itja \'at az adatv\'edelem \'erdek\'eben.
\item \textbf{Modell t\'ipusa:} A rendszer egy \textbf{feder\'alt tanul\'asi (FL)} keretrendszer. A lok\'alis modellek \textbf{m\'ely tanul\'asi (deep learning)} modellek, pontosabban \textbf{3-r\'eteg\H u, teljesen \"osszekapcsolt Deep Neural Network (DNN)} architekt\'ur\'ak. Az \"osszehasonl\'it\'ashoz haszn\'alt\'ak m\'eg a Kitsune modellt is, amely \textbf{autoencoder-alap\'u}.
\item \textbf{Vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'eny (Loss function):} A f\H o oszt\'alyoz\'asi feladathoz \textbf{keresztentr\'opia vesztes\'eget (cross entropy loss)} haszn\'alnak. A FedDef v\'edelmi mechanizmusa saj\'at optimaliz\'aci\'os vesztes\'eget (\$L\_\textbraceleft Pri\textbraceright\$ \'es \$L\_\textbraceleft Per\textbraceright\$) alkalmaz, amely tartalmazza a gradiensek k\"oz\"otti \textbf{Euklideszi (\$L2\$) t\'avols\'agot}.
\item \textbf{Tan\'it\'asi algoritmus:} A glob\'alis aggreg\'aci\'ohoz a \textbf{FedAvg (Federated Averaging)} algoritmust haszn\'alj\'ak. A perturb\'aci\'os optimaliz\'al\'ashoz az \textbf{Adam optimaliz\'al\'ot} alkalmazz\'ak.

\end{itemize}

\par
\textbf{Megjegyz\'es az el\'erhet\H os\'egr\H ol:} A v\'alaszok a forr\'ask\'ent megadott cikkr\'eszletek alapj\'an k\'esz\"ultek, amelyek lefedik az absztraktot, a m\'odszertant, a k\'is\'erleti be\'all\'it\'asokat \'es az eredm\'enyek \"osszefoglal\'as\'at, \'igy az inform\'aci\'ok nem csup\'an metaadatokon alapulnak.},

@inproceedings{cinarMachineLearningSupported2024,
  title = {Machine {{Learning Supported Network Attack Detection}} with a {{Novel Method Based}} on {{Random Forest-Based Feature Fusion}}},
  booktitle = {2024 17th {{International Conference}} on {{Information Security}} and {{Cryptology}} ({{ISCT\"urkiye}})},
  author = {{\c C}inar, Cihan and Do{\v g}ru, {\.I}brahim Alper and Atacak, {\.I}smail},
  year = 2024,
  month = oct,
  pages = {1--6},
  publisher = {IEEE},
  address = {Ankara, Turkiye},
  doi = {10.1109/ISCTrkiye64784.2024.10779248},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {Nowadays, Innovative techniques, including artificial intelligence and machine learning (ML), have been employed in attack detection systems across numerous domains to create effective detection mechanisms that identify attacks more quickly and accurately. In this study, we propose a novel machine learning-based method that utilizes Random Forest (RF)-based feature fusion (RFBFF) to detect anomalies in computer networks. This method integrates two basic procedures: feature fusion and classification. In feature fusion, unlike traditional feature selection, all features are utilized in the creation of the new data set. This allows for the incorporation of the impact of each feature on the outcome. The feature fusion procedure generates a reduced dataset by combining similar features based on RF-derived gain scores, while the classification procedure uses this refined dataset to label the attack status in the network using various ML algorithms. Specifically, Decision Tree (DT), K-Nearest Neighbor (k-NN), Additive Logistic Regression (Logitboost), and Na\"ive Bayes (NB) have been employed as classification algorithms. The effectiveness of the proposed method in network attack detection has been validated by comparing it with ML methods based on RF-based feature selection (RFBFS). For a robust comparison and see the performance of the model in the real world, the NSL-KDD and UNSW\_NB15 data sets, which contain data packet examples of normal or attack traffic that can be encountered in the internet environment, categorized with binary classification, were preferred as the benchmark data set. Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed approach excels in detecting network attacks, achieving an accuracy rate of 0.995 on the NSL-KDD dataset and 0.924 on the UNSW-NB15 dataset. Leveraging the gain scores of all features, even if they are relatively low, has been found to positively impact the model's overall performance.},

  isbn = {979-8-3315-3364-9},

Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) have expanded the scope of what is possible with machine learning, allowing for new applications in areas such as computer vision, natural language processing, and creative AI. GANs, in particular, have been used for a wide range of tasks, including image and video generation, data augmentation, style transfer, and anomaly detection. They have also been used for medical imaging and drug discovery, where they can generate synthetic data to augment small datasets, reduce the need for expensive experiments, and lower the number of real patients that must be included in medical trials. Given these developments, we propose using the power of GANs to create and augment flow-based network traffic datasets. We evaluate a series of GAN architectures, including Wasserstein, conditional, energy-based, gradient penalty, and LSTM-GANs. We evaluate their performance on a set of flow-based network traffic data collected from 16 subjects who used their computers for home, work, and study purposes. The performance of these GAN architectures is described according to metrics that involve networking principles, data distribution among a collection of flows, and temporal data distribution. Given the tendency of network intrusion detection datasets to have a very imbalanced data distribution, i.e., a large number of samples in the ``normal traffic'' category and a comparatively low number of samples assigned to the ``intrusion'' categories, we test our GANs by augmenting the intrusion data and checking whether this helps intrusion detection neural networks in their task. We publish the resulting UPBFlow dataset and code on GitHub.                                1},

@article{dasilvaruffoGenerativeAdversarialNetworks2025,
  title = {Generative Adversarial Networks to Detect Intrusion and Anomaly in {{IP}} Flow-Based Networks},
  author = {Da Silva Ruffo, Vitor G. and Lent, Daniel M. Brand{\~a}o and Carvalho, Luiz F. and Lloret, Jaime and Proen{\c c}a, Mario Lemes},
  year = 2025,
  month = feb,
  journal = {Future Generation Computer Systems},
  volume = {163},
  pages = {107531},
  issn = {0167739X},
  doi = {10.1016/j.future.2024.107531},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {Computer networks facilitate regular human tasks, providing services like data streaming, online shopping, and digital communications. These applications require more and more network capacity and dynamicity to accomplish their goals. The networks may be targeted by attacks and intrusions that compromise the applications that rely on them and lead to potential losses. We propose a semi-supervised systematic methodology for developing a detection system for traffic volume anomalies in IP flow-based networks. The system is implemented with a vanilla Generative Adversarial Network (GAN). The mitigation module is triggered whenever an anomaly is detected, automatically blocking the suspect IPs and restoring the correct network functioning. We implemented three versions of the proposed solution by incorporating Long Short-Term Memory (LSTM), 1D-Convolutional Neural Network (1D-CNN), and Temporal Convolutional Network (TCN) into the GAN internal structure. The experiments are conducted on three public benchmark datasets: Orion, CIC-DDoS2019, and CIC-IDS2017. The results show that the three considered deep learning models have distinct impacts on the GAN model and, consequently, on the overall system performance. The 1D-CNN-based GAN implementation is the best since it reasonably solves the mode collapse problem, has the most efficient computational complexity, and achieves competitive Matthews Correlation Coefficient scores for the anomaly detection task. Also, the mitigation module can drop most anomalous flows, blocking only a slight portion of legitimate traffic. For comparison with state-of-the-art models, we implemented 1D-CNN, LSTM, and TCN separately from the GAN. The generative networks show improved overall results in the considered performance metrics compared to the other models.},
  langid = {english},
  note = {A forr\'ask\'ent megadott \textbf{"Generative adversarial networks to detect intrusion and anomaly in IP flow-based networks"} (da Silva Ruffo \'es mtsai., 2025) c\'im\H u tanulm\'any alapj\'an az al\'abbi v\'alaszokat adom a k\'erd\'eseire:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Alkalmas-e a megold\'as zero-day t\'amad\'asok \'eszlel\'es\'ere?} \textbf{Igen.} A rendszer egy anom\'alia-alap\'u h\'al\'ozati behatol\'asjelz\H o rendszer (NIDS), amely a norm\'al forgalmi profilt\'ol val\'o elt\'er\'eseket keresi, \'igy k\'epes mind az ismert, mind az \textbf{ismeretlen (unknown/zero-day) anom\'ali\'ak azonos\'it\'as\'ara}.
\item \textbf{Milyen tanul\'asi paradigm\'at haszn\'al, \'es hogyan kategoriz\'alhat\'o?} A cikk egy \textbf{f\'el-fel\"ugyelt (semi-supervised)} szisztematikus m\'odszertant javasol,. A modellt kiz\'ar\'olag legitim forgalmi mint\'akon tan\'itj\'ak, hogy megtanulja azok statisztikai eloszl\'as\'at,.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk a kiegyens\'ulyozatlan adathalmazok kezel\'es\'evel?} \textbf{Igen.} A szerz\H ok elismerik, hogy a t\'amad\'asok a val\'os\'agban ritk\'ak, ez\'ert az \textbf{Orion adathalmazt sz\'and\'ekosan kiegyens\'ulyozatlanul} hagyt\'ak. A probl\'em\'at \'ugy kezelik, hogy \textbf{f\'el-fel\"ugyelt m\'odon csak a t\"obbs\'egi (legitim) oszt\'alyon tan\'itanak}, \'igy a modell nem f\"ugg a t\'amad\'asi c\'imk\'ek mennyis\'eg\'et\H ol,.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk a concept drift jelens\'eg\'evel?} A tanulm\'any megeml\'iti a \textbf{legitim forgalom concept drift} jelens\'eg\'et, de azt \textbf{j\"ov\H obeli kutat\'asi ir\'anyk\'ent} jel\"oli meg, \'es a jelenlegi munk\'aban nem javasol r\'a konkr\'et megold\'ast.
\item \textbf{Fontos szempont-e az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg? Haszn\'alnak-e speci\'alis technik\'akat?} \textbf{Igen}, a szerz\H ok szerint fontos meg\'erteni, hogyan hozz\'ak meg a modellek a d\"ont\'eseiket. Ennek \'erdek\'eben a \textbf{SHAP (SHapley Additive exPlanations)} m\'odszert alkalmazz\'ak, hogy vizualiz\'alj\'ak az egyes jellemz\H ok (pl. IP \'es port entr\'opi\'ak) hozz\'aj\'arul\'as\'at a kimenethez.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'eseket haszn\'alnak?} Az alkalmazott l\'ep\'esek a k\"ovetkez\H ok:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Hi\'anyz\'o \'ert\'ekek kezel\'ese:} a s\'er\"ult vagy \'erv\'enytelen \'ert\'ekeket null\'ara \'all\'itj\'ak.
\item \textbf{Entr\'opia sz\'am\'it\'as:} Shannon-entr\'opi\'at sz\'amolnak a forr\'as \'es c\'el IP-c\'imekre, valamint portokra,.
\item \textbf{Jellemz\H o-sk\'al\'az\'as (Feature scaling):} az entr\'opi\'ak standardiz\'al\'asa (pl. min-max),.
\item \textbf{Ablakfriss\'it\'es (Window updating):} cs\'usz\'oablak mechanizmus alkalmaz\'asa.
\item \textbf{Adatspecifikus sz\H ur\'es:} az \"ures m\'asodpercek \'es a "gyenge" DDoS pr\'ob\'alkoz\'asok elt\'avol\'it\'asa (a CIC-DDoS2019 adathalmaz eset\'en).

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Mely adathalmazokat haszn\'alt\'ak vagy tesztelt\'ek?} A k\'is\'erletekhez h\'arom nyilv\'anos benchmark adathalmazt haszn\'altak: \textbf{Orion}, \textbf{CIC-DDoS2019} \'es \textbf{CIC-IDS2017},.

\item \textbf{Milyen t\'ipus\'u modellt mutatnak be?} A bemutatott megold\'as egy \textbf{m\'ely tanul\'asi (Deep Learning)} modell, pontosabban egy \textbf{vanilla Generative Adversarial Network (GAN)}. A GAN bels\H o szerkezet\'ebe \textbf{LSTM (Long Short-Term Memory)}, \textbf{1D-CNN (1D-Convolutional Neural Network)} vagy \textbf{TCN (Temporal Convolutional Network)} egys\'egeket integr\'altak,.
\item \textbf{Milyen vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt (loss function) haszn\'alnak?} A GAN hagyom\'anyos \textbf{minimax objekt\'iv f\"uggv\'eny\'et} haszn\'alj\'ak, ahol a gener\'ator minimaliz\'alja, a diszkrimin\'ator pedig maximaliz\'alja a logaritmikus val\'osz\'in\H us\'egeken alapul\'o k\"olts\'egf\"uggv\'enyt,.
\item \textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'o algoritmust alkalmaznak?} A tan\'it\'as a \textbf{gradiens s\"ullyed\'es (gradient descent)} algoritmuson alapul. Az optimaliz\'al\'ashoz \textbf{Adam} vagy \textbf{rmsprop} algoritmust haszn\'alnak, a hiperparam\'eterek finomhangol\'asa pedig \textbf{r\'acs alap\'u keres\'essel (grid search)} t\"ort\'enik.

\end{itemize}

@article{deivakaniIntelligentSecurityMechanism2024,
  title = {An Intelligent Security Mechanism in Mobile {{Ad-Hoc}} Networks Using Precision Probability Genetic Algorithms ({{PPGA}}) and Deep Learning Technique ({{Stacked LSTM}})},
  author = {Deivakani, M. and Sheela, M. Sahaya and Priyadarsini, K. and Farhaoui, Yousef},
  year = 2024,
  month = sep,
  journal = {Sustainable Computing: Informatics and Systems},
  volume = {43},
  pages = {101021},

  doi = {10.1016/j.suscom.2024.101021},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {The Mobile Ad-hoc Networks (MANETs) have gained a significant attention in the recent years due to their proliferation and huge application purposes. To defend from many types of modern cyber dangers like Distributed Denial of Service (DDoS) attacks, Advanced Persistent Threats (APTs), Insider Threats, Ransomware, Zero-Day Exploits, Social Engineering tactics, and etc is not easy when it comes to keeping MANETs security. These complex assaults focus on network infrastructure, take advantage of weaknesses in communication protocols and control user actions. Although there have been improvements in intrusion detection systems (IDS), it is still difficult to fully safeguard MANETs. The purpose of this research is to create advanced methods that can accurately find and decrease attacks inside MANETs. Applying Sensor-based Feature Extraction (SFE) to extract useful network features such as Received Signal Strength Indication (RSSI) and Time of Travel (TOT) from datasets NSL-KDD and CICIDS-2017. Utilizing the fresh method of Precise Probability Genetic Algorithm (PPGA) optimization for removing unrelated details, which enhances precision in detecting attacks. Predicting normal and attacking labels by applying Stacked Recurrent Long Short Term Memory (SRLSTM) method, fine-tuning classifier's parameters in every layer to improve outcomes. In order to authenticate and compare the suggested methods with current attack detection tactics, this study will make use of various evaluation measurements. The NSL-KDD, which is a benchmark dataset in network intrusion detection research, has a wide variety of network traffic data with instances that are labeled as normal and different attacks. CICIDS-2017 is similar because it contains an extensive dataset too - this includes real-world traces from network traffic where there's both regular activity and harmful actions. The purpose is to enhance the existing status of MANET security so as it can withstand more strongly against cyber dangers. According to the outcomes, it is analyzed that the attack detection accuracy has improved greatly 99\,\% when compared to other methods, as shown by the detailed assessment measurements. Better handling of big datasets with top detection accuracy reduces the time needed 8.9\,s for training and testing models. Decrease in misclassification results and better ability to differentiate normal network actions from harmful intrusions. Improved resistance of MANETs to different cyber dangers, guaranteeing the safety and dependability of network communication in changing and non-centralized settings.},
  langid = {english},
