@article{duanApplicationDynamicLine2023,
  title = {Application of a {{Dynamic Line Graph Neural Network}} for {{Intrusion Detection With Semisupervised Learning}}},
  author = {Duan, Guanghan and Lv, Hongwu and Wang, Huiqiang and Feng, Guangsheng},
  year = 2023,
  journal = {IEEE Transactions on Information Forensics and Security},
  volume = {18},
  pages = {699--714},
  issn = {1556-6021},
  doi = {10.1109/TIFS.2022.3228493},
  urldate = {2025-06-14},
  abstract = {Deep learning (DL) greatly enhances binary anomaly detection capabilities through effective statistical network characterization; nevertheless, the intrusion class differentiation performance is still insufficient. Two related challenges have not been fully explored. 1) Statistical attack characteristics are overemphasized while ignoring inherent attack topologies; sequence features are extracted from whole traffic flows, but the interaction evolution of each IP pair over time is rarely considered, such as in long short-term memory (LSTM) and gated recurrent units (GRUs). 2) Meeting the need for many high-quality labeled data samples is an expensive and labor-intensive task in large-scale, complex, and heterogeneous networks. To address these issues, we propose a dynamic line graph neural network (DLGNN)-based intrusion detection method with semisupervised learning. Our model converts network traffic into a series of spatiotemporal graphs. A dynamic GNN (DGNN) is employed to extract spatial information from each discrete snapshot and capture the contextual evolution of communication between IP pairs through consecutive snapshots. Moreover, a line graph realizes edge embedding expressions corresponding to network communications and strengthens the message aggregation ability of graph convolution. Experiments on 6 novel datasets demonstrate that our approach achieves 98.15--99.8\% accuracy in abnormality detection with fewer labeled samples. Meanwhile, state-of-the-art multiclass performance is achieved, e.g., the average detection accuracy for DDoS across the 6 datasets reaches 95.32\%.},
  keywords = {Behavioral sciences,Feature extraction,Graph neural network,Image edge detection,intrusion detection,Intrusion detection,IP networks,line graph,Network topology,Not checked,semisupervised learning,Spatiotemporal phenomena},
  annotation = {Access to Full Paper is not available},
  note = {A forr\'ask\'ent megadott cikk (Duan et al., 2022) metaadatai \'es absztraktja alapj\'an az al\'abbi v\'alaszok adhat\'ok a k\'erd\'eseire:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Alkalmas-e a megold\'as zero-day t\'amad\'asok \'eszlel\'es\'ere?} Igen, a cikk szerint a javasolt modell egy \textbf{anom\'alia-alap\'u} bet\"or\'esjelz\H o m\'odszer. Az anom\'alia-\'eszlel\'es c\'elja a h\'al\'ozati forgalom statisztikai jellemz\'ese \'es az abnorm\'alis mint\'ak azonos\'it\'asa, ami lehet\H ov\'e teszi a kor\'abban ismeretlen, \'ugynevezett \textbf{zero-day} t\'amad\'asok felismer\'es\'et.
\item \textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'asi paradigm\'at haszn\'al?} A modell \textbf{f\'el-fel\"ugyelt tanul\'ast (semi-supervised learning)} alkalmaz. Ez a kateg\'oria lehet\H ov\'e teszi a h\'al\'ozat tan\'it\'as\'at korl\'atozott mennyis\'eg\H u felc\'imk\'ezett \'es nagy mennyis\'eg\H u c\'imk\'ezetlen adat felhaszn\'al\'as\'aval.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk a kiegyens\'ulyozatlan adatk\'eszletek kezel\'es\'evel?} A rendelkez\'esre \'all\'o absztrakt \'es metaadatok \textbf{nem tesznek eml\'it\'est} a kiegyens\'ulyozatlan adatk\'eszletek specifikus kezel\'es\'er\H ol (p\'eld\'aul SMOTE vagy s\'ulyoz\'as alkalmaz\'as\'ar\'ol).
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk a koncepci\'ov\'alt\'as (concept drift) jelens\'eg\'evel?} B\'ar a "concept drift" kifejez\'es nem szerepel az absztraktban, a modell kifejezetten a \textbf{h\'al\'ozati forgalom id\H obeli fejl\H od\'es\'enek (temporal evolution)} \'es a dinamikus h\'al\'ozati felt\'eteleknek a kezel\'es\'ere k\'esz\"ult. A rendszer spatiotemporal (t\'erid\H obeli) gr\'afokat haszn\'al, hogy lek\"ovesse az IP-p\'arok k\"oz\"otti kommunik\'aci\'o v\'altoz\'asait az id\H oben.
\item \textbf{Fontos szempont-e az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg?} Az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg \textbf{nem jelenik meg kiemelt szempontk\'ent} a cikk metaadataiban vagy absztraktj\'aban, \'es nem eml\'itenek hozz\'a kapcsol\'od\'o technik\'akat.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'eseket alkalmaznak?} Az absztrakt alapj\'an az al\'abbi l\'ep\'esek azonos\'ithat\'ok:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item A h\'al\'ozati forgalom \'atalak\'it\'asa \textbf{t\'erid\H obeli (spatiotemporal) gr\'afok} sorozat\'av\'a.
\item A forgalom feloszt\'asa \textbf{diszkr\'et pillanatfelv\'etelekre (snapshots)}.
\item \textbf{\'El-be\'agyaz\'asok (edge embeddings)} gener\'al\'asa vonalgr\'af transzform\'aci\'o seg\'its\'eg\'evel.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Mely adatk\'eszleteket haszn\'alt\'ak a vizsg\'alat sor\'an?} Az absztrakt szerint a modellt \textbf{6 \'uj (novel) adatk\'eszleten} tesztelt\'ek, de ezek pontos nev\'et a metaadatok nem sorolj\'ak fel. (M\'as forr\'asok hivatkoznak a szerz\H ok munk\'aj\'ara olyan \"osszef\"ugg\'esben, ahol az UNSW-NB15 vagy IoT-specifikus adatok szerepelnek, de az eredeti absztrakt csak "6 novel dataset"-et eml\'it).
\item \textbf{Alkalmaznak-e speci\'alis jellemz\H o-m\'ern\"oki (feature engineering) m\'odszert?} Igen, a modell egy speci\'alis \textbf{vonalgr\'af (line graph)} transzform\'aci\'ot alkalmaz. Ez a m\'odszer a h\'al\'ozati kommunik\'aci\'ot reprezent\'al\'o \'eleket csom\'opontokk\'a alak\'itja a vonalgr\'afban, ami meger\H os\'iti a gr\'af-konvol\'uci\'o \"uzenet-aggreg\'aci\'os k\'epess\'eg\'et.
\item \textbf{Milyen t\'ipus\'u modellt mutatnak be?} A bemutatott modell egy \textbf{Dynamic Line Graph Neural Network (DLGNN)}, amely a \textbf{m\'ely tanul\'as (deep learning)} \'es a \textbf{gr\'af-alap\'u} modellek kateg\'ori\'aj\'aba tartozik. K\"uls\H o hivatkoz\'asok szerint a strukt\'ura \textbf{GCN} (Graph Convolutional Network) \'es \textbf{GRU} (Gated Recurrent Unit) egys\'egeket is tartalmazhat az id\H obeli dinamika kezel\'es\'ere.
\item \textbf{Milyen vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt haszn\'alnak?} A forr\'ask\'ent megadott metaadatok \'es absztrakt \textbf{nem tartalmaznak inform\'aci\'ot} a konkr\'et vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyr\H ol.
\item \textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'asi algoritmust alkalmaznak?} A modell \textbf{f\'el-fel\"ugyelt tan\'it\'ast} alkalmaz, amely spatiotemporal gr\'af-pillanatfelv\'eteleken alapul, hogy egyszerre ragadja meg a t\'erbeli (topol\'ogiai) inform\'aci\'okat \'es az id\H obeli kontextust.

\end{itemize}

\par
\textbf{Megjegyz\'es:} A tanulm\'any \textbf{teljes sz\"ovege nem hozz\'af\'erhet\H o} a megadott forr\'asokban (a ResearchGate-en kereszt\"ul k\'erhet\H o el a szerz\H okt\H ol), \'igy a fenti v\'alaszok kiz\'ar\'olag a cikk \textbf{metaadataib\'ol \'es absztraktj\'ab\'ol}, valamint a kapcsol\'od\'o szakirodalmi hivatkoz\'asokb\'ol sz\'armaznak.}
}

This approach combines the strengths of six distinct deep learning algorithms: DNN, CNN, RNN, LSTM, GRU, and a Hybrid CNN-LSTM architecture. The NSL-KDD dataset, a widely recognized benchmark for intrusion detection research, was utilized for implementation and evaluation. In binary classification, the approach demonstrates exceptional capabilities, with the GRU approach outperforming others. Similarly, the DNN, LSTM, CNN, and RNN approaches exhibit robust performance, showcasing their efficacy in detecting anomalies within network data. In the multi-classification setting, the DNN approach stands out with outstanding performance. While other approaches, including RNN, CNN, LSTM, GRU, and the Hybrid CNN-LSTM approach, also maintain commendable results, the DNN approach proves to be the most effective in handling complex network patterns. This research provides valuable insights into the application of deep learning approaches using the NSL-KDD dataset for network anomaly detection, emphasizing their versatility and reliability across different classification scenarios. The findings lay the groundwork for further exploration and utilization of deep learning methodologies in enhancing network security.},

@article{fengNetworkAnomalyEarly2023,
  title = {Network {{Anomaly Early Warning}} through {{Generalized Network Temperature}} and {{Deep Learning}}},

  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {A successful DDoS attack disables systems and causes huge economic losses, which is every company's worst fear. The goal of IT security is to avoid this worst-case situation. Anomaly detection is one of the most imperative currently. However, feature selection and accuracy detection limitations are still pertinent to traditional algorithms. Moreover, these methods cannot predict and respond to DDoS attacks in advance. Therefore, this paper proposes a network anomaly early warning method based on Generalized Network Temperature (GNT) and deep learning. The approach first classifies DDoS-induced network congestion. Then, it predicts network traffic characteristics using Bi-GRU and discovers the class of congestion states corresponding to each feature collection through the Stacking model. In what follows, this method connects the two models to achieve the early warning function. Furthermore, a combination of criteria is designed based on congestion states and attack probability to improve the early warning accuracy. The proposed model is validated using the intrusion detection dataset CICIDS2017 and UNSW-NB15. The Bi-GRU model achieves the best result of 77.95\% and 81.96\% for the R-Squared traffic prediction on the two datasets. The Stacking model achieves 94.37\% accuracy and 95.82\% for congestion states classification, respectively. After the model is connected, our proposed approach performs the best warning accuracy of 96.84\% on the CICIDS2017 dataset and 95.68\% on the UNSW-NB15 dataset.},

Ez\'altal a riaszt\'asok ,,finomabb szemcs\'ezetts\'eg\H u'' magyar\'azatot kapnak.

@article{gengDUdetectorDualgranularityUnsupervised2025,
  title = {{{{\emph{DUdetector}}}}: {{A}} Dual-Granularity Unsupervised Model for Network Anomaly Detection},
  shorttitle = {{{{\emph{DUdetector}}}}},
  author = {Geng, Haijun and Ma, Qi and Chi, Haotian and Zhang, Zhi and Yang, Jing and Yin, Xia},
  year = 2025,
  month = feb,
  journal = {Computer Networks},
  volume = {257},
  pages = {110937},
  issn = {1389-1286},
  doi = {10.1016/j.comnet.2024.110937},
  urldate = {2025-06-12},
  abstract = {Internet of Things (IoT) devices are often used as springboards for network intrusion due to the open nature of IoT protocol stacks that enable automatic inter-connection and data sharing among devices, so it is critical to develop network anomaly detection algorithms that can be deployed at important nodes such as gateways and routers. However, existing detection algorithms based on signature rules and supervised machine learning heavily rely on known anomaly types, yielding low detection accuracy when deployed in realistic network environments with a significant number of unknown attacks. With this in mind, we propose DUdetector, an unsupervised anomaly detection algorithm by employing Transformer and Conv1d\&MaxPool1d AutoEncoder with residual connection (abbr., CM\&RC-AE) to realize a dual-granularity learning from the perspective of segments and points, respectively. Specifically, we perform coarse-grained segment-level anomaly detection based on an improved Transformer to detect whether there is any anomalous traffic within a time window. Then, we perform fine-grained point-level anomaly detection based on CM\&RC-AE for each packet within the problematic segment output by the first step. Extensive experiments on three datasets (SSDP Flood, Mirai and IDS2017) demonstrate that our DUdetector achieves a better performance than existing work: an F1-score of 95.98\% for Mirai, and over 99.2\% for both SSDP Flood and IDS2017, with false positive rates less than 0.5\% for all three datasets.},
  keywords = {AutoEncoder,Dual-granularity,Internet of things attack,Not checked,Transformer,Unsupervised anomaly detection},
  note = {Based on the provided excerpts from the article "DUdetector: A dual-granularity unsupervised model for network anomaly detection," here are the answers to your questions:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Is the solution suitable for detecting zero-day attacks?} \textbf{Yes.} The article states that because the model is \textbf{unsupervised} and learns only from normal samples, it can identify anything that does not fit the normal pattern as a potential attack, allowing it to effectively handle \textbf{unknown attacks},.
\item \textbf{What training paradigm does it use, and how can it be categorized?} It uses an \textbf{unsupervised} training paradigm,,. The model is trained exclusively on \textbf{normal traffic samples},.
\item \textbf{Does the article address the handling of imbalanced datasets?} \textbf{No}, the provided excerpts do not explicitly discuss techniques for handling imbalanced datasets (like oversampling or undersampling). In the context of this study, the model is trained only on normal data, which inherently avoids the class imbalance problem typically found in supervised learning where models must distinguish between majority (normal) and minority (attack) classes,.
\item \textbf{Does the article deal with the concept drift phenomenon?} \textbf{No}, the concept drift phenomenon is not explicitly addressed, nor is a specific solution for it proposed in the provided text.
\item \textbf{Is interpretability considered an important aspect?} The article acknowledges that \textbf{traditional methods} are known for strong interpretability. However, it does \textbf{not apply specific interpretability techniques} (like LIME or SHAP) to the DUdetector model. In fact, the authors mention that assigning a specific attack type to identified anomalies is "beyond the scope of this paper" due to the nature of unsupervised learning.
\item 
\par
\textbf{What data preprocessing steps are used?} The preprocessing steps include:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Parsing and filtering} essential packet header fields (timestamp, IP/MAC address, ports) using \textbf{Tshark},.
\item \textbf{Calculating basic attributes}: bandwidth (Size), rate (Count), and inter-packet delay (Jitter),.
\item \textbf{Vectorization} using an \textbf{Improved Damped Incremental Statistics (DIS)} method to aggregate information from previous traffic,,.
\item \textbf{Generating statistical features} across five distinct damped windows (\$\textbackslash lambda = 5, 3, 1, 0.1, 0.01\$),.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Which datasets were used or tested in the study?} The study used three datasets: \textbf{SSDP Flood}, \textbf{Mirai}, and \textbf{IDS2017} (specifically a subset of Wednesday traffic),,,.
\item \textbf{Is there any special feature engineering method applied?} \textbf{Yes.} The authors developed an \textbf{Improved Damped Incremental Statistics (DIS)} method,. Unlike the original DIS, they incorporate four types of 2D statistics for the "Jitter" attribute, resulting in a \textbf{135-dimensional} vectorized representation for each packet,,.
\item 
\par
\textbf{What type of model is presented?} The DUdetector is a \textbf{dual-granularity unsupervised model} consisting of two main components:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Segment-level}: An \textbf{Enhanced Transformer} model (where the Feed-Forward layer is refined using an \textbf{LSTM-AutoEncoder}),,,.
\item \textbf{Point-level}: A \textbf{Conv1d\&MaxPool1d AutoEncoder with residual connection (CM\&RC-AE)},,.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{What loss function is used?} The model uses \textbf{Mean Squared Error (MSE)} as the reconstruction loss function,,.
\item \textbf{What training algorithm is applied?} The parameters are optimized using a \textbf{mini-batch gradient descent algorithm},,.
\item \textbf{Accessibility of the article:} The answers provided above are based on the \textbf{excerpts from the full article} provided in the sources, which include detailed sections on design, methodology, and evaluation. These are not inferred solely from metadata.

\end{itemize}},
  file = {/home/premissa/Zotero/storage/N84Y9VLI/Geng és mtsai. - 2025 - DUdetector A dual-granularity unsupervised model for network anomaly detection.pdf}
}

@article{ghadermaziGTAEIDSGraphTransformerBased2025,
  title = {{{GTAE-IDS}}: {{Graph Transformer-Based Autoencoder Framework}} for {{Real-Time Network Intrusion Detection}}},
  shorttitle = {{{GTAE-IDS}}},
  author = {Ghadermazi, Jalal and Hore, Soumyadeep and Shah, Ankit and Bastian, Nathaniel D.},

  urldate = {2025-06-12},
  abstract = {Network intrusion detection systems (NIDS) utilize signature and anomaly-based methods to detect malicious activities within networks. Advances in machine learning (ML) and deep learning (DL) algorithms have enabled NIDS to analyze large volumes of data and identify complex patterns. However, traditional ML/DL approaches in NIDS have primarily relied on flow-based features and utilized flat data formats, such as vectors or grids, which limit their ability to recognize the structural and contextual nuances of network attacks, particularly in real-time. Additionally, most NIDS depend on supervised or semi-supervised learning, requiring extensive labeled data that is time-consuming to generate and not always feasible. This reliance restricts their ability to detect novel attacks, as they typically only recognize threats similar to those encountered during training. Hence, there is a significant need to develop NIDS that can operate in near real-time, eliminate the need for labeled data, and effectively identify novel attack patterns. We propose GTAE-IDS, a novel unsupervised packet-based graph neural network framework aimed at early and precise anomaly detection in network traffic. GTAE-IDS employs graph embeddings to capture and process network traffic data swiftly, creating sequential packet-based graphs that reflect network communications. Our approach employs graph autoencoders to identify structural and global patterns in benign data without needing labeled graph data, enhancing detection capabilities against novel attacks. Incorporating transformers in the encoder segment, GTAE-IDS effectively discerns contextual patterns in network traffic, achieving over 98\% accuracy in identifying malicious activities on benchmark network intrusion data sets.},

@article{ghadermaziRealTimeNetworkIntrusion2025,
  title = {Towards {{Real-Time Network Intrusion Detection With Image-Based Sequential Packets Representation}}},
  author = {Ghadermazi, Jalal and Shah, Ankit and Bastian, Nathaniel D.},
  year = 2025,
  month = feb,
  journal = {IEEE Transactions on Big Data},
  volume = {11},
  number = {1},
  pages = {157--173},
  issn = {2332-7790, 2372-2096},
  doi = {10.1109/TBDATA.2024.3403394},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {Machine learning (ML) and deep learning (DL) advancements have greatly enhanced anomaly detection of network intrusion detection systems (NIDS) by empowering them to analyze Big Data and extract patterns. ML/DL-based NIDS are trained using either flow-based or packet-based features. Flow-based NIDS are suitable for offline traffic analysis, while packet-based NIDS can analyze traffic and detect attacks in real-time. Current packet-based approaches analyze packets independently, overlooking the sequential nature of network communication. This results in biased models that exhibit increased false negatives and positives. Additionally, most literature-proposed packet-based NIDS capture only payload data, neglecting crucial information from packet headers. This oversight can impair the ability to identify header-level attacks, such as denial-of-service attacks. To address these limitations, we propose a novel artificial intelligence-enabled methodological framework for packet-based NIDS that effectively analyzes header and payload data and considers temporal connections among packets. Our framework transforms sequential packets into two-dimensional images. It then develops a convolutional neural network-based intrusion detection model to process these images and detect malicious activities. Through experiments using publicly available big datasets, we demonstrate that our framework is able to achieve high detection rates of 97.7\% to 99\% across different attack types and displays promising resilience against adversarial examples.},
  copyright = {https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-nc-nd/4.0/},
  note = {A megadott forr\'asok alapj\'an az al\'abbi v\'alaszokat adom a k\'erd\'eseire:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'asi paradigm\'at haszn\'al, \'es hogyan kategoriz\'alhat\'o?} A javasolt modell a \textbf{fel\"ugyelt tanul\'as} (supervised learning) kateg\'ori\'aj\'aba tartozik, mivel c\'imk\'ezett adatk\'eszleteket (benignus \'es k\"ul\"onb\"oz\H o t\'amad\'asi t\'ipusok) haszn\'al a tan\'it\'ashoz \'es valid\'al\'ashoz. A feladatot \textbf{bin\'aris oszt\'alyoz\'asi probl\'emak\'ent} kezelik.

\textbf{Milyen adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'eseket alkalmaznak?}
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Csomag-elemz\'es (Packet Parsing):} Inform\'aci\'ok kinyer\'ese nyers pcap f\'ajlokb\'ol.
\item \textbf{Jellemz\H ok elt\'avol\'it\'asa (Bias removal):} Bizonyos fejl\'ecek (pl. Ethernet, IP verzi\'o, IP c\'imek, portok, IP/TCP opci\'ok) t\"orl\'ese a torz\'it\'as cs\"okkent\'ese \'erdek\'eben.

\item \textbf{Milyen vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt (loss function) haszn\'alnak?} A tan\'it\'ashoz a \textbf{Binary Crossentropy} (bin\'aris keresztentr\'opia) vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt alkalmazt\'ak.

@article{ghaniDeepLearningApproach2023,
  title = {A {{Deep Learning Approach}} for {{Network Intrusion Detection Using}} a {{Small Features Vector}}},
  author = {Ghani, Humera and Virdee, Bal and Salekzamankhani, Shahram},
  year = 2023,
  month = aug,
  journal = {Journal of Cybersecurity and Privacy},
  volume = {3},
  number = {3},
  pages = {451--463},
  issn = {2624-800X},
  doi = {10.3390/jcp3030023},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {With the growth in network usage, there has been a corresponding growth in the nefarious exploitation of this technology. A wide array of techniques is now available that can be used to deal with cyberattacks, and one of them is network intrusion detection. Artificial Intelligence (AI) and Machine Learning (ML) techniques have extensively been employed to identify network anomalies. This paper provides an effective technique to evaluate the classification performance of a deep-learning-based Feedforward Neural Network (FFNN) classifier. A small feature vector is used to detect network traffic anomalies in the UNSW-NB15 and NSL-KDD datasets. The results show that a large feature set can have redundant and unuseful features, and it requires high computation power. The proposed technique exploits a small feature vector and achieves better classification accuracy.},
  langid = {english},
  note = {A v\'alaszt az al\'abbiakban, a megadott forr\'asdokumentum tartalma alapj\'an foglalom \"ossze:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Zero-day t\'amad\'asok:} Az \'attekintett forr\'asok nem haszn\'alj\'ak kifejezetten a "zero-day" (napi nulla) kifejez\'est. Azonban a cikk megk\"ul\"onb\"ozteti a szignat\'ura-alap\'u \'es az \textbf{anom\'alia-alap\'u} technik\'akat, ut\'obbit \'ugy defini\'alva, mint ami a norm\'al forgalmi profilhoz k\'epest mutatkoz\'o elt\'er\'eseket \'eszleli. Mivel a szerz\H ok saj\'at megk\"ozel\'it\'es\"uket anom\'alia-alap\'unak \'es h\'al\'ozati anom\'ali\'ak \'eszlel\'es\'ere alkalmasnak \'irj\'ak le, a megold\'as elm\'eletileg alkalmas ismeretlen t\'amad\'asok detekt\'al\'as\'ara is.
\item \textbf{Tan\'it\'asi paradigma:} A modell \textbf{fel\"ugyelt (supervised)} tanul\'ast alkalmaz, mivel c\'imk\'ezett adatokat ("normal" \'es "attack") haszn\'al a tan\'it\'ashoz \'es a tesztel\'eshez, a teljes\'itm\'enyt pedig a j\'oslatok \'es a t\'enyleges c\'imk\'ek \"osszevet\'es\'evel \'ert\'ekeli.
\item \textbf{Kiegyens\'ulyozatlan adathalmazok kezel\'ese:} A cikk \textbf{nem foglalkozik} saj\'at m\'odszer\'en bel\"ul a kiegyens\'ulyozatlan adathalmazok kezel\'es\'evel. Megjegyzik ugyan, hogy m\'as kutat\'ok alkalmaztak ilyen technik\'akat (p\'eld\'aul GAN-t vagy szint\'ezist), \'es elismerik, hogy ez jav\'ithatja az eredm\'enyeket, de a saj\'at munk\'ajukra n\'ezve kijelentik, hogy ez nem kapcsol\'odik k\"ozvetlen\"ul hozz\'a, \'igy nem t\'argyalj\'ak.
\item \textbf{Concept drift (koncepci\'ov\'alt\'as):} A forr\'asok \textbf{nem tesznek eml\'it\'est} a concept drift jelens\'eg\'er\H ol, \'es nem javasolnak r\'a megold\'ast.
\item \textbf{Interpret\'alhat\'os\'ag:} A cikk \textbf{nem t\'argyalja} az interpret\'alhat\'os\'agot mint szempontot, \'es nem eml\'it olyan technik\'akat, mint a LIME vagy a SHAP.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'esek:}
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item A redundancia \'es az inkonzisztencia elt\'avol\'it\'asa.
\item \textbf{Jellemz\H ok sk\'al\'az\'asa (feature scaling):} az adatok normaliz\'al\'asa a tan\'it\'ashoz \'es tesztel\'eshez.
\item \textbf{C\'imkek\'odol\'as (label encoding):} a nomin\'alis \'ert\'ekek (normal, attack) eg\'eszekk\'e alak\'it\'asa.

\end{itemize}

\item \textbf{Felhaszn\'alt adathalmazok:} \textbf{UNSW-NB15} \'es \textbf{NSL-KDD}.
\item \textbf{Speci\'alis jellemz\H o-m\'ern\"oks\'eg (feature engineering):} A szerz\H ok egy \textbf{cs\"okkentett jellemz\H ovektort} (small features vector) haszn\'alnak. Saj\'at jellemz\H o-kiv\'alaszt\'asi algoritmust nem futtattak, hanem egy kor\'abbi kutat\'asban a \textbf{"fruit fly" (muslica) algoritmussal} azonos\'itott legfontosabb jellemz\H oket vett\'ek \'at (9 jellemz\H o az UNSW-NB15-h\"oz \'es 11 az NSL-KDD-hez).
\item \textbf{A modell t\'ipusa:} M\'ely tanul\'as (deep learning); konkr\'etan egy \textbf{el\H orecsatolt neur\'alis h\'al\'ozat (Feedforward Neural Network - FFNN)}. A modell \"ot rejtett r\'etegb\H ol \'all (50, 30, 15, 5 \'es 2 neuronnal).
\item \textbf{Vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'eny (loss function):} \textbf{Bin\'aris keresztentr\'opia (Binary Cross Entropy)}.
\item \textbf{Tan\'it\'asi algoritmus:} \textbf{Visszaterjeszt\'eses (backpropagation)} algoritmust alkalmaznak az \textbf{Adam optimaliz\'al\'oval} kombin\'alva. A hiperparam\'eterek (p\'eld\'aul tanul\'asi sebess\'eg, aktiv\'aci\'os f\"uggv\'eny) finomhangol\'as\'ahoz a \emph{GridSearchCV} oszt\'alyt haszn\'alt\'ak.

\end{itemize}

\par
A v\'alaszok a \textbf{teljes cikk tartalma alapj\'an} k\'esz\"ultek, mivel a forr\'asok a tanulm\'any minden l\'enyeges fejezet\'et (bevezet\'es, m\'odszertan, eredm\'enyek, k\"ovetkeztet\'esek) tartalmazz\'ak.},

@article{gudnavarCyberThreatDetection2025,
  title = {Cyber {{Threat Detection}} and {{Analysis Using Dual-Layered Approach}}},
  author = {Gudnavar, Anand and Naregal, Keerti and Madagouda, Basavaraj K.},

  month = sep,

  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {Integrating anomaly detection with network traffic analysis presents a promising dual-layered approach to enhance cybersecurity defense and improve threat detection. The challenge is to effectively detect and mitigate sophisticated cyber threats by combining anomaly detection and network traffic analysis to enhance overall security. The objective is to develop a robust dual-layered framework that combines anomaly detection and network traffic analysis to improve the accuracy and efficiency of cyber threat detection and response. Data pre-processing for anomaly detection and network traffic analysis involves cleaning, normalizing, feature extraction, and addressing class imbalance to prepare data for machine learning models. Hierarchical Capacity Particle Filtering (HCPF) enhances detection by tracking dynamic network states and identifying complex anomalies like Distributed Denial of Service (DDoS) attacks. Intrusion Detection Systems with Generative Adversarial Networks (IDS-GAN) enhance cybersecurity by detecting anomalies, generating synthetic traffic for training, improving adaptability to new threats, reducing false positives, and providing comprehensive analysis of network patterns for effective threat mitigation. Agglomerative Hierarchical Clustering Algorithm (AHCA) aids in anomaly detection by identifying outliers, analyzing traffic patterns, revealing relationships between data points, enabling multi-level analysis, and enhancing threat identification in network environments. This proposes a dual-layered approach that integrates anomaly detection with network traffic analysis to enhance cybersecurity defense. The result shows that the proposed method achieves the lowest rate at 1.5\%, outperforming all other approaches, implemented using Python software. The future scope for integrating anomaly detection and network traffic analysis in a dual-layered approach lies in enhancing real-time threat identification through advanced machine learning models and adaptive algorithms.},

@article{guLearningbasedIntrusionDetection2023,
  title = {Learning-Based Intrusion Detection for High-Dimensional Imbalanced Traffic},
  author = {Gu, Yuheng and Yang, Yu and Yan, Yu and Shen, Fang and Gao, Minna},
  year = 2023,
  month = dec,
  journal = {Computer Communications},
  volume = {212},
  pages = {366--376},
  issn = {01403664},
  doi = {10.1016/j.comcom.2023.10.018},
  urldate = {2026-03-04},
  abstract = {With the rapid development of Industry 4.0, industrial big data has become a hot topic in the field of smart manufacturing. However, the large-scale data flow generated by industrial IoT also has serious security challenges. This paper proposes a new multi-module intrusion detection system: DWGF-IDS, which consists of three modules: feature extraction, imbalance processing and traffic anomaly detection. Firstly, a deep denoising autoencoder is used to extract the deep feature representation of the data and improve the generalization performance of the detection model by adding noise to the autoencoder. Secondly, a Wasserstein Generative Adversarial Network - Gradient Penalty optimized based on the self-attention mechanism is used to generate a few classes in the anomalous traffic. Finally, the weights and bias values in the deep denoising autoencoder are transferred to the deep neural network structure, and a DNN improved based on focal loss is used to implement multi-classification detection on the reduced dimensional balanced traffic data. The system performance was evaluated using two datasets, namely NSL-KDD and CSE-CIC-IDS-2018. The multi-classification accuracy achieved on these datasets were 85.05\% and 99.57\%, respectively. The experimental results show that DWGF-IDS effectively copes with the high dimensionality and imbalance of IoT data, improves the detection rate of unknown attacks, and improves the misclassification of rare classes of attack traffic.},
  langid = {english},
  note = {Al\'abb olvashat\'oak a v\'alaszok a forr\'asdokumentum ("Learning-based intrusion detection for high-dimensional imbalanced traffic") alapj\'an:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Alkalmas-e a megold\'as zero-day t\'amad\'asok \'eszlel\'es\'ere?} \textbf{Igen.} A szerz\H ok \'all\'it\'asa szerint a Deep Denoising Autoencoder (DDAE) alkalmaz\'asa n\"oveli a modell \'eszlel\'esi ar\'any\'at az \textbf{ismeretlen t\'amad\'asokkal} szemben. A k\'is\'erleti eredm\'enyek is azt mutatj\'ak, hogy a rendszer hat\'ekonyan kezeli a tesztk\'eszletben l\'ev\H o, de a tan\'it\'ok\'eszletben nem szerepl\H o \'uj t\'amad\'asi t\'ipusokat.
\item \textbf{Milyen tanul\'asi paradigm\'at haszn\'al, \'es hogyan kategoriz\'alhat\'o?} A rendszer egy t\"obbr\'eszes, hibrid megk\"ozel\'it\'est alkalmaz. A funkci\'okinyer\H o modul (DDAE) \textbf{fel\"ugyelet n\'elk\"uli (unsupervised)} mint\'akat haszn\'al a tan\'it\'ashoz. A v\'egs\H o oszt\'alyoz\'o modul (FLDNN) azonban \textbf{fel\"ugyelet melletti (supervised)} tanul\'ast val\'os\'it meg a t\"obboszt\'alyos oszt\'alyoz\'asi feladatokhoz.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk az kiegyens\'ulyozatlan adathalmazok kezel\'es\'evel?} \textbf{Igen.} A megold\'as egy dedik\'alt "imbalance processing" modult tartalmaz. Ez \textbf{v\'eletlenszer\H u alulmintav\'etelez\'est (random undersampling)} alkalmaz a t\"obbs\'egi oszt\'alyokon, valamint egy kombin\'alt fel\"ulmintav\'etelez\'esi elj\'ar\'ast (\textbf{Borderline-SMOTE} \'es \textbf{SAWGAN}) a ritka t\'amad\'asi oszt\'alyok szintetiz\'al\'as\'ara. Emellett az oszt\'alyoz\'oban \textbf{Focal Loss}-t haszn\'alnak a nehezen oszt\'alyozhat\'o mint\'ak s\'ulyoz\'as\'ara.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk a "concept drift" jelens\'eggel?} A forr\'asok eml\'itik a gyorsan v\'altoz\'o ipari IoT k\"ornyezetet \'es az \'uj t\'amad\'asi mint\'ak folyamatos fejl\H od\'es\'et mint kih\'iv\'ast. Megold\'ask\'ent a \textbf{transzfer tanul\'ast (finomhangol\'ast)} javasolj\'ak, amely lehet\H ov\'e teszi a modell gyors adapt\'al\'as\'at a v\'altoz\'o k\"ornyezethez, de kifejezett "concept drift" detekci\'os algoritmust nem mutatnak be.
\item \textbf{Fontos szempont-e az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg?} A forr\'asok \textbf{nem eml\'itik}, hogy az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg (interpretability) kiemelt szempont lett volna a DWGF-IDS fejleszt\'ese sor\'an, \'es nem haszn\'alnak olyan technik\'akat, mint a LIME vagy a SHAP. Az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eget csak m\'as kutat\'asok kontextus\'aban eml\'itik.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen adat-el\H ofeldolgoz\'asi l\'ep\'eseket haszn\'alnak?} Az alkalmazott l\'ep\'esek a k\"ovetkez\H ok:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Adattiszt\'it\'as (cleaning):} a hi\'anyz\'o \'ert\'ekeket tartalmaz\'o adatok elt\'avol\'it\'asa.
\item \textbf{Szelekci\'o (statute):} irrelev\'ans vagy rendellenes jellemz\H ok (pl. Timestamp, Protocol) elt\'avol\'it\'asa.
\item \textbf{One-hot k\'odol\'as:} a kategorikus jellemz\H ok numerikuss\'a alak\'it\'asa.
\item \textbf{Normaliz\'al\'as:} a MinMaxScaler m\'odszer haszn\'alata a jellemz\H ok sk\'al\'az\'as\'ara.

M\'asr\'eszt a SAWGAN modulban \textbf{self-attention (\"onfigyelmi) mechanizmust} alkalmaznak, hogy a gener\'ator jobban megragadja a jellemz\H ok k\"oz\"otti \"osszef\"ugg\'eseket a ritka mint\'ak eset\'eben.

\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt (loss function) haszn\'alnak?}
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item DDAE: \textbf{Huber loss}.
\item SAWGAN: \textbf{Wasserstein t\'avols\'ag} gradiens b\"untet\'essel (gradient penalty).
\item FLDNN: \textbf{Focal loss}.

\end{itemize}

\item \textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'asi algoritmust alkalmaznak?} A modellek optimaliz\'al\'as\'ahoz az \textbf{Adam} algoritmust haszn\'alj\'ak. A tan\'it\'asi folyamat r\'esze a DDAE fel\"ugyelet n\'elk\"uli el\H otan\'it\'asa, a GAN altern\'al\'o tan\'it\'asa, valamint a transzfer tanul\'as (pre-training \'es fine-tuning) az oszt\'alyoz\'o h\'al\'ozatn\'al.

@article{guleriaIDSLADIntrusionDetection2025,
  title = {{{IDS-LAD}}: {{Intrusion}} Detection System Using Logical Analysis of Data},
  shorttitle = {{{IDS-LAD}}},
  author = {Guleria, Bhaumikaditya and Deshmukh, Maroti and Nautiyal, Rakhi},
  year = 2025,
  month = dec,
  journal = {Cluster Computing},
  volume = {28},
  number = {15},
  issn = {1386-7857, 1573-7543},
  doi = {10.1007/s10586-025-05724-z},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {Intrusion Detection Systems are essential for networks to ensure the confidentiality, integrity, and availability of data. In today's increasingly interconnected world, IDS remain a critical component of cybersecurity strategies. Traditional IDS, and even recent Deep Learning based approaches, often suffer from high false positive rates, computational inefficiencies, and limited adaptability. To address these challenges, We proposed a LAD-based IDS model in which we introduced a binarization technique that efficiently extracts critical patterns from heterogeneous and imbalanced network traffic data. The proposed model is evaluated using well-known datasets, such as NSL-KDD and KDD-Cup99, to ensure a rigorous assessment against diverse attack scenarios. Experimental evaluation reveals that the IDS-LAD-based method achieved an accuracy of 80.026\% and 99.872\%, precision of 80.921\% and 99.870\%, recall of 81.260\% and 99.782\%, and an F2-Score of 80.498\% and 99.865\% on the NSL-KDD and KDD-Cup99 datasets, respectively.},
  langid = {english},
  note = {A megadott forr\'asdokumentum ("IDS-LAD: Intrusion detection system using logical analysis of data", 2025) alapj\'an az al\'abbi v\'alaszokat adom a k\'erd\'eseire:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Alkalmas-e a megold\'as zero-day t\'amad\'asok \'eszlel\'es\'ere?} \textbf{Nem.} A cikk szerint a szignat\'ura-alap\'u rendszerek korl\'atja, hogy nem k\'epesek \'eszlelni a zero-day t\'amad\'asokat,. A szerz\H ok kifejezetten kijelentik, hogy a jelenlegi megval\'os\'it\'asuk egy \textbf{szignat\'ura-alap\'u IDS modell}, \'es nem alkalmas anom\'alia-alap\'u \'eszlel\'esre.
\item \textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'asi paradigm\'at haszn\'al?} A modell a \textbf{fel\"ugyelt tanul\'asi (supervised learning)} kateg\'ori\'aba sorolhat\'o. B\'ar a LAD-ot adatb\'any\'aszati m\'odszerk\'ent \'irj\'ak le, a folyamat sor\'an c\'imk\'ezett adatokat haszn\'alnak az adatok binariz\'al\'as\'ahoz (oszt\'alyar\'anyok szerinti pontoz\'as) \'es a szab\'alyok gener\'al\'as\'ahoz (oszt\'alyfedetts\'eg m\'er\'ese),,,.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk az imbolyg\'o (imbalanced) adathalmazok kezel\'es\'evel?} \textbf{Igen.} Az absztrakt eml\'iti, hogy a javasolt binariz\'aci\'os technika hat\'ekonyan vonja ki a kritikus mint\'akat az \textbf{imbolyg\'o h\'al\'ozati forgalmi adatokb\'ol}. Emellett hivatkoznak a LAD \'es a k\"olts\'eg\'erz\'ekeny tanul\'as (cost-sensitive learning) integr\'aci\'oj\'ara is a probl\'ema kezel\'ese \'erdek\'eben.
\item \textbf{Kezeli-e a cikk a concept drift jelens\'eget?} A cikk \textbf{nem t\'argyalja n\'even nevezve a concept drift jelens\'eget}, \'es nem javasol r\'a konkr\'et technikai megold\'ast. Eml\'itik ugyan a "fejl\H od\H o fenyeget\'eseket" mint kih\'iv\'ast, de a modellt statikus szignat\'ura-alap\'uk\'ent defini\'alj\'ak,.
\item \textbf{Fontos szempont-e az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg (interpretability)?} \textbf{Igen, kiemelten.} A LAD m\'odszer l\'enyege az \textbf{\'ertelmezhet\H o \'es magyar\'azhat\'o szab\'alyok} (Boolean f\"uggv\'enyek) gener\'al\'asa, amelyek \'atl\'athat\'ov\'a teszik a d\"ont\'esi folyamatot,. Nem haszn\'alnak olyan k\"uls\H o technik\'akat, mint a LIME vagy a SHAP, mert maga a modell logik\'aja (Sum of Product form\'atum\'u szab\'alyok) eleve \'ertelmezhet\H o,.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'eseket haszn\'alnak?} A binariz\'aci\'os folyamat sor\'an az al\'abbi l\'ep\'eseket alkalmazz\'ak:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Rendez\'es (Sorting):} A folytonos adatok sorba rendez\'ese.
\item \textbf{Binning (Csoportos\'it\'as):} A numerikus adatok intervallumokba sorol\'asa rekurz\'iv v\'ag\'opont-keres\'essel egy specifikus pontoz\'o k\'eplet alapj\'an,,.
\item \textbf{Label encoding (C\'imkek\'odol\'as):} A nomin\'alis kateg\'ori\'ak \'es csoportok el\H ojeles eg\'eszekk\'e alak\'it\'asa,.
\item \textbf{Binary encoding (Bin\'aris k\'odol\'as):} Az eg\'esz sz\'am\'u c\'imk\'ek bitenk\'enti felbont\'asa k\"ul\"on\'all\'o bin\'aris jellemz\H okk\'e,.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Mely adathalmazokat haszn\'alt\'ak vagy tesztelt\'ek a tanulm\'anyban?} A modell \'ert\'ekel\'es\'ehez az \textbf{NSL-KDD+} \'es a \textbf{KDD-Cup99 (10\%)} benchmark adathalmazokat haszn\'alt\'ak,,.
\item \textbf{Alkalmaztak-e speci\'alis feature engineering m\'odszert?} \textbf{Igen.} A f\H o speci\'alis m\'odszer a \textbf{binariz\'aci\'o}, amely a heterog\'en adatokat bin\'aris form\'atumba alak\'itja \'ugy, hogy k\"ozben meg\H orzi a jellemz\H ok megk\"ul\"onb\"oztet\H o erej\'et,. Ennek sor\'an minden k\'odolt bitb\H ol egy \'uj, k\"ul\"on\'all\'o bin\'aris jellemz\H ot hoznak l\'etre,.
\item \textbf{Milyen t\'ipus\'u modellt mutatnak be?} A modell a \textbf{Logical Analysis of Data (LAD)} m\'odszeren alapul, amely Boole-f\"uggv\'enyeket \'es kombinatorikus optimaliz\'al\'ast haszn\'al,.   abstract = {Network Intrusion Detection Systems (NIDS) are essential for identifying and mitigating malicious activities in network environments. As cyber threats evolve in complexity, traditional NIDSs often struggle to detect sophisticated attacks effectively, especially those involving intricate temporal and spatial dependencies within network traffic. The ability to capture these dependencies is crucial for reducing false positive rates and improving detection accuracy. However, existing models face significant challenges, including handling varying sequence lengths and capturing long-range dependencies, which are essential for accurate anomaly detection. This paper proposes a hybrid model that combines Sequence to Sequence (Seq2Seq) architecture with Convolutional Long Short-Term Memory (ConvLSTM) units to address these challenges. This hybrid model handles spatial and temporal dependencies by incorporating convolutional layers within LSTM cells. This enables the model to leverage the spatial feature extraction capabilities of Convolutional Neural Networks (CNN) alongside the sequential learning strengths of LSTM networks. In addition, to enhance the interpretability of the model, the proposed architecture integrates Explainable Artificial Intelligence (XAI) through Local Interpretable Model-agnostic Explanations (LIME). This approach provides insights into the model's decision-making process, highlighting the temporal and spatial features that influence predictions, and improving transparency in detecting anomalies. Experimental evaluations on benchmark datasets, including CIC-IDS 2017, CIC- ToN-IoT, and UNSW-NB15, demonstrate that the proposed hybrid ConvLSTM-Seq2Seq model outperforms existing methods in reducing false positives and achieving higher accuracy. This model offers a promising solution for NIDSs by improving detection capabilities and better handling complex temporal and spatial relationships in network data.},

@article{hashemiDetectingUnseenAnomalies2023,
  title = {Detecting {{Unseen Anomalies}} in {{Network Systems}} by {{Leveraging Neural Networks}}},
  author = {Hashemi, Mohammad J. and Keller, Eric and {Tizpaz-Niari}, Saeid},
  year = 2023,
  month = sep,

  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {Despite all the progress achieved in recent years in detecting anomalies in network systems, detecting unseen anomalies such as zero-day attacks still remained a challenging task. Traditional signature-based Network Intrusion Detection Systems (NIDS) cannot detect such anomalies as there exists no known signature for them. Moreover, Machine Learning-based (ML-based) NIDS trained with a vanilla supervised learning method cannot detect them as they come from a different distribution compared to what the model has been trained on. Domain adaptation techniques help transfer the knowledge gained from a labeled source domain to an unlabeled target domain. Such techniques have the potential to make a model trained on a dataset containing a few network attacks to detect new types of anomalies that might happen in the future. However, recent domain adaptation methods have been mostly designed for images and provide very limited benefits when applied to network traffic. In this paper, we introduce Proportional Progressive Pseudo-Labeling (PPPL), an effective approach for building a more general domain adaptation technique that can be leveraged to detect unseen anomalies in network systems. At the beginning of the training phase, PPPL progressively reduces target domain classification error, by training the model directly with pseudo-labeled target domain samples, while excluding samples with pseudo-labels that are more likely to be wrong from the training set and postponing training on such samples. Our evaluation conducted on the CICIDS2017 dataset shows that PPPL can significantly outperform other baselines in detecting unseen anomalies with up to 58\% improvement based on the average F1 score.},
  copyright = {https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/Xplorehelp/downloads/license-information/IEEE.html},
  annotation = {Access to Full Paper is not available},
  note = {Based on the provided article, \textbf{"Detecting Unseen Anomalies in Network Systems by Leveraging Neural Networks,"} here are the answers to your questions:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Is the solution suitable for detecting zero-day attacks?} \textbf{Yes.} The article explicitly introduces Proportional Progressive Pseudo-Labeling (PPPL) as a domain adaptation technique designed to detect \textbf{unseen anomalies, such as zero-day attacks}, which traditional signature-based and vanilla supervised systems struggle to identify,,.
\item \textbf{What training paradigm does it use and how can it be categorized?} The model uses an \textbf{unsupervised domain adaptation} paradigm,. It leverages knowledge from a labeled source domain and transfers it to an \textbf{unlabeled target domain} using \textbf{pseudo-labeling},,.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Does the article address the handling of imbalanced datasets? If yes, how?} \textbf{Yes.} The authors acknowledge that network datasets are typically highly imbalanced,. They address this by:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Downsampling benign inputs} during the initial training phase so they match the size of malicious inputs.
\item Using an \textbf{"Adjust" function} that changes pseudo-labels of the most uncertain samples if the predicted class ratio exceeds the expected ratio,.
\item Using the \textbf{F1 score} as the primary evaluation metric because it is better suited for imbalanced datasets than accuracy.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Does the article deal with the concept drift phenomenon?} \textbf{Yes.} The authors address "domain shift" or "domain gap," where the input data distribution changes over time,. Their proposed PPPL method is specifically a \textbf{domain adaptation technique} designed to mitigate this shift between the training (source) distribution and the evolving (target) distribution,.
\item \textbf{Is interpretability considered an important aspect?} The article \textbf{does not focus on interpretability} as a core contribution. While it mentions that traditional methods are often used because they have known signatures,, the authors do not apply specific interpretability techniques like LIME or SHAP to their neural network. (Note: SMOTE is mentioned in your query but is a data augmentation technique for imbalance, not an interpretability technique; it is not used in this study).
\item 
\par
\textbf{What data preprocessing steps are used?} The preprocessing steps include:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Grouping packets} based on their source and destination IP addresses.
\item \textbf{Feature extraction} from the Ethernet, IP, TCP, and UDP headers.
\item Extracting \textbf{inter-arrival time} and \textbf{direction} for each packet.
\item \textbf{Vectorization}: In total, 29 features are extracted per packet.
\item \textbf{Concatenation}: Feature vectors of every \textbf{20 consecutive packets} in a group are concatenated to form a single input for the classifier.
\item \textbf{Downsampling} of benign traffic to balance the classes during training.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Which datasets were used or tested in the study?} The primary evaluation was conducted on the \textbf{CICIDS2017} dataset,,. To test generalization, the authors also used \textbf{CIC-ToN-IoT} and \textbf{NF-UNSW-NB15},.
\item \textbf{Is there any special feature engineering method applied?} \textbf{Yes.} Beyond standard header extraction, the authors use a \textbf{temporal aggregation method} where they concatenate the features of \textbf{20 consecutive packets} to capture the behavior of network flows over time.
\item \textbf{What type of model is presented?} The authors present a \textbf{deep learning} model. Specifically, it is a \textbf{two-layer fully-connected neural network} (2048 {$\rightarrow$} 2 architecture) using \textbf{ReLU non-linearity}.
\item \textbf{What loss function is used?} The primary loss function for the PPPL algorithm is \textbf{Mean Square Error (MSE)},. The authors argue that MSE is superior to Cross-Entropy (CE) for pseudo-labeling because it forces target samples into tighter clusters in the logit space, effectively reducing the domain gap,,.
\item 
\par
\textbf{What training algorithm is applied?} The authors apply their custom \textbf{Proportional Progressive Pseudo-Labeling (PPPL)} algorithm. This involves iteratively:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item Assigning pseudo-labels based on model predictions.
\item Calculating a \textbf{certainty score} for each sample.
\item Assigning \textbf{weights} to samples based on certainty and \textbf{progressively including} more samples into the training set as the epochs increase,.
\item Optimization is performed using the \textbf{Adam optimizer},.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Article Accessibility:} The \textbf{full article was accessible} and used for this response. The information provided is drawn from the detailed methodology, design insights, and evaluation sections of the paper, not just the metadata,,.

\end{itemize}},

  abstract = {The exponential growth in data exchange has increased cybersecurity risks, making privacy-preserving intrusion detection a challenge. Traditional intrusion detection systems (IDS) often rely on centralized architectures that compromise data privacy and are vulnerable to security breaches. Moreover, current privacy-preserving IDS approaches have high computational overhead, limited real-time applicability, and inefficient processing of encrypted data. To address these challenges, Empowering Privacy-Preserving Anomaly Detection (EPAD), a novel framework integrating a lightweight homomorphic encryption (HE) library and federated learning (FL), is proposed. This paper optimizes the homomorphic encryption library to significantly reduce computational overhead while preserving data privacy. It also addresses heterogeneous traffic patterns by integrating multiple real-world and benchmark datasets, including BOT-IoT, NSL-KDD, ToN-IoT, and UNSW-NB15, which enables practical deployment in resource-constrained environments such as IoT and edge computing. Unlike conventional IDS, EPAD ensures data confidentiality by performing encrypted computations locally at each client while only sharing encrypted model updates with the central server. By optimizing the homomorphic encryption library, EPAD overcomes the high computational overhead associated with encrypted learning, making it more scalable and efficient. Experimental results demonstrate that EPAD achieves an accuracy of 98.21\%, a precision of 99.14\%, a recall of 99.01\%, and an F1-score of 97.13\%, while reducing execution time to 0.2 s. These findings confirm that EPAD offers a robust, scalable, and real-time privacy-preserving IDS.},

B\'ar a cikk eml\'iti, hogy a m\'elytanul\'as mindk\'et paradigm\'aval haszn\'alhat\'o, a javasolt munkafolyamat sor\'an \textbf{c\'imk\'ezett p\'eld\'anyokat} haszn\'alnak az oszt\'alyoz\'ashoz, \'es az eredm\'enyeket konf\'uzi\'os m\'atrixszal, precizit\'assal \'es felid\'ez\'essel \'ert\'ekelik.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk a kiegyens\'ulyozatlan adathalmazok kezel\'es\'evel?} A cikk \textbf{elismeri a probl\'em\'at}, de a javasolt modellben nem alkalmaz specifikus technik\'at (mint pl. a SMOTE) a kezel\'es\'ere. A "J\"ov\H obeli ir\'anyok" r\'eszben a szerz\H ok megjegyzik, hogy bizonyos t\'amad\'asok sikertelen oszt\'alyoz\'as\'anak egyik oka az \textbf{adathalmaz kiegyens\'ulyozatlans\'aga}, \'es a j\"ov\H oben t\"orekednek az optim\'alisabb tan\'it\'oadatok el\'er\'es\'ere.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk a concept drift jelens\'eg\'evel?} A "concept drift" kifejez\'es nem szerepel a sz\"ovegben, de a szerz\H ok eml\'itik a \textbf{folyamatosan v\'altoz\'o kibert\'amad\'asok} jelentette kih\'iv\'ast, amelyre a m\'elytanul\'asi (DL) technik\'ak sk\'al\'azhat\'os\'aga \'es alkalmazkod\'ok\'epess\'ege jelenthet megold\'ast.
\item \textbf{Fontos szempont-e az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg (interpretability)?} A forr\'as \textbf{nem kezeli kiemelt szempontk\'ent} a saj\'at modellje eset\'eben. Az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eget csak a kapcsol\'od\'o munk\'ak k\"oz\"ott eml\'itik egy m\'asik kutat\'as (Maonan et al.) kapcs\'an, amely a SHAP technik\'at haszn\'alta, de a cikk \'altal javasolt CNN-GRU modellhez nem kapcsolnak ilyen technik\'at.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'eseket haszn\'alnak?} A haszn\'alt l\'ep\'esek:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Redund\'ans vagy duplik\'alt p\'eld\'anyok elt\'avol\'it\'asa} az adathalmaz dimenzi\'oj\'anak cs\"okkent\'ese \'erdek\'eben.
\item \textbf{Jellemz\H o-optimaliz\'al\'as (Feature Selection)} a Pearson-f\'ele korrel\'aci\'os egy\"utthat\'o haszn\'alat\'aval a nem korrel\'al\'o, megk\"ul\"onb\"oztet\H o jellemz\H ok kiv\'alaszt\'as\'ara.
\item Az adathalmaz feloszt\'asa \textbf{tan\'it\'o (67\%) \'es tesztel\H o (33\%) halmazra}.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Mely adathalmazokat haszn\'alt\'ak vagy tesztelt\'ek a tanulm\'anyban?} A f\H o vizsg\'alt adathalmaz a \textbf{CICIDS-2017} benchmark dataset.
\item \textbf{Is there any special feature engineering method applied?} Igen, a \textbf{Pearson-f\'ele korrel\'aci\'os egy\"utthat\'ot (Pearson's Correlation Coefficient)} alkalmazz\'ak sz\H ur\H ok\'ent, hogy kiv\'alassz\'ak a legjobb jellemz\H o-r\'eszhalmazt, amivel az eredeti 77 jellemz\H ot k\"or\"ulbel\"ul a fel\'ere (sub-datasetenk\'ent v\'altoz\'oan, de jellemz\H oen 44 al\'a) cs\"okkentett\'ek.
\item \textbf{Milyen t\'ipus\'u modellt mutatnak be?} Egy \textbf{hibrid m\'elytanul\'asi (Hybrid Deep Learning)} modellt, amely a \textbf{CNN (Convolutional Neural Network)} \'es a \textbf{GRU (Gated Recurrent Unit)} architekt\'ur\'akat \"otv\"ozi. A strukt\'ura h\'arom CNN r\'etegb\H ol \'es k\'et GRU szekvenci\'ab\'ol \'all.
\item \textbf{Milyen vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt (loss function) haszn\'alnak?} A modell a \textbf{Sparse Categorical Cross-Entropy} technik\'at haszn\'alja a vesztes\'eg sz\'am\'it\'as\'ara. (Az algoritmus-le\'ir\'asban a 'categorical\_crossentropy' is eml\'it\'esre ker\"ul).
\item \textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'asi algoritmust alkalmaznak?} Az optimaliz\'al\'ashoz az \textbf{Adagrad optimert} haszn\'alj\'ak. A tan\'it\'as sor\'an k\"ul\"onb\"oz\H o sz\'am\'u \textbf{epoch-ot (20 \'es 100 k\"oz\"ott)} alkalmaztak az egyes al-adathalmazok m\'eret\'et\H ol f\"ugg\H oen.

\end{itemize}

\par
\textbf{Megjegyz\'es az el\'erhet\H os\'egr\H ol:} A v\'alaszok a Sensors foly\'oiratban megjelent teljes cikk kivonatai alapj\'an k\'esz\"ultek, nem csup\'an metaadatokb\'ol. A rendelkez\'esre \'all\'o sz\"oveg r\'eszletes inform\'aci\'okat tartalmazott a m\'odszertanr\'ol, az algoritmusr\'ol \'es az eredm\'enyekr\H ol.},

@article{heReinforcementLearningMeets2024,
  title = {Reinforcement {{Learning Meets Network Intrusion Detection}}: {{A Transferable}} and {{Adaptable Framework}} for {{Anomaly Behavior Identification}}},
  shorttitle = {Reinforcement {{Learning Meets Network Intrusion Detection}}},
  author = {He, Mingshu and Wang, Xiaojuan and Wei, Peng and Yang, Liu and Teng, Yinglei and Lyu, Renjian},
  year = 2024,
  month = apr,
  journal = {IEEE Transactions on Network and Service Management},
  volume = {21},
  number = {2},
  pages = {2477--2492},
  issn = {1932-4537},
  doi = {10.1109/TNSM.2024.3352586},
  urldate = {2025-06-12},
  abstract = {Anomaly detection plays an essential role in network security and traffic classification. Many studies have focused on anomaly detection to improve network security, including machine learning and deep learning methods. These methods often require numerous samples and must obtain the results by classifying the entire data set, thereby limiting their inflexibility. Although transfer and multitask learning have achieved some results in the model's transferability, these methods must manually label or reprocess the test set. These problems limit the application of previous methods in network security management. To solve these problems, we propose a transferable and adaptable network intrusion detection system (TA-NIDS) based on deep reinforcement learning. The interaction process between the agent and the environment varies every time. A small-scale data set can be used to produce many interactive processes. Therefore, robustness is guaranteed when there are few samples. Then, a reasonable reward function allows the agent to learn how to first choose outliers without classifying the entire data set. This makes the TA-NIDS more adaptable to the scene when we prioritize apparent outliers. More importantly, the original features are transformed into the state of the environment, so no requirement exists for the feature dimension. Furthermore, the general rather than the specific state of one data set makes the model transferable to other data sets. The experimental results for IDS2017, IDS2018, NSL-KDD, UNSW-NB15 and CIC-IoT2023 show that the proposed framework maintains good accuracy when prioritizing outliers and transferability are prioritized simultaneously.},
  keywords = {adaptable framework,anomaly detection,Anomaly detection,Behavioral sciences,Data models,Deep reinforcement learning,Feature extraction,Not checked,Reinforcement learning,robustness,Telecommunication traffic,Training,transferable framework},
  annotation = {Access to Full Paper is not available},

A teljes cikk nem hozz\'af\'erhet\H o, \'igy bizonyos technikai r\'eszletek nem ismertek.

\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Alkalmaztak-e speci\'alis feature engineering m\'odszert?} Igen, a modell az \textbf{eredeti jellemz\H oket a k\"ornyezet \'allapot\'av\'a transzform\'alja}, aminek k\"osz\"onhet\H oen a le\'ir\'as szerint \textbf{nincs megk\"ot\'es a jellemz\H ok dimenzi\'oj\'ara (no requirement for feature dimension)}, \'es a modell \'altal\'anosabb \'allapotokat tanul meg a specifikus jellemz\H ok helyett.
\item \textbf{Milyen t\'ipus\'u modellt mutatnak be?} A javasolt modell neve \textbf{TA-NIDS} (Transferable and Adaptable Network Intrusion Detection System), amely \textbf{m\'ely meger\H os\'it\'eses tanul\'ason (Deep Reinforcement Learning)} alapul.
\item \textbf{Milyen vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt (loss function) haszn\'alnak?} Az absztrakt \textbf{nem nevezi meg} a haszn\'alt vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt.

@article{hirsiHSFHybridSVMRF2025,
  title = {{{HSF}}: {{A Hybrid SVM-RF Machine Learning Framework}} for {{Dual-Plane DDoS Detection}} and {{Mitigation}} in {{Software-Defined Networks}}},
  shorttitle = {{{HSF}}},
  author = {Hirsi, Abdinasir and Audah, Lukman and Alhartomi, Mohammed A. and Salh, Adeb and Ansa, Godwin Okon and Hamdi, Mustafa Maad and Saputri, Diani Galih and Ahmed, Salman and Farah, Abdullahi},

  urldate = {2026-03-04},
  abstract = {Software-defined networking (SDN) has revolutionized network management by centralizing control through software, thereby enabling dynamic traffic adjustments that are independent of the data plane. However, this innovation introduces significant security vulnerabilities because the existing solutions are largely adaptations of traditional methods and fail to address the unique challenges of SDN environments. To address this issue, this study proposes a machine-learning (ML)-based intrusion detection framework tailored specifically for SDN. In particular, the framework utilizes a hybrid model that combines a Support Vector Machine (SVM) and Random Forest (RF) classifiers (HSF), which significantly improves intrusion detection accuracy. Specifically, the proposed solution is structured as a three-layer protection mechanism. First, the Data Plane Monitoring layer examines features, such as packet count and byte count, to detect anomalies. Second, the Control Plane Monitoring layer evaluates attributes such as the source IP, destination IP, and protocols to identify suspicious activity. Finally, the Detection Layer leverages the hybrid ML approach to further strengthen detection capabilities and ensure timely responses. Importantly, the experimental results reveal that the HSF technique achieves an anomaly detection rate exceeding 99\% across both data and control planes. This highlights its efficacy in securing the next-generation SDN networks.},
  copyright = {https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/legalcode},

\begin{itemize}

A keretrendszer c\'elja a kor\'abban ismeretlen DDoS t\'amad\'asok felismer\'ese \'es kategoriz\'al\'asa.
\item \textbf{Milyen tanul\'asi paradigm\'at haszn\'al, \'es hogyan kategoriz\'alhat\'o?} A keretrendszer \textbf{hibrid g\'epi tanul\'asi (Hybrid ML)} megk\"ozel\'it\'est alkalmaz. Els\H osorban a \textbf{fel\"ugyelt tanul\'as (supervised learning)} kateg\'ori\'aj\'aba sorolhat\'o, mivel mind a Random Forest (RF), mind a Support Vector Machine (SVM) fel\"ugyelt m\'odszerk\'ent van le\'irva a sz\"ovegben.

\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'eseket haszn\'alnak?} A cikkben eml\'itett el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'esek a k\"ovetkez\H ok:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Jellemz\H ok kinyer\'ese} a csomagfejl\'ecekb\H ol \'es statisztikai adatokb\'ol.
\item Az adatok \textbf{t\'abl\'azatos form\'atumba rendez\'ese} oszt\'alyoz\'asi c\'imk\'ekkel.
\item Az adathalmaz \textbf{feloszt\'asa tan\'it\'o (70-80\%) \'es tesztel\H o (20-30\%) k\'eszletekre}.
\item \textbf{K\"usz\"ob\'ert\'ek alap\'u el\H osz\H ur\'es}, ahol a csomagokat a csomagsebess\'eg (packet rate) alapj\'an sz\H urik a g\'epi tanul\'asi f\'azis el\H ott.

@article{horeSequentialDeepLearning2024,
  title = {A Sequential Deep Learning Framework for a Robust and Resilient Network Intrusion Detection System},
  author = {Hore, Soumyadeep and Ghadermazi, Jalal and Shah, Ankit and Bastian, Nathaniel D.},
  year = 2024,
  month = sep,
  journal = {Computers \& Security},
  volume = {144},
  pages = {103928},
  issn = {0167-4048},
  doi = {10.1016/j.cose.2024.103928},
  urldate = {2025-06-12},
  abstract = {Ensuring the security and integrity of computer and network systems is of utmost importance in today's digital landscape. Network intrusion detection systems (NIDS) play a critical role in continuously monitoring network traffic and identifying unauthorized or potentially malicious activities that could compromise the confidentiality, availability, and integrity of these systems. However, traditional NIDS face a daunting challenge in effectively adapting to the evolving tactics of cyber attackers. To address this challenge, we propose a multistage artificial intelligence enabled framework for intrusion detection in network traffic, capable of handling zero-day, out-of-distribution, and adversarial evasion attacks. Our framework comprises three sequential deep neural network (DNN) architectures: one for the classifier and two for specific autoencoders, designed to effectively detect both known attack patterns and novel, previously unseen samples. We introduce an innovative transfer learning technique where specific combinations of neurons and layers in the DNN architectures are frozen during one-shot learning to enhance the framework's robustness to novel attacks. To validate the effectiveness of our framework, we conducted extensive experimentation using publicly available benchmark intrusion detection data sets. Leveraging the one-shot learning approach in the transfer learning component of the framework, we demonstrate continuous improvement in detection accuracy for both known and novel network traffic patterns. The results demonstrate the effectiveness of the multiple stages in the framework by achieving, on average, 98.5\% accuracy in detecting various attacks.},
  keywords = {Anomaly detector,Malicious packet classifier,Multistage AI-based NIDS,Network intrusion detection system (NIDS),Novelty detector,One-shot learning,Sequential deep neural networks,Transfer learning},
  note = {A Sakong \'es Kim (2024) cikk\'ehez hasonl\'oan a megadott forr\'asdokumentum ("Deep ResNIDS") alapj\'an az al\'abbi v\'alaszokat adom a k\'erd\'eseire:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Alkalmas-e a megold\'as zero-day t\'amad\'asok detekt\'al\'as\'ara?} \textbf{Igen.} A keretrendszert kifejezetten \'ugy tervezt\'ek, hogy kezelni tudja a \textbf{zero-day}, az eloszl\'ason k\'iv\"uli (OOD) \'es az advezeri\'alis kij\'atsz\'asos t\'amad\'asokat. Az anom\'alia-detekt\'al\'o \'es \'ujdons\'ag-detekt\'al\'o (novelty detector) szakaszok k\'epesek felismerni a kor\'abban nem l\'atott mint\'akat is.
\item \textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'asi paradigm\'at haszn\'al, \'es hogyan kategoriz\'alhat\'o?} A megold\'as egy \textbf{hibrid} megk\"ozel\'it\'est alkalmaz, amely egyes\'iti a \textbf{fel\"ugyelt (supervised)}, a \textbf{fel\"ugyelet n\'elk\"uli (unsupervised)} \'es a \textbf{transzfer tanul\'asi (transfer learning)} m\'odszertanokat. Az els\H o szakasz (oszt\'alyoz\'o) fel\"ugyelt, m\'ig a m\'asodik \'es harmadik szakasz (autoencoderek) fel\"ugyelet n\'elk\"uli tanul\'ast alkalmaz. A friss\'it\'eshez \textbf{one-shot learning} technik\'at haszn\'alnak.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk az imbalansz adatk\'eszletek kezel\'es\'evel? Ha igen, hogyan?} A cikk eml\'it\'est tesz m\'as tanulm\'anyokr\'ol, amelyek s\'ulyoz\'asi m\'odszerekkel kezelik az imbalanszot, de a saj\'at m\'odszertan\'aban nem hangs\'ulyozza kiemelt l\'ep\'esk\'ent a hagyom\'anyos \'ertelemben vett oszt\'aly-kiegyenl\'it\'est. Ugyanakkor az \'ujdons\'ag-detekt\'al\'o modellt \textbf{szintetikusan gener\'alt advezeri\'alis mint\'akkal} tan\'itj\'ak, ami seg\'it a ritka t\'amad\'asi form\'ak felismer\'es\'eben.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk a concept drift jelens\'eg\'evel? Ha igen, javasolnak-e r\'a megold\'ast?} \textbf{Igen.} B\'ar a kifejez\'est nem haszn\'alj\'ak expliciten, a cikk k\"ozponti t\'em\'aja a \textbf{folyamatos tanul\'as (continuous learning)} hi\'anya a szakirodalomban, \'es megold\'ask\'ent a \textbf{one-shot transzfer tanul\'ason alapul\'o \'ujratan\'it\'asi strat\'egi\'at} javasolj\'ak. Ez lehet\H ov\'e teszi, hogy a rendszer adapt\'ivan fejl\H odj\"on az id\H oben v\'altoz\'o fenyeget\'esekkel szemben.
\item \textbf{Fontos szempont-e az interpret\'alhat\'os\'ag? Ha igen, milyen technik\'akat haszn\'alnak?} Az interpret\'alhat\'os\'ag \textbf{nem jelenik meg fontos szempontk\'ent} a javasolt keretrendszer le\'ir\'as\'aban, \'es a szerz\H ok nem eml\'itenek olyan technik\'akat, mint a LIME vagy a SMOTE.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'eseket haszn\'alnak?} A csomag-szint\H u jellemz\H ok kinyer\'ese sor\'an az al\'abbi l\'ep\'eseket alkalmazz\'ak:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Ethernet fejl\'ec elt\'avol\'it\'asa} (14 b\'ajt, pl. MAC c\'imek).
\item \textbf{IP fejl\'ec forr\'as- \'es c\'elc\'imeinek elt\'avol\'it\'asa} (11 b\'ajt).
\item \textbf{Port inform\'aci\'ok elt\'avol\'it\'asa} a transzport r\'eteg fejl\'ec\'eb\H ol (4 b\'ajt).

  abstract = {In recent years, deep learning techniques have been widely applied to network intrusion detection. However, current detection methods suffer from a low detection rate, rendering them useless in fighting unknown attacks. Thus, in this article, we proposed a hybrid detection system based on deep learning techniques. First, to understand comprehensively the pattern of normal network traffic and anomaly detection, a designed nonsymmetric autoencoder (NAE) is proposed. The NAE extracts the latent feature of network traffic with two different convolution neural networks and multiple linear layers are applied to the NAE's decoder to reconstruct the input. Besides, a latent feature extraction scheme using the NAE encoder is proposed and a deep neural network (DNN) is trained with this latent feature to achieve detection. In addition, in order to strengthen system stability, a hybrid scheme is proposed that considers the detection results of NAE and DNN, and makes the comprehensive decision. Experiments are performed on the NSL-KDD, N-baIoT and BoT-IoT datasets respectively to evaluate the proposed hybrid model. The evaluation is carried out through classification evaluation indexes such as accuracy, precision, recall, F1-score. The results have shown that our proposed hybrid model gets the best detection ability of abnormal traffic compared with several state-of-the-art intrusion detection methods.},

@article{huangLaplacianChangePoint2024,
  title = {Laplacian {{Change Point Detection}} for {{Single}} and {{Multi-view Dynamic Graphs}}},
  author = {Huang, Shenyang and Coulombe, Samy and Hitti, Yasmeen and Rabbany, Reihaneh and Rabusseau, Guillaume},
  year = 2024,
  month = apr,
  journal = {ACM Transactions on Knowledge Discovery from Data},
  volume = {18},
  number = {3},
  pages = {1--32},
  issn = {1556-4681, 1556-472X},
  doi = {10.1145/3631609},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {Dynamic graphs are rich data structures that are used to model complex relationships between entities over time. In particular, anomaly detection in temporal graphs is crucial for many real-world applications such as intrusion identification in network systems, detection of ecosystem disturbances, and detection of epidemic outbreaks. In this article, we focus on change point detection in dynamic graphs and address three main challenges associated with this problem: (i) how to compare graph snapshots across time, (ii) how to capture temporal dependencies, and (iii) how to combine different views of a temporal graph. To solve the above challenges, we first propose Laplacian Anomaly Detection (LAD) which uses the spectrum of graph Laplacian as the low dimensional embedding of the graph structure at each snapshot. LAD explicitly models short-term and long-term dependencies by applying two sliding windows. Next, we propose MultiLAD, a simple and effective generalization of LAD to multi-view graphs. MultiLAD provides the first change point detection method for multi-view dynamic graphs. It aggregates the singular values of the normalized graph Laplacian from different views through the scalar power mean operation. Through extensive synthetic experiments, we show that (i) LAD and MultiLAD are accurate and outperforms state-of-the-art baselines and their multi-view extensions by a large margin, (ii) MultiLAD's advantage over contenders significantly increases when additional views are available, and (iii) MultiLAD is highly robust to noise from individual views. In five real-world dynamic graphs, we demonstrate that LAD and MultiLAD identify significant events as top anomalies such as the implementation of government COVID-19 interventions which impacted the population mobility in multi-view traffic networks.},

@article{idrissiFedANIDSFederatedLearning2023,
  title = {Fed-{{ANIDS}}: {{Federated}} Learning for Anomaly-Based Network Intrusion Detection Systems},
  shorttitle = {Fed-{{ANIDS}}},
  author = {Idrissi, Meryem Janati and Alami, Hamza and El Mahdaouy, Abdelkader and El Mekki, Abdellah and Oualil, Soufiane and Yartaoui, Zakaria and Berrada, Ismail},
  year = 2023,
  month = dec,
  journal = {Expert Systems with Applications},
  volume = {234},
  pages = {121000},
  issn = {09574174},
  doi = {10.1016/j.eswa.2023.121000},
  urldate = {2026-03-04},
  abstract = {As computer networks and interconnected systems continue to gain widespread adoption, ensuring cybersecurity has become a prominent concern for organizations, regardless of their scale or size. Meanwhile, centralized machine learning-based Anomaly Detection (AD) methods have shown promising results in improving the accuracy and efficiency of Network Intrusion Detection Systems (NIDS). However, new challenges arise such as privacy concerns and regulatory restrictions that must be tackled. Federated Learning (FL) has emerged as a solution that allows distributed clients to collaboratively train a shared model while preserving the privacy of their local data. In this paper, we propose Fed-ANIDS, a NIDS that leverages AD and FL to address the privacy concerns associated with centralized models. To detect intrusions, we compute an intrusion score based on the reconstruction error of normal traffic using various AD models, including simple autoencoders, variational autoencoders, and adversarial autoencoders. We thoroughly evaluate Fed-ANIDS using various settings and popular datasets, including USTC-TFC2016, CIC-IDS2017, and CSE-CIC-IDS2018. The proposed method demonstrates its effectiveness by achieving high performance in terms of different metrics while preserving the data privacy of distributed clients. Our findings highlight that autoencoder-based models outperform other generative adversarial network-based models, achieving high detection accuracy coupled with fewer false alarms. In addition, the FL framework (FedProx), which is a generalization and re-parametrization of the standard method for FL (FedAvg), achieves better results. The code is available at https://github.com/meryemJanatiIdrissi/Fed-ANID},
  langid = {english},
  note = {A megadott forr\'asok alapj\'an a \textbf{Fed-ANIDS} keretrendszerr\H ol sz\'ol\'o cikk k\'erd\'eseire az al\'abbi v\'alaszok adhat\'ok:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Zero-day t\'amad\'asok:} \textbf{Igen}, a megold\'as alkalmas ezek \'eszlel\'es\'ere. A cikk kifejti, hogy m\'ig a szignat\'ura-alap\'u rendszerek (SNIDS) k\'eptelenek azonos\'itani a kor\'abban nem l\'atott fenyeget\'eseket (\textbf{zero-day}), az anom\'alia-alap\'u rendszerek (ANIDS) --- mint amilyen a Fed-ANIDS is --- adapt\'ivabbak \'es hat\'ekonyabbak az \'uj fenyeget\'esek felismer\'es\'eben.
\item \textbf{Tan\'it\'asi paradigma:} A modell \textbf{fel\"ugyelet n\'elk\"uli (unsupervised)} tanul\'ast haszn\'al. A tan\'it\'as sor\'an a modell kiz\'ar\'olag \textbf{norm\'al forgalmi mint\'akat} kap, hogy megtanulja a rendszer alapszint\H u viselked\'es\'et, \'es k\'es\H obb \'eszlelje az att\'ol val\'o elt\'er\'eseket.
\item \textbf{Kiegyens\'ulyozatlan adatk\'eszletek:} \textbf{Igen}, a cikk kezeli ezt a k\'erd\'est. A szerz\H ok megjegyzik, hogy a val\'os h\'al\'ozati forgalomban a norm\'al adatok sokkal b\H os\'egesebbek, mint a t\'amad\'asi mint\'ak. Megold\'ask\'ent a modellt \textbf{kiz\'ar\'olag norm\'al adatokon} tan\'itj\'ak, \'es a tan\'it\'o k\'eszletet egyenl\H oen \'es v\'eletlenszer\H uen osztj\'ak el a kliensek k\"oz\"ott, biztos\'itva a m\'elt\'anyos adateloszl\'ast.
\item \textbf{Concept drift (koncepci\'ov\'alt\'as):} A forr\'asok \textbf{nem eml\'itik kifejezetten} a "concept drift" kifejez\'est, \'es nem javasolnak r\'a specifikus megold\'ast. A j\"ov\H obeni munk\'ak k\"oz\"ott azonban szerepel a \textbf{dom\'en generaliz\'aci\'o} kutat\'asa a teljes\'itm\'eny jav\'it\'asa \'erdek\'eben k\"ul\"onb\"oz\H o h\'al\'ozati k\"ornyezetekben.
\item \textbf{\'Ertelmezhet\H os\'eg (Interpretability):} A cikk megeml\'iti, hogy a t\'abl\'azatos adatk\'eszletek \textbf{\'ertelmezhet\H obbek}, mint a nyers forgalmi k\'epek, de \textbf{nem mutat be specifikus technik\'akat} (p\'eld\'aul LIME vagy SHAP) a modell d\"ont\'eseinek magyar\'azat\'ara. (Megjegyz\'es: A SMOTE-ot a k\'erd\'es technika p\'eld\'ajak\'ent eml\'iti, de az egy adat-kiegyens\'ulyoz\'o elj\'ar\'as, amit ez a cikk nem haszn\'al).
\item 
\par
\textbf{Adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'esek:}
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{pcapfix:} A s\'er\"ult PCAP f\'ajlok jav\'it\'asa.
\item \textbf{reordercap:} A csomagok id\H ob\'elyeg szerinti sorba rendez\'ese.
\item \textbf{CICFlowMeter:} 87 statisztikai forgalmi jellemz\H o kinyer\'ese.
\item \textbf{Feature encoding:} A karakteres adatok numerikus \'ert\'ekekk\'e alak\'it\'asa.
\item \textbf{Min-max scaling:} Az adatok normaliz\'al\'asa a 0 \'es 1 k\"oz\"otti tartom\'anyba.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Haszn\'alt adatk\'eszletek:} A tanulm\'anyban a \textbf{USTC-TFC2016}, a \textbf{CIC-IDS2017} \'es a \textbf{CSE-CIC-IDS2018} adatk\'eszleteket haszn\'alt\'ak.
\item \textbf{Speci\'alis feature engineering:} A szerz\H ok a \textbf{CICFlowMeter friss\'itett verzi\'oj\'at} alkalmazz\'ak a megb\'izhat\'obb forgalmi jellemz\H ok kinyer\'es\'ere, amivel kik\"usz\"ob\"olik a kor\'abbi adatk\'eszletek hib\'ait (pl. redundancia, rossz c\'imk\'ez\'es).
\item \textbf{Modell t\'ipusa:} A Fed-ANIDS egy \textbf{autoencoder-alap\'u (AE)} m\'ely tanul\'asi modell. H\'arom vari\'anst vizsg\'altak: egyszer\H u autoencoder, vari\'aci\'os autoencoder (\textbf{VAE}) \'es adverz\'alis autoencoder (\textbf{AAE}).
\item \textbf{Vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'eny (Loss function):} Az alapmodell a \textbf{n\'egyzetes hib\'at (Mean Squared Error - MSE)} minimaliz\'alja a bemenet \'es a rekonstrukci\'o k\"oz\"ott. A FedProx algoritmus haszn\'alata eset\'en ehhez egy \textbf{proxim\'alis regul\'aci\'os tagot} is hozz\'aadnak. Az AAE modelln\'el tov\'abbi adverz\'alis vesztes\'egek (\$L\_\textbraceleft dis\textbraceright\$, \$L\_\textbraceleft prior\textbraceright\$) is megjelennek.
\item \textbf{Tan\'it\'asi algoritmus:} A keretrendszer a \textbf{feder\'alt tanul\'as (FL)} m\'odszer\'et alkalmazza, konkr\'etan a \textbf{FedAvg} \'es a \textbf{FedProx} algoritmusokat. A lok\'alis optimaliz\'al\'ashoz \textbf{sztochasztikus gradiens ereszked\'est (SGD)} vagy mini-batch gradiens ereszked\'est haszn\'alnak.

\end{itemize}

  abstract = {With the advent of communication infrastructure and automation, conventional substations have transformed into digital substations as cyber-physical systems. These substations lack matured and intelligent techniques to ensure cybersecurity, making them vulnerable to cyber-attacks. Several studies have been conducted in the literature on effective Intrusion Detection Systems (IDS) for digital substations based on supervised machine learning techniques. However, there are persistent challenges due to unlabeled datasets, and inaccurate detection with high false alarm rates. This paper proposes an intelligent intrusion detection mechanism (IIDM) with high accuracy to detect anomalies in substation Generix Object-Oriented Substation Event (GOOSE) packets caused by Data Manipulation, Man-in-the-middle attacks (MITM), replay, and Denial of Service (DoS). The IIDM is based on data-driven (statistical and ensemble unsupervised learning techniques) and model-based techniques considering the Single Line Diagram of the substation. The efficiency of the proposed IIDM for forensic analysis is discussed using standard GOOSE data sets. The accuracy of the proposed mechanism is tested in real-time on a substation testbed, demonstrating the capabilities of IIDM to provide suitable real-time intrusion detection for substations with high accuracy.},
