Signature-based misuse detection, hybrid detection, and anomaly detection, while effective in isolation, often struggle to keep pace with the ever-evolving tactics employed by attackers. This research stems from the pressing need to develop intrusion detection models that offer improved accuracy, adaptability, and robustness in the face of rapidly changing attack vectors. Our study leverages a comprehensive set of experiments conducted on diverse datasets, including N-BaIoT, NSL-KDD, and CICIDS2017. The primary focus is on the evaluation and comparison of machine learning algorithms such as Random Forest, XGBoost, and decision trees. Notably, our research culminates in the development of a hybrid intrusion detection model that combines the strengths of these algorithms. The results of our experiments indicate that the hybrid model, particularly when combining Random Forest and XGBoost, exhibits exceptional accuracy. This approach outperforms individual algorithms, achieving an accuracy rate of 97\% in certain cases. We attribute this success to the ensemble learning approach, which capitalizes on the consensus of diverse classifiers. In conclusion, our research not only demonstrates the effectiveness of ensemble learning in enhancing intrusion detection but also underscores the importance of continuous adaptation in the face of evolving threats. By leveraging network traffic data, our hybrid model offers a promising avenue for bolstering intrusion classification in Big Data environments.},

@article{kamalAEDTNNAutoencoderDense2025,
  title = {{{AE-DTNN}}: {{Autoencoder}}--{{Dense}}--{{Transformer Neural Network Model}} for {{Efficient Anomaly-Based Intrusion Detection Systems}}},
  shorttitle = {{{AE-DTNN}}},
  author = {Kamal, Hesham and Mashaly, Maggie},
  year = 2025,
  month = aug,
  journal = {Machine Learning and Knowledge Extraction},
  volume = {7},
  number = {3},
  pages = {78},
  issn = {2504-4990},
  doi = {10.3390/make7030078},
  urldate = {2026-03-04},
  abstract = {In this study, we introduce an enhanced hybrid Autoencoder--Dense--Transformer Neural Network (AE-DTNN) model for developing an effective intrusion detection system (IDS) aimed at improving the performance and robustness of threat detection strategies within a rapidly changing and increasingly complex network landscape. The Autoencoder component restructures network traffic data, while a stack of Dense layers performs feature extraction to generate more meaningful representations. The Transformer network then facilitates highly precise and comprehensive classification. Our strategy incorporates adaptive synthetic sampling (ADASYN) for both binary and multi-class classification tasks, complemented by the edited nearest neighbors (ENN) technique and the use of class weights to mitigate class imbalance issues. In experiments conducted on the NF-BoT-IoT-v2 dataset, the AE-DTNN-based IDS achieved outstanding performance, with 99.98\% accuracy in binary classification and 98.30\% in multi-class classification. On the NSL-KDD dataset, the model reached 98.57\% accuracy for binary classification and 97.50\% for multi-class classification. Additionally, the model attained 99.92\% and 99.78\% accuracy in binary and multi-class classification, respectively, on the CSE-CIC-IDS2018 dataset. These results demonstrate the exceptional effectiveness of the proposed model in contrast to conventional approaches, highlighting its strong potential to detect a broad range of network intrusions with high reliability.},
  langid = {english},
  note = {A rendelkez\'esre \'all\'o forr\'asok alapj\'an az al\'abbi v\'alaszokat adom a k\'erd\'eseire a Kamal \'es Mashaly (2025) \'altal jegyzett cikk kapcs\'an:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Alkalmas-e a megold\'as zero-day t\'amad\'asok detekt\'al\'as\'ara?} \textbf{Igen.} A cikk egy anom\'alia-alap\'u behatol\'asjelz\H o rendszert (AIDS) mutat be, amelyr\H ol kijelenti, hogy hat\'ekonyabb az \textbf{\'uj vagy ismeretlen (unfamiliar) t\'amad\'asok} detekt\'al\'as\'aban, mint a szignat\'ura-alap\'u rendszerek. A modell architekt\'ur\'aja lehet\H ov\'e teszi a "novel" (\'ujszer\H u) t\'amad\'ast\'ipusok azonos\'it\'as\'at a k\"ul\"onb\"oz\H o adatk\'eszleteken.
\item \textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'asi paradigm\'at haszn\'al, \'es hogyan kategoriz\'alhat\'o?} A modell \textbf{fel\"ugyelt tanul\'asi (supervised learning)} paradigm\'at haszn\'al. B\'ar tartalmaz egy Autoencoder komponenst (amelyet gyakran fel\"ugyelet n\'elk\"ul tan\'itanak), a teljes keretrendszer bin\'aris \'es t\"obboszt\'alyos oszt\'alyoz\'ast v\'egez c\'imk\'ezett adatk\'eszleteken.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk az imbalansz adatk\'eszletek kezel\'es\'evel? Ha igen, hogyan?} \textbf{Igen.} A cikk k\"ozponti t\'em\'aja az oszt\'aly-imbalansz kezel\'ese. Erre a c\'elra az \textbf{ADASYN} (Adaptive Synthetic Sampling) t\'ulmintav\'etelez\'esi technik\'at, az \textbf{ENN} (Edited Nearest Neighbors) alulmintav\'etelez\'esi m\'odszert \'es az \textbf{oszt\'alys\'ulyoz\'ast (class weights)} alkalmazz\'ak egy\"uttesen.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk a concept drift jelens\'eg\'evel? Ha igen, javasolnak-e r\'a megold\'ast?} A forr\'asok \textbf{nem eml\'itik explicit m\'odon a "concept drift" kifejez\'est}. Ugyanakkor a szerz\H ok hangs\'ulyozz\'ak a modell k\'epess\'eg\'et a "dinamikusan v\'altoz\'o" h\'al\'ozati k\"ornyezethez \'es a "fejl\H od\H o t\'amad\'asi mint\'akhoz" val\'o alkalmazkod\'asra.
\item \textbf{Fontos szempont-e az interpret\'alhat\'os\'ag? Ha igen, milyen technik\'akat haszn\'alnak (pl. SMOTE, LIME stb.)?} Az interpret\'alhat\'os\'ag \textbf{nem jelenik meg fontos szempontk\'ent} a javasolt modell le\'ir\'as\'aban. A LIME \'es SMOTE technik\'akat csak a kapcsol\'od\'o munk\'ak vagy kor\'abbi kutat\'asok \"osszefoglal\'as\'an\'al eml\'iti a sz\"oveg.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'eseket haszn\'alnak?} A cikk egy kifinomult el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi cs\H ovezet\'eket (pipeline) v\'azol fel:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Hi\'anyz\'o \'ert\'ekek} kezel\'ese (elt\'avol\'it\'as).
\item \textbf{Duplik\'alt rekordok} t\"orl\'ese.
\item \textbf{Outlier (kiugr\'o \'ert\'ek) detekt\'al\'as} hibrid Z-Score \'es LOF (Local Outlier Factor) m\'odszerrel.
\item \textbf{Jellemz\H o-kiv\'alaszt\'as} korrel\'aci\'o-alap\'u elemz\'essel.
\item \textbf{Normaliz\'al\'as} MinMaxScaler haszn\'alat\'aval.
\item \textbf{Dimenzi\'ocs\"okkent\'es} PCA (Principal Component Analysis) seg\'its\'eg\'evel.
\item \textbf{Oszt\'aly-kiegyenl\'it\'es} ADASYN \'es ENN technik\'akkal.

\end{enumerate}

\item 
\par
\textbf{Mely adatk\'eszleteket haszn\'alt\'ak vagy tesztelt\'ek a tanulm\'anyban?} A modellt h\'arom f\H o adatk\'eszleten \'ert\'ekelt\'ek:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{NF-BoT-IoT-v2}
\item \textbf{NSL-KDD}
\item \textbf{CSE-CIC-IDS2018}

\end{itemize}

\item \textbf{Alkalmaznak-e valamilyen speci\'alis feature engineering m\'odszert?} \textbf{Igen.} A modell \textbf{Autoencoder} komponense struktur\'alja \'at \'es jav\'itja a h\'al\'ozati forgalmi mint\'akat, m\'ig a \textbf{Dense r\'etegek} hierarchikus jellemz\H o-kinyer\'est (feature extraction) v\'egeznek, hogy jelent\'estelibb reprezent\'aci\'okat hozzanak l\'etre a Transformer sz\'am\'ara.
\item \textbf{Milyen t\'ipus\'u modellt mutatnak be?} Egy hibrid m\'ely tanul\'asi modellt, az \textbf{AE-DTNN}-t (Autoencoder--Dense--Transformer Neural Network). Az architekt\'ura egy \textbf{Autoencoderb\H ol}, egy \textbf{Dense r\'etegcsomagb\'ol} \'es egy \textbf{Transformer} h\'al\'ozatb\'ol \'all.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen loss functiont (vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt) haszn\'alnak?}
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item Az Autoencoder eset\'eben: \textbf{Mean Squared Error (MSE)}.
\item A bin\'aris oszt\'alyoz\'ashoz: \textbf{Binary cross-entropy}.
\item A t\"obboszt\'alyos oszt\'alyoz\'ashoz: \textbf{Categorical cross-entropy}.

\end{itemize}

  urldate = {2026-03-04},
  abstract = {Network backbone black holes(BH) pose significant challenges in the Internet by causing disruptions and data loss as routers silently drop packets without notification. These silent BH failures, stemming from issues like hardware malfunctions or misconfigurations, uniquely affect point-to-point packet flows without disrupting the entire network. Unlike cyber attacks and network intrusions, BHs are often untraceable, making early detection vital and challenging. This study addresses the need for an effective forecasting solution for BH occurrences, especially in environments with unlabeled traffic data where traditional anomaly detection methods fall short. The Type-Independent Black Hole Forecasting Model is introduced to predict BH occurrences with high precision across various anomalies, including contextual and collective anomaly types. The three-stage methodology processes unlabeled time-series network data, where the data is not pre-labeled as anomaly or normal, using machine learning and deep learning techniques to identify and forecast potential BH occurrences. The `Point BH Identification and Segregation' stage segregates point BH traffic using Density-Based Spatial Clustering of Applications with Noise(DBSCAN), followed by Reintegration and Time Series Smoothing. The final stage, Advanced Contextual and Collective BH Detection, leverages Convolutional AutoEncoder(Conv-AE) with window sliding for advanced anomaly detection. Evaluation using a dual-dataset approach, including real backbone network traffic and a time-series adapted public dataset, demonstrates the adaptability of the model to real backbone BH detection systems. Experimental results show superior performance compared to state-of-the-art unsupervised anomaly forecasting models, with a 98\% detection rate and 90\% F-1 score, outperforming models like MultiHeadSelfAttention, which is the main building block of Transformers.},

\item \textbf{What training algorithm is applied?} The methodology follows a \textbf{three-stage methodology}: first segregating point anomalies with DBSCAN, followed by reintegration/smoothing, and finally advanced detection using a Conv-AE or a WGAN-based attention architecture,. However, the attributes that make SDN attractive, its centralized management, also make it a prime target for a wide range of cyberattacks. While previous studies have addressed security threats in legacy networks, SDN introduces new vulnerabilities that require fresh insights and strategies. Various attacks can disrupt legitimate network services, posing a significant challenge in early detection and mitigation due to the inherent complexity of SDN traffic. To assess its impact on SDN controller performance, we implement the lightweight framework in a simulated SDN environment. Our results demonstrate that the proposed solution incurs minimal overhead, ensuring that network performance remains largely unaffected. This research contributes to the ongoing efforts to secure SDN architectures, providing a practical and efficient approach to detect and mitigate emerging threats in SDNs. It underscores the importance of proactive measures to safeguard the integrity and availability of SDN-based network services.},

An anomaly-based NIDS has attracted researchers to develop a system to detect malign traffic in a network using Machine Learning (ML) models. This work has achieved a Decision Tree model with 96.37\% accuracy and 96.33\% F1-score and the AdaBoost model with 96.37\% accuracy and 96.33\% F1-score for multiclass classification. The results show that a balanced CICIDS-2017 dataset improves the performance of decision trees and AdaBoost classifiers. @article{kimRelativeFrequencyRankEncoding2024,
  title = {Relative {{Frequency-Rank Encoding}} for {{Unsupervised Network Anomaly Detection}}},
  author = {Kim, Minsong and Jang, Woohyuk and Hur, JunNyung and Yoon, MyungKeun},
  year = 2024,
  journal = {IEEE/ACM Transactions on Networking},
  volume = {32},
  number = {4},
  pages = {3453--3467},
  issn = {1558-2566},
  doi = {10.1109/TNET.2024.3391396},
  urldate = {2025-06-13},
  abstract = {Network-based anomaly detection plays a pivotal role in cybersecurity. Most detection models are based on unsupervised machine learning to learn such a normal flow pattern of network traffic as the numbers of incoming/outgoing packets, traffic volumes in bytes, duration time, etc., most of which are numerical features. On the contrary, non-numerical features have not been fully utilized yet although they often give a decisive hint to the detection of unseen attacks; for example, rarely observed combinations of IP addresses and port numbers can reveal an uncommon attack attempt. This heuristic has already been used by human experts for decades, but not fully utilized yet by deep learning models. In this paper, we present a new encoding scheme for non-numerical features such as IP addresses and port numbers that might have been mistakenly considered as numerical features. The new encoding scheme first ranks non-numerical features in their frequency order and then evenly places each rank between 0 and 1, which transforms raw data into a form that is easy for machines to understand. The anomaly detection performance is significantly improved when this new encoding scheme is applied to the same deep learning model. For example, a simple autoencoder model with the new encoding scheme achieved the Area Under Receiver Operating Characteristic, AUROC, of 0.99 for the well-known CICIDS2017 dataset while the previous record was 0.91. Experimental results from three different open datasets show that the proposed encoding scheme can significantly enhance the performance of anomaly detection models.},
  keywords = {anomaly detection,Anomaly detection,Deep learning,encoding,Encoding,Feature extraction,intrusion detection,IP networks,network flow,Network security,Not checked,Numerical models,Security},
  annotation = {Access to Full Paper is not available},
  note = {Az al\'abbi v\'alaszok kiz\'ar\'olag a megadott absztrakt sz\"ovege alapj\'an k\'esz\"ultek. \begin{itemize}

@article{kingEulerDetectingNetwork2023,
  title = {{\textsc{Euler}} : {{Detecting Network Lateral Movement}} via {{Scalable Temporal Link Prediction}}},
  author = {King, Isaiah J. and Huang, H. Howie},
  year = 2023,
  month = aug,
  journal = {ACM Transactions on Privacy and Security},
  volume = {26},
  number = {3},
  pages = {1--36},
  issn = {2471-2566, 2471-2574},
  doi = {10.1145/3588771},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {Lateral movement is a key stage of system compromise used by advanced persistent threats. Detecting it is no simple task. When network host logs are abstracted into discrete temporal graphs, the problem can be reframed as anomalous edge detection in an evolving network. Research in modern deep graph learning techniques has produced many creative and complicated models for this task. However, as is the case in many machine learning fields, the generality of models is of paramount importance for accuracy and scalability during training and inference. In this article, we propose a formalized approach to this problem with a framework we call              Euler. It consists of a model-agnostic graph neural network stacked upon a model-agnostic sequence encoding layer such as a recurrent neural network. Models built according to the              Euler framework can easily distribute their graph convolutional layers across multiple machines for large performance improvements. Additionally, we demonstrate that Euler-based models are as good, or better, than every state-of-the-art approach to anomalous link detection and prediction that we tested. As anomaly-based intrusion detection systems, our models efficiently identified anomalous connections between entities with high precision and outperformed all other unsupervised techniques for anomalous lateral movement detection. Additionally, we show that as a piece of a larger anomaly detection pipeline, Euler models perform well enough for use in real-world systems. With more advanced, yet still lightweight, alerting mechanisms ingesting the embeddings produced by Euler  models, precision is boosted from 0.243, to 0.986 on real-world network traffic.},
  langid = {english},
  note = {A kutat\'asi cikk alapj\'an az al\'abbi v\'alaszokat adom a k\'erd\'eseidre:
\par

A modell egy alapvonalat (baseline) hoz l\'etre a norm\'al viselked\'esr\H ol kiz\'ar\'olag j\'oindulat\'u adatok alapj\'an, \'igy nincs sz\"uks\'ege c\'imk\'ezett adatokra a tan\'it\'asi f\'azisban,.

@article{kolukisaEfficientNetworkIntrusion2024,
  title = {An Efficient Network Intrusion Detection Approach Based on Logistic Regression Model and Parallel Artificial Bee Colony Algorithm},
  author = {Kolukisa, Burak and Dedeturk, Bilge Kagan and Hacilar, Hilal and Gungor, Vehbi Cagri},
  year = 2024,
  month = apr,
  journal = {Computer Standards \& Interfaces},
  volume = {89},
  pages = {103808},
  issn = {09205489},
  doi = {10.1016/j.csi.2023.103808},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {In recent years, the widespread use of the Internet has created many issues, especially in the area of cybersecurity. It is critical to detect intrusions in network traffic, and researchers have developed network intrusion and anomaly detection systems to cope with high numbers of attacks and attack variations. In particular, machine learning and meta-heuristic methods have been widely used for network intrusion detection systems (NIDS). However, existing studies on these systems usually suffer from low performance results such as accuracy, F1-measure, false positive rate, and false negative rate, and generally do not use automatic parameter tuning techniques. To address these challenges, this study proposes a novel approach based on a logistic regression model trained using a parallel artificial bee colony (LR-ABC) algorithm with a hyper-parameter optimization technique. The performance of the proposed model is evaluated against state-of-the-art machine learning and deep learning models on two publicly available NIDS datasets. Comparative performance evaluations show that the proposed method achieved satisfactory results with accuracy of 88.25\% on the UNSW-NB15 dataset and 90.11\% on the NSL-KDD dataset, and F1-measures of 88.26\% and 90.15\%, respectively. These findings demonstrate the efficacy of the proposed LR-ABC model in enhancing the accuracy and reliability, while providing a scalable solution to adapt to the dynamic and evolving landscape of cybersecurity threats.},
  langid = {english},
  note = {A megadott forr\'asok alapj\'an az al\'abbi v\'alaszokat adom a k\'erd\'eseire:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Alkalmas-e a megold\'as zero-day t\'amad\'asok detekt\'al\'as\'ara?} \textbf{Igen.} A cikk szerint az \textbf{anom\'alia-alap\'u detekt\'al\'as} k\'epes azonos\'itani az \textbf{\'uj \'es ismeretlen (novel/unfamiliar) t\'amad\'asi t\'ipusokat}, mivel nem a kor\'abbi szignat\'ur\'akra, hanem a tanult norm\'al viselked\'esi mint\'akt\'ol val\'o elt\'er\'esre t\'amaszkodik,. A javasolt modell egy sk\'al\'azhat\'o megold\'ast k\'in\'al a dinamikusan fejl\H od\H o kiberbiztons\'agi fenyeget\'esekhez val\'o alkalmazkod\'ashoz,.
\item \textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'asi paradigm\'at haszn\'al, \'es hogyan kategoriz\'alhat\'o?} A modell \textbf{fel\"ugyelt tanul\'ast (supervised learning)} haszn\'al. B\'ar az optimaliz\'al\'ashoz haszn\'alt ABC algoritmus term\'eszet ihlette \'es kevesebb el\H ozetes tud\'ast ig\'enyel a c\'imk\'ekr\H ol, a cikk egy\'ertelm\H uen le\'irja, hogy a logisztikus regresszi\'os modellt \textbf{c\'imk\'ezett tan\'it\'o adatk\'eszleteken} (\$y \textbackslash in \textbraceleft 0, 1\textbraceright\$) tr\'eningezik a bin\'aris oszt\'alyoz\'asi feladathoz,.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk az imbalansz adatk\'eszletek kezel\'es\'evel? Ha igen, hogyan?} A forr\'asok eml\'itik, hogy a h\'al\'ozati forgalom term\'eszet\'eb\H ol ad\'od\'o \textbf{oszt\'aly-imbalansz (class imbalance)} kih\'iv\'ast jelent, \'es megjegyzik, hogy a m\'odszer egy kor\'abbi v\'altozata hat\'ekonynak bizonyult kiegyens\'ulyozatlan adatokon,. Ugyanakkor ebben a konkr\'et tanulm\'anyban \textbf{nem alkalmaztak speci\'alis adatmanipul\'aci\'os technik\'at} (p\'eld\'aul SMOTE-ot), hanem a modell robusztuss\'ag\'ara \'es a hiper-param\'eterek SMAC \'altali optimaliz\'al\'as\'ara t\'amaszkodtak,.

Ugyanakkor hangs\'ulyozza, hogy a modell k\'epes alkalmazkodni a h\'al\'ozati forgalom \'es a t\'amad\'asi taktik\'ak \textbf{dinamikus term\'eszet\'ehez}, \'es a gyors (p\'arhuzamos\'itott) tan\'it\'as lehet\H ov\'e teszi a modell gyakori friss\'it\'es\'et az \'uj fenyeget\'esek ellen,.
\item \textbf{Fontos szempont-e az interpret\'alhat\'os\'ag? Ha igen, milyen technik\'akat haszn\'alnak?} Az interpret\'alhat\'os\'ag \textbf{nem k\"ozponti t\'em\'aja} a cikknek. Megeml\'itik, hogy a g\'epi tanul\'asi eredm\'enyek \'es azok \'ertelmez\'ese k\"oz\"otti "szemantikai szakad\'ek" neh\'ezs\'eget jelent, de \textbf{nem alkalmaznak interpret\'aci\'os technik\'akat} (mint a LIME vagy SHAP). Megjegyzik viszont, hogy a logisztikus regresszi\'o \"onmag\'aban az egyszer\H us\'eg\'er\H ol \'es hat\'ekonys\'ag\'ar\'ol ismert.

\par
\textbf{Milyen adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'eseket haszn\'alnak?} A cikk az al\'abbi l\'ep\'eseket sorolja fel:

\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{One-Hot k\'odol\'as (One-hot encoding)}: A kateg\'oriat\'ipus\'u jellemz\H ok (pl. service, state, proto) numerikus bin\'aris vektorokk\'a alak\'it\'asa.
\item \textbf{Adat-normaliz\'al\'as (Data normalization)}: \textbf{Max-abs normaliz\'al\'ast} alkalmaztak, amely a jellemz\H oket a 0 \'es 1 k\"oz\"otti tartom\'anyba sk\'al\'azza,. A szerz\H ok kiemelt\'ek, hogy tiszt\'it\'asi folyamatot (cleaning) nem v\'egeztek az adatokon.

\item \textbf{Milyen loss functiont (vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt) haszn\'alnak?} A modell \textbf{n\'egyzetes hiba (Mean Squared Error - MSE)} f\"uggv\'enyt haszn\'al, amelyet \textbf{L2 regulariz\'aci\'oval (Ridge regression)} eg\'esz\'itettek ki a t\'ultanul\'as elker\"ul\'ese \'erdek\'eben,.
\item \textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'o algoritmust alkalmaznak?} Egy speci\'alisan fejlesztett \textbf{P\'arhuzamos Mesters\'eges M\'ehkol\'onia (Parallel Artificial Bee Colony)} algoritmust, amelyet CPU-n \'es GPU-n (CuPy k\"onyvt\'arral) egyar\'ant implement\'altak a tan\'it\'asi id\H o cs\"okkent\'ese \'erdek\'eben,,.

\end{itemize}

@inproceedings{lanvinUnderstandingAlertsRaised2023,
  title = {Towards {{Understanding Alerts}} Raised by {{Unsupervised Network Intrusion Detection Systems}}},
  booktitle = {Proceedings of the 26th {{International Symposium}} on {{Research}} in {{Attacks}}, {{Intrusions}} and {{Defenses}}},
  author = {Lanvin, Maxime and Gimenez, Pierre-Fran{\c c}ois and Han, Yufei and Majorczyk, Fr{\'e}d{\'e}ric and M{\'e}, Ludovic and Totel, Eric},
  year = 2023,
  month = oct,
  pages = {135--150},
  publisher = {ACM},
  address = {Hong Kong China},
  doi = {10.1145/3607199.3607247},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {The use of Machine Learning for anomaly detection in cyber security-critical applications, such as intrusion detection systems, has been hindered by the lack of explainability. Without understanding the reason behind anomaly alerts, it is too expensive or impossible for human analysts to verify and identify cyber-attacks. Our research addresses this challenge and focuses on unsupervised network intrusion detection, where only benign network traffic is available for training the detection model. We propose a novel post-hoc explanation method, called AE-pvalues, which is based on the p-values of the reconstruction errors produced by an Auto-Encoder-based anomaly detection method. Our work identifies the most informative network traffic features associated with an anomaly alert, providing interpretations for the generated alerts. We conduct an empirical study using a large-scale network intrusion dataset, CICIDS2017, to compare the proposed AE-pvalues method with two state-of-the-art baselines applied in the unsupervised anomaly detection task. Our experimental results show that the AE-pvalues method accurately identifies abnormal influential network traffic features. Furthermore, our study demonstrates that the explanation outputs can help identify different types of network attacks in the detected anomalies, enabling human security analysts to understand the root cause of the anomalies and take prompt action to strengthen security measures.},
  isbn = {979-8-4007-0765-0},
  langid = {english},
  note = {A megadott forr\'as (\textbf{Lanvin \'es mtsai., 2023 -- Towards Understanding Alerts raised by Unsupervised Network Intrusion Detection Systems}) alapj\'an a v\'alaszok a k\"ovetkez\H ok:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Alkalmas-e a megold\'as zero-day t\'amad\'asok \'eszlel\'es\'ere?} \textbf{Igen.} A forr\'asok szerint a g\'epi tanul\'assal seg\'itett h\'al\'ozatbiztons\'agi rendszerek rugalmas detekt\'al\'ast tesznek lehet\H ov\'e, amely seg\'iti a SOC csapatokat az \textbf{\'uj, felbukkan\'o biztons\'agi incidensekre} (emerging new security incidents) val\'o gyors reag\'al\'asban. Mivel a m\'odszer fel\"ugyelet n\'elk\"uli (unsupervised) anom\'aliadetekt\'al\'ast alkalmaz, amely a norm\'al profilt\'ol jelent\H osen elt\'er\H o forgalmat azonos\'itja, alkalmas ismeretlen t\'amad\'asok felismer\'es\'ere is.
\item \textbf{Milyen tanul\'asi paradigm\'at haszn\'al?} A cikk \textbf{fel\"ugyelet n\'elk\"uli (unsupervised)} h\'al\'ozati behatol\'as\'eszlel\'est alkalmaz. Specifikusan az \textbf{egyoszt\'alyos tanul\'as (one-class learning)} megk\"ozel\'it\'est k\"oveti, ahol a modellt kiz\'ar\'olag benignus (tiszta) forgalmi adatokon tan\'itj\'ak, hogy megtanulj\'ak a norm\'al tev\'ekenys\'egek profilj\'at.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk az kiegyens\'ulyozatlan adatk\'eszletek kezel\'es\'evel?} A forr\'as elismeri, hogy a vizsg\'alt CICIDS2017 adatk\'eszletben a t\'amad\'asi t\'ipusok eloszl\'asa \textbf{er\H osen kiegyens\'ulyozatlan} (p\'eld\'aul bizonyos t\'amad\'asokb\'ol csak 1-13 darab szerepel a t\"obb mint 1,6 milli\'o benignus rekord mellett). Ugyanakkor a javasolt paradigma jelleg\'eb\H ol ad\'od\'oan \textbf{nem alkalmaz specifikus kiegyens\'ulyoz\'asi technik\'at} (p\'eld\'aul t\'ulmintav\'etelez\'est), mivel a modell tan\'it\'as\'ahoz eleve csak a t\"obbs\'egi, benignus oszt\'aly adatait haszn\'alj\'ak fel.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk a concept drift jelens\'eggel?} A megadott forr\'asdokumentum \textbf{nem eml\'iti} a concept drift jelens\'eget vagy annak kezel\'es\'et.
\item \textbf{Fontos szempont-e az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg (interpretability)?} \textbf{Igen, ez a tanulm\'any k\"ozponti k\'erd\'ese.} A forr\'as szerint az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg hi\'anya akad\'alyozza az anom\'aliadetekt\'al\'o rendszerek elterjed\'es\'et, mert magyar\'azat n\'elk\"ul a hum\'an elemz\H ok sz\'am\'ara t\'ul dr\'aga vagy lehetetlen a riaszt\'asok ellen\H orz\'ese. A szerz\H ok c\'elja \'eppen a ,,fekete doboz'' jelleg\H u modellek \'atl\'athat\'os\'ag\'anak jav\'it\'asa.
\item \textbf{Milyen adat-el\H ofeldolgoz\'asi l\'ep\'eseket haszn\'alnak?} Az el\H ofeldolgoz\'as sor\'an a nyers h\'al\'ozati adatokat (.pcap) \textbf{Zeek} seg\'its\'eg\'evel elemzik logok kinyer\'es\'ehez, majd ezekb\H ol egy \textbf{biztons\'agi objektum gr\'afot} (security objects graph) \'ep\'itenek. A kateg\'oriav\'altoz\'okat \textbf{one-hot k\'odol\'assal} vektoriz\'alj\'ak. A folytonos attrib\'utumokat (p\'eld\'aul forgalom id\H otartama) \textbf{Gauss-kever\'ekmodellel (GMM)} klaszterezik, \'es a kapott csoportc\'imk\'eket haszn\'alj\'ak kateg\'oriak\'ent a one-hot \'abr\'azol\'asban.
\item \textbf{Mely adatk\'eszleteket haszn\'alt\'ak vagy tesztelt\'ek?} A k\'is\'erleteket \'es az \'ert\'ekel\'est a \textbf{CICIDS2017} h\'al\'ozati behatol\'as\'eszlel\'esi adatk\'eszleten v\'egezt\'ek el.
\item \textbf{Alkalmaztak-e speci\'alis jellemz\H o-kinyer\'esi (feature engineering) m\'odszert?} Igen. A \textbf{Sec2graph} elj\'ar\'ast alkalmazz\'ak, amely a h\'al\'ozati logokat egy heterog\'en gr\'af strukt\'ur\'aban \'abr\'azolja, hogy felt\'arja az adatok k\"oz\"otti struktur\'alis kapcsolatokat (p\'eld\'aul kliensek, protokollok \'es szerverek \"osszek\"ottet\'eseit). Az \'eleket ezut\'an ritka (sparse) vektorokk\'a k\'odolj\'ak.
\item \textbf{Milyen t\'ipus\'u modellt mutat be a cikk?} A cikk egy \textbf{Auto-Encoder (AE)} alap\'u m\'ely neur\'alis h\'al\'ozati modellt alkalmaz az anom\'ali\'ak \'eszlel\'es\'ere, amelyhez egy \'uj, p-\'ert\'ekeken alapul\'o ut\'olagos magyar\'az\'o m\'odszert, az \textbf{AE-pvalues}-t fejleszti ki.
\item \textbf{Milyen vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt (loss function) haszn\'alnak?} Az anom\'aliadetekt\'al\'o modell a bemenet \'es a kimenet k\"oz\"otti rekonstrukci\'os hiba sz\'am\'it\'as\'ahoz \textbf{bin\'aris keresztentr\'opi\'at (binary cross-entropy - BCE)} alkalmaz.
\item \textbf{Milyen tanul\'asi algoritmust alkalmaznak?} A modell egy \textbf{rekonstrukci\'o-alap\'u tanul\'asi algoritmust} haszn\'al fel\"ugyelet n\'elk\"uli m\'odon (unsupervised training), ahol a c\'el a norm\'al forgalmi mint\'ak h\H u vissza\'all\'it\'asa. A magyar\'azatok gener\'al\'as\'ahoz egy olyan algoritmust haszn\'alnak, amely a dimenzi\'onk\'enti rekonstrukci\'os hiba empirikus eloszl\'as\'ab\'ol sz\'am\'it \textbf{p-\'ert\'ekeket} az anom\'ali\'at okoz\'o jellemz\H ok rangsorol\'as\'ahoz.

\end{itemize}

A v\'alaszad\'ashoz a megadott cikk r\'eszletei \'alltak rendelkez\'esre.},

@article{liEnhancingNetworkIntrusion2024,
  title = {Enhancing {{Network Intrusion Detection Through}} the {{Application}} of the {{Dung Beetle Optimized Fusion Model}}},

  abstract = {With the rapid development of information communication and mobile device technologies, smart devices have become increasingly popular, providing convenience to households and enhancing the level of intelligence in daily life. This trend is also driving innovation and progress in various fields, including healthcare, transportation, and industry. However, as technology continues to proliferate, network security concerns have become increasingly prominent, making the protection of digital life and data security an urgent priority. Intrusion detection has always played an important role in the field of network security. Traditional intrusion detection systems predominantly rely on anomaly detection technology to identify potential intrusions by detecting abnormal patterns in network traffic. With technological advancements, machine learning-based methods have emerged as the cornerstone of modern intrusion detection, enabling more precise identification of abnormal behaviors and potential intrusions by learning the patterns of normal network traffic. In response to these challenges, this paper introduces an innovative intrusion detection model that amalgamates the Attention-CNN-BiLSTM (ACBL) and Temporal Convolutional Network (TCN) architectures. The ACBL and TCN models excel in processing spatial and temporal features within network traffic data, respectively. This integration harnesses diverse neural network structures to elevate overall model performance and accuracy. Furthermore, a unique approach inspired by dung beetles' natural behavior, incorporating Tent mapping-enhanced Dung Beetle Optimization Algorithm (TDBO), is leveraged for both optimizing feature selection parameters and searching for optimal model hyperparameters. The feature selection parameters obtained from TDBO are then combined with the importance ranking from the Random Forest algorithm, ensuring optimal features can be better selected to enhance model performance. This paper introduces a novel intrusion detection model, the TDBO-ACBLT model, and validates its performance using the UNSW-NW15 dataset. TDBO excels in feature selection compared to common algorithms and achieves superior parameter optimization accuracy over Harris's Hawk Optimization (HHO), Particle Swarm Optimization (PSO), and Dung Beetle Optimization (DBO). The proposed model achieves higher accuracy than prevalent machine learning models.},

@article{linIntrusionDetectionMethod2023,
  title = {An Intrusion Detection Method Based on Granular Autoencoders},
  author = {Lin, Sihong and Zhang, Kunbin and Guan, Dun and He, Linjie and Chen, Yumin},
  year = 2023,
  month = may,
  journal = {Journal of Intelligent \& Fuzzy Systems},
  volume = {44},
  number = {5},
  pages = {8413--8424},
  issn = {10641246, 18758967},
  doi = {10.3233/JIFS-223649},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {Intrusion detection systems have become one of the important tools for network security due to the frequent attacks brought about by the explosive growth of network traffic. Autoencoder is an unsupervised learning model with a neural network structure. It has a powerful feature learning capability and is effective in intrusion detection. However, its network construction suffers from overfitting and gradient disappearance problems. Traditional granular computing methods have advantages in solving such problems, but the process is relatively complex, the granularity dimension is high, and the computational cost is large, which is not suitable for application in intrusion detection systems. To address these problems, we propose a novel autoencoder: Granular AutoEncoders (GAE). The granulation reference set is constructed by random sampling. The granulation of training samples is based on single-feature similarity in a reference set to form granules. The granulation of multiple features results in granular vectors. Some operations of granules are defined. Furthermore, we propose some granular measures, including granular norms and granular loss functions. The GAE is further applied to the field of intrusion detection by designing an anomaly detection algorithm based on the GAE. The algorithm determines whether the network flows are anomalous by comparing the difference between an input granular vector and its output granular vector that is reconstructed by the GAE. Finally, some experiments are conducted using an intrusion detection dataset, comparing multiple metrics in terms of precision, recall, and F1-Score. The experimental results validate the correctness and effectiveness of the intrusion detection method based on GAE. And contrast experiments show that the proposed method has stronger ability for detecting anomalies than the correlation algorithms.},
  note = {A megadott forr\'asok alapj\'an az al\'abbi v\'alaszokat adom a k\'erd\'eseire:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Alkalmas-e a megold\'as zero-day t\'amad\'asok detekt\'al\'as\'ara?} B\'ar a "zero-day" kifejez\'es nem szerepel explicit m\'odon a sz\"ovegben, a forr\'asok szerint a modell \textbf{anom\'alia-detekt\'al\'ason} alapul, amely a h\'al\'ozati forgalmat a norm\'al rendszer-viselked\'essel hasonl\'itja \"ossze. A cikk kijelenti, hogy a m\'odszert \'ugy tervezt\'ek, hogy jobban alkalmazkodjon az \textbf{intr\'uzi\'os t\'amad\'asi mint\'ak dinamikus v\'altoz\'asaihoz}. Mivel a modell a norm\'al adatokt\'ol val\'o elt\'er\'est (rekonstrukci\'os hiba) figyeli, elviekben alkalmas az ismeretlen vagy \'uj t\'ipus\'u (zero-day) t\'amad\'asok felismer\'es\'ere.
\item \textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'asi paradigm\'at haszn\'al, \'es hogyan kategoriz\'alhat\'o?} A modell \textbf{fel\"ugyelet n\'elk\"uli (unsupervised)} tanul\'asi paradigm\'at alkalmaz.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk az imbalansz adatk\'eszletek kezel\'es\'evel? Ha igen, hogyan?} Igen, a cikk eml\'iti az adatk\'eszletbeli mint\'ak egyenl\H otlens\'eg\'et (sample imbalance). Ezt nem technikai adatmanipul\'aci\'oval (mint a SMOTE), hanem a \textbf{ki\'ert\'ekel\'esi metrik\'ak megv\'alaszt\'as\'aval} kezeli: a pontoss\'ag (Accuracy) helyett a \textbf{Precision (precizit\'as), Recall (visszah\'iv\'as) \'es F1-Score} mutat\'okat haszn\'alj\'ak a modell teljes\'itm\'eny\'enek m\'er\'es\'ere, mivel ezek jobban t\"ukr\"ozik a hat\'ekonys\'agot kiegyens\'ulyozatlan adatok eset\'en.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk a concept drift jelens\'eg\'evel? Ha igen, javasolnak-e r\'a megold\'ast?} A forr\'asok \textbf{nem eml\'itik} n\'ev szerint a "concept drift" kifejez\'est, \'es nem javasolnak r\'a specifikus megold\'ast a modell dinamikus v\'altoz\'asokhoz val\'o alkalmazkod\'ok\'epess\'eg\'enek eml\'it\'es\'en t\'ul.
\item \textbf{Fontos szempont-e az interpret\'alhat\'os\'ag? Ha igen, milyen technik\'akat haszn\'alnak?} A forr\'asok \textbf{nem t\'argyalj\'ak} az interpret\'alhat\'os\'agot mint szempontot, \'es nem eml\'itenek olyan technik\'akat, mint a LIME vagy a SHAP.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'eseket haszn\'alnak?} Az al\'abbi l\'ep\'eseket alkalmazz\'ak:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{De-szingulariz\'aci\'o} (De-singularization).
\item \textbf{Normaliz\'al\'as}: Maximum-minimum normaliz\'al\'ast haszn\'alnak az adatok 0 \'es 1 k\"oz\"otti tartom\'anyba szor\'it\'as\'ara.
\item \textbf{Granul\'al\'as (Granulating)}: A h\'al\'ozati forgalmi adatokat granul\'atumokk\'a \'es granul\'aris vektorokk\'a alak\'itj\'ak.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Mely adatk\'eszleteket haszn\'alt\'ak vagy tesztelt\'ek a tanulm\'anyban?} A k\'is\'erletekhez a \textbf{KDDCUP99 10\%} adatk\'eszletet haszn\'alt\'ak.
\item \textbf{Alkalmaznak-e valamilyen speci\'alis feature engineering m\'odszert?} Igen, a \textbf{granul\'aris sz\'am\'it\'ast (granular computing)} alkalmazz\'ak: v\'eletlenszer\H u mintav\'etelez\'essel egy "referencia halmazt" hoznak l\'etre, majd az adatok hasonl\'os\'ag\'at vizsg\'alj\'ak ezen referencia-mint\'akhoz k\'epest, \'igy hozva l\'etre a granul\'aris vektorokat.
\item \textbf{Milyen t\'ipus\'u modellt mutatnak be?} A modell egy \textbf{m\'ely tanul\'as\'u (deep learning)} strukt\'ura, pontosabban egy \textbf{Granul\'aris Autoencoder (GAE)}. Ez egy h\'aromr\'eteg\H u neur\'alis h\'al\'ozat (input, rejtett \'es kimeneti r\'eteg), amely granul\'aris neuronokb\'ol \'ep\"ul fel.
\item \textbf{Milyen loss functiont (vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt) haszn\'alnak?} A modell \textbf{n\'egyzetes vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt (squared loss function)} haszn\'al, amelyet granul\'aris form\'aban (granular loss function) alkalmaznak.
\item \textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'o algoritmust alkalmaznak?} A tan\'it\'as sor\'an \textbf{visszaterjeszt\'eses (backpropagation)} algoritmust \'es \textbf{gradiens s\"ullyed\'est (gradient descent)} alkalmaznak a s\'ulyparam\'eterek friss\'it\'es\'ere.

\end{itemize}

@article{liuEfficientTransformerDynamicAnomaly2025,
  title = {{{EfficientTransformer}}: {{A Dynamic Anomaly Detection Model}} for {{Industrial Control Networks}}},
  shorttitle = {{{EfficientTransformer}}},
  author = {Liu, Jinyang and Wang, Guogang and Zong, Xuejun and Ning, Bowei and He, Kan},
  year = 2025,
  journal = {IEEE Access},
  volume = {13},
  pages = {37931--37945},
  issn = {2169-3536},
  doi = {10.1109/ACCESS.2025.3545659},
  urldate = {2026-03-04},
  abstract = {As industrial control network threats become increasingly complex, traditional intrusion detection systems (IDS) struggle to capture implicit relationships due to feature redundancy and intricate feature interactions. This leads to increased computational complexity and higher detection latency, making it difficult for industrial control systems(ICS) to meet the fast and accurate response requirements for security events. In this study, we propose a dynamic anomaly detection model for industrial control networks, named EfficientTransformer. The model uses a 1D Convolutional Neural Network (1D-CNN) to extract local features from the data, while a linear multi-head self-attention mechanism, replacing the traditional Transformer's multi-head attention mechanism, provides global learning capabilities. This reduces computational complexity and enables efficient parallel learning. Additionally, to address the issue of class imbalance, the model incorporates a weighted cross-entropy loss function that assigns higher weights to the minority class of abnormal traffic, thereby improving the model's anomaly detection ability. This innovation further mitigates issues of feature redundancy and complex feature interactions, enhancing the model's dynamic processing capability and accuracy. The method was validated on the Oil and Gas Gathering and Transportation Full-Process Industrial Platform Attack-Defense Field, and the Catalytic Reforming Unit Process Platform at the Key Laboratory of Information Security for the Petrochemical Industry in Liaoning Province. Experimental results show that the proposed EfficientTransformer improves accuracy by 1.01\% and 2.26\% compared to the standard Transformer on the two datasets and significantly reduces testing time, demonstrating its applicability in the field of industrial information security.},
  copyright = {https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/legalcode},

@article{luMaskbasedSelfsupervisedNetwork2025,
  title = {Mask-Based {{Self-supervised Network Intrusion Detection System}}},
  author = {Lu, Xiaoya and Liu, Yifan and Feng, Fan and Liu, Yi and Liu, Zhenpeng},
  year = 2025,
  month = sep,
  journal = {Applied Soft Computing},
  volume = {181},
  pages = {113498},
  issn = {15684946},
  doi = {10.1016/j.asoc.2025.113498},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {A significant number of network intrusion detection systems utilize unsupervised anomaly detection methodologies, the majority of which fail to account for the potential for contamination in the data, resulting in suboptimal detection outcomes.This paper proposes an unsupervised method, designated MS-IDS (Mask-based Self-supervised Network Intrusion Detection System), which employs the techniques of mask shielding and Stacked Sparse Autoencoder (SSAE).MS-IDS is trained on data that has been contaminated in some way, generating a variety of masks through the process of learning. These masked inputs are subsequently reconstructed by SSAE. A composite loss function is devised, encompassing losses from both the mask unit and the SSAE. During the training phase, the combined loss function is optimized with the objective of identifying the optimal parameters and transformations for the SSAE. In the testing phase, the loss function assigns a score to each sample, which is used to classify outliers based on their scores. The performance of MS-IDS was evaluated across four intrusion datasets: The datasets used for evaluation were NSL-KDD, CIC-IDS2017, ToN-IoT, and CIC-DDOS2019. The results demonstrate that even when varying levels of contamination are introduced into the benign traffic, MS-IDS maintains robust performance with minimal decline. Notably, MS-IDS outperforms other models in terms of accuracy, AUC-ROC, and F1 scores, and its ability to detect attacks in contaminated data undergoes significant enhancement.},
  langid = {english},
  note = {Based on the provided sources, here are the answers to your questions regarding the article \textbf{"Mask-based Self-supervised Network Intrusion Detection System" (MS-IDS)}:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Is the solution suitable for detecting zero-day attacks?} \textbf{Yes.} The article states that traditional systems often fail against unknown threats, whereas \textbf{unsupervised/self-supervised} techniques like MS-IDS do not rely on pre-defined attack features. Experimental results in the study specifically demonstrate that the model can effectively detect \textbf{unknown attack types} (such as PROBE attacks excluded from the training set) by identifying them as reconstruction anomalies.
\item \textbf{What training paradigm does it use, and how can it be categorized?} The model uses a \textbf{self-supervised learning (SSL)} paradigm, which is categorized as a type of \textbf{unsupervised learning}. It utilizes the intrinsic structure of the data to generate its own labels/supervision signals through a masking mechanism.
\item \textbf{Does the article address the handling of imbalanced datasets?} The article mentions that the \textbf{NSL-KDD} dataset was selected because it addresses inherent \textbf{data imbalance} issues found in its predecessor, KDD 99. However, the primary focus of the authors' methodology is addressing \textbf{data contamination} (unlabeled attack data mixed into the benign training set) rather than class imbalance specifically.
\item \textbf{Does the article deal with the concept drift phenomenon?} \textbf{Yes.} The authors acknowledge "input distribution drift" and \textbf{"abrupt drift"} caused by new attack patterns. They propose that while the model shows robustness to these drifts, potential future solutions include \textbf{fine-tuning the mask structures, periodic retraining, or incorporating online concept drift detection mechanisms}.
\item \textbf{Is interpretability considered an important aspect?} \textbf{Yes.} The article highlights transparency and credibility as important. To achieve this, the authors use \textbf{SHAP (Explainable AI)} to provide both local explanations (for specific attack instances) and global explanations (feature importance across the dataset).
\item \textbf{What data preprocessing steps are used?} The article explicitly mentions that contaminated data undergoes preprocessing to obtain \textbf{encoded and standardized data}.
\item \textbf{Which datasets were used or tested in the study?} The study evaluated MS-IDS across four public datasets: \textbf{NSL-KDD, ToN-IoT, CICIDS2017} (specifically the Wednesday subset), and \textbf{CIC-DDOS2019}.
\item \textbf{Is there any special feature engineering method applied?} The model employs a \textbf{learnable masking strategy}. A mask generator (consisting of a feature extractor and a sigmoid function) creates multiple mask matrices that selectively suppress or obscure certain features to help the model learn deeper correlations between features.
\item \textbf{What type of model is presented?} It is a \textbf{deep learning} model based on a \textbf{Stacked Sparse Autoencoder (SSAE)} architecture combined with a \textbf{learnable mask generator}.
\item 
\par
\textbf{What loss function is used?} The authors designed a \textbf{joint loss function} consisting of:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Mask Loss (\$L\_\textbraceleft mask\textbraceright\$):} Derived from the similarity/inner product of different mask matrices to distinguish normal and anomalous correlations.
\item \textbf{SSAE Loss (\$L\_\textbraceleft ssae\textbraceright\$):} Combines \textbf{reconstruction loss} (\$L\_\textbraceleft rec\textbraceright\$) with a \textbf{sparsity constraint} using \textbf{Kullback--Leibler (KL) divergence}.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{What training algorithm is applied?} The model is trained end-to-end using the \textbf{Adam} gradient optimizer.
\item \textbf{Full article accessibility:} The answers provided above are based on the \textbf{full content provided in the sources}, which include the abstract, introduction, detailed design, experimental results, and conclusions.

\end{itemize}},

@article{lunardiARCADEAdversariallyRegularized2023,
  title = {{{ARCADE}}: {{Adversarially Regularized Convolutional Autoencoder}} for {{Network Anomaly Detection}}},
  shorttitle = {{{ARCADE}}},
  author = {Lunardi, Willian Tessaro and Lopez, Martin Andreoni and Giacalone, Jean-Pierre},
  year = 2023,
  month = jun,
  journal = {IEEE Transactions on Network and Service Management},
  volume = {20},
  number = {2},
  pages = {1305--1318},
  issn = {1932-4537},
  doi = {10.1109/TNSM.2022.3229706},
  urldate = {2025-06-14},
  abstract = {As the number of heterogenous IP-connected devices and traffic volume increase, so does the potential for security breaches. The undetected exploitation of these breaches can bring severe cybersecurity and privacy risks. Anomaly-based Intrusion Detection Systems (IDSs) play an essential role in network security. In this paper, we present a practical unsupervised anomaly-based deep learning detection system called ARCADE (Adversarially Regularized Convolutional Autoencoder for unsupervised network anomaly DEtection). With a convolutional Autoencoder (AE), ARCADE automatically builds a profile of the normal traffic using a subset of raw bytes of a few initial packets of network flows so that potential network anomalies and intrusions can be efficiently detected before they cause more damage to the network. ARCADE is trained exclusively on normal traffic. An adversarial training strategy is proposed to regularize and decrease the AE's capabilities to reconstruct network flows that are out-of-the-normal distribution, thereby improving its anomaly detection capabilities. The proposed approach is more effective than state-of-the-art deep learning approaches for network anomaly detection. Even when examining only two initial packets of a network flow, ARCADE can effectively detect malware infection and network attacks. ARCADE presents 20 times fewer parameters than baselines, achieving significantly faster detection speed and reaction time.},

@article{maksimovicUsingTripleModular2024,
  title = {Using {{Triple Modular Redundancy}} for {{Threshold Determination}} in {{DDOS Intrusion Detection Systems}}},
  author = {Maksimovi{\'c}, Aleksa N. and Nikoli{\'c}, Vojkan R. and Vidojevi{\'c}, Dejan V. and Randjelovi{\'c}, Milan D. and Djukanovi{\'c}, Slavi{\v s}a M. and Randjelovi{\'c}, Dragan M.},

  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {This paper describes an Intrusion Detection System (IDS) which uses several existing known IDS algorithms and Triple Modular Redundancy (TMR) algorithm to make decision about eventual existing attack by majority voting in one constructed ensemble model which solves practically the problem of binary classification. Proposed novel model belongs to so called stacking ensemble methods of machine learning algorithms which uses exactly four algorithms from the group of best binary classification algorithms: Decision trees, Naive Bayes, Support Vector Machine, k-nearest neighbors, logistic regression and AdaBoost and is applicable for any similar problem. Using proposed method, we get a more precisely determined threshold than it is case using whatever of in method individual applied algorithm as well as those algorithms that are the state of the art in the field of binary classification. Besides that, one of the main disadvantages of classic TMR used for classification network traffic, which is the problem of bad over-voting was successfully avoided by improving classic TMR with a new algorithm proposed by the authors. Today a denial of service attacks (DoS) and distributed denial of service (DDoS) are one of most present type on Internet and that is why the authors in this paper paid special attention to them and because of that the authors of proposed method chose to use known KAGGLE dataset which contains data of these type of attacks for the examination of quality of proposed IDS method implemented in suitable software. The dataset itself consists of a wide range of simulated intrusions in a military network environment in United States. Obtained results showed that IDS software with implemented proposed method worked precise and timely, which means alarms was trigger properly and efficiently with better results of quality of classification in most important measures than the individual included algorithms who are the state of the art in binary classification.},

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Mely adatk\'eszleteket haszn\'alt\'ak vagy tesztelt\'ek?} A kutat\'ashoz a \textbf{KAGGLE platformr\'ol} sz\'armaz\'o "Network Intrusion Detection" adatk\'eszletet haszn\'alt\'ak, amely egy katonai h\'al\'ozati k\"ornyezet (US Air Force LAN) szimul\'alt bet\"or\'eseit tartalmazza.
\item \textbf{Alkalmaztak-e speci\'alis jellemz\H o-kinyer\'esi (feature engineering) m\'odszert?} A legfontosabb speci\'alis m\'odszer a \textbf{Sturges-szab\'aly} haszn\'alata volt az adatok intervallumokba sorol\'as\'ahoz \'es az eredeti \'ert\'ekek \'uj csoporttags\'agokhoz val\'o hozz\'arendel\'es\'ehez (mapping).
\item \textbf{Milyen t\'ipus\'u modellt mutat be a cikk?} Egy \'ujszer\H u \textbf{ensemble (egy\"uttes) modellt}, amely a \textbf{Stacking (halmoz\'as)} technik\'ara \'es a \textbf{Triple Modular Redundancy (TMR - h\'armas modul\'aris redundancia)} algoritmusra \'ep\"ul. A rendszer alapmodellk\'ent hat algoritmust haszn\'al (DT, NB, SVM, k-NN, LR, AdaBoost), amelyek k\"oz\"ul a n\'egy legjobbat v\'alasztja ki az egy\"uttes d\"ont\'eshozatalhoz.
\item \textbf{Milyen vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt (loss function) haszn\'alnak?} Az egy\"uttes modellre vonatkoz\'oan nincs explicit glob\'alis vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'eny megnevezve. Az egyes algoritmusok saj\'at m\'odszereket haszn\'alnak (pl. a d\"ont\'esi f\'ak az \textbf{entr\'opi\'at} vagy \textbf{inform\'aci\'onyerem\'enyt}, a logisztikus regresszi\'o pedig a becs\"ult val\'osz\'in\H us\'egek \'es a t\'enyleges kimenetek k\"oz\"otti k\"ul\"onbs\'eg minimaliz\'al\'as\'at).
\item \textbf{Milyen tanul\'asi algoritmust alkalmaznak?} A modelleket a Python \textbf{sklearn} k\"onyvt\'ar\'anak \textbf{fit} funkci\'oj\'aval tan\'itj\'ak. A folyamat tartalmaz egy speci\'alis \textbf{tan\'it\'asi f\'azist}, ahol meghat\'arozz\'ak a legjobb n\'egy algoritmust \'es a legjobb TMR kombin\'aci\'ot az adatk\'eszleten. Az AdaBoost algoritmus eset\'eben iterat\'iv s\'ulyoz\'ast alkalmaznak a hib\'asan oszt\'alyozott mint\'ak jav\'it\'as\'ara.

\end{itemize}

\par
\textbf{A teljes cikk hozz\'af\'erhet\H o volt}, a v\'alaszok a sz\"oveg eg\'esze alapj\'an k\'esz\"ultek.},
  file = {/home/premissa/Zotero/storage/Q22ZUUBX/Maksimović és mtsai. - 2024 - Using Triple Modular Redundancy for Threshold Determination in DDOS Intrusion Detection Systems.pdf}
}

@article{medina-arcoMethodologyDetectionContaminated2024,
  title = {Methodology for the {{Detection}} of {{Contaminated Training Datasets}} for {{Machine Learning-Based Network Intrusion-Detection Systems}}},
  author = {{Medina-Arco}, Joaqu{\'i}n Gaspar and {Mag{\'a}n-Carri{\'o}n}, Roberto and {Rodr{\'i}guez-G{\'o}mez}, Rafael Alejandro and {Garc{\'i}a-Teodoro}, Pedro},
  year = 2024,
  month = jan,
  journal = {Sensors},
  volume = {24},
  number = {2},
  pages = {479},
  issn = {1424-8220},
  doi = {10.3390/s24020479},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {With the significant increase in cyber-attacks and attempts to gain unauthorised access to systems and information, Network Intrusion-Detection Systems (NIDSs) have become essential detection tools. Anomaly-based systems use machine learning techniques to distinguish between normal and anomalous traffic. They do this by using training datasets that have been previously gathered and labelled, allowing them to learn to detect anomalies in future data. However, such datasets can be accidentally or deliberately contaminated, compromising the performance of NIDS. This has been the case of the UGR'16 dataset, in which, during the labelling process, botnet-type attacks were not identified in the subset intended for training. This paper addresses the mislabelling problem of real network traffic datasets by introducing a novel methodology that (i) allows analysing the quality of a network traffic dataset by identifying possible hidden or unidentified anomalies and (ii) selects the ideal subset of data to optimise the performance of the anomaly detection model even in the presence of hidden attacks erroneously labelled as normal network traffic. To this end, a two-step process that makes incremental use of the training dataset is proposed. Experiments conducted on the contaminated UGR'16 dataset in conjunction with the state-of-the-art NIDS, Kitsune, conclude with the feasibility of the approach to reveal observations of hidden botnet-based attacks on this dataset.},
  langid = {english},
  note = {Az al\'abbi v\'alaszok a \textbf{,,Methodology for the Detection of Contaminated Training Datasets for Machine Learning-Based Network Intrusion-Detection Systems''} c\'im\H u cikk r\'eszletei \'es metaadatai alapj\'an k\'esz\"ultek:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Alkalmass\'ag zero-day t\'amad\'asok \'eszlel\'es\'ere:} Igen, a cikk kifejezetten eml\'iti, hogy az \textbf{anom\'alia-alap\'u bet\"or\'esjelz\'es} (NIDS) lehet\H ov\'e teszi az \textbf{\'uj vagy kor\'abban ismeretlen (zero-day)} t\'amad\'asok kezel\'es\'et, mivel ezek olyan forgalmi mint\'akat gener\'alnak, amelyek kor\'abban nem fordultak el\H o.
\item \textbf{Tan\'it\'asi paradigma:} A cikkben alkalmazott \textbf{Kitsune/KitNET} modell a \textbf{nem fel\"ugyelt (unsupervised)} tanul\'asi kateg\'ori\'aba tartozik. A rendszer felt\'etelezi, hogy a tan\'it\'o adathalmaz csak norm\'al forgalmat tartalmaz, \'es ebb\H ol tanulja meg felismerni az elt\'er\'eseket.
\item \textbf{Kiegyens\'ulyozatlan adathalmazok kezel\'ese:} Igen, a szerz\H ok foglalkoznak ezzel a probl\'em\'aval. Megjegyzik, hogy a val\'os forgalmi adathalmazok gyakran er\H osen kiegyens\'ulyozatlanok (pl. az UGR'16-ban a t\'amad\'asok csak a forgalom 2\%-\'at teszik ki). Ezt nem adat-mintav\'etelez\'essel, hanem a \textbf{teljes\'itm\'enym\'er\'esi m\'odszerek} megv\'alaszt\'as\'aval kezelik: az \textbf{F1-pontsz\'amot} \'es az \textbf{AUC (Area Under the ROC Curve)} \'ert\'eket tekintik m\'ervad\'onak az egyszer\H u pontoss\'ag (accuracy) helyett, mivel ezek jobban t\"ukr\"ozik a modell hat\'ekonys\'ag\'at ritka esem\'enyek eset\'en.
\item \textbf{Concept drift (koncepci\'ov\'alt\'as) jelens\'ege:} A cikk nem javasol specifikus algoritmust a concept drift kezel\'es\'ere, de a bemutatott \textbf{cs\'usz\'oablakos (sliding window) m\'odszertan} k\"ozvetve kezeli a h\'al\'ozati forgalom id\H obeli v\'altoz\'asait \'es a szekvenci\'alis mint\'ak jelent\H os\'eg\'et.
\item \textbf{\'Ertelmezhet\H os\'eg (interpretability):} A forr\'as az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eget a \textbf{rejtett anom\'ali\'ak vizu\'alis azonos\'it\'as\'aval} t\'amogatja. A m\'odszertan seg\'it a szakembereknek felt\'arni a hib\'asan c\'imk\'ezett szakaszokat a teljes\'itm\'enymutat\'ok (pl. F1-score hirtelen es\'ese) \'es a forgalmi statisztik\'ak \textbf{vizualiz\'aci\'oja} r\'ev\'en. Speci\'alis technik\'akat, mint a LIME vagy SMOTE, ez a cikk nem eml\'it.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'esek:}
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Csomagelemz\'es (Packet Parsing):} Metaadatok (IP-c\'imek, id\H ob\'elyegek, portok, m\'eret) kinyer\'ese.
\item \textbf{Jellemz\H o-kinyer\'es (Feature Extraction):} Statisztikai adatok gy\H ujt\'ese a forgalmi mint\'akr\'ol.
\item \textbf{Jellemz\H o-lek\'epez\'es (Feature Mapper):} Dimenzi\'ocs\"okkent\'es klaszterez\'esi technik\'akkal.
\item \textbf{Feature as a Counter (FaaC):} A h\'al\'ozati folyamatok aggreg\'al\'asa egyetlen megfigyel\'esi m\'atrixba (pl. percenk\'enti sz\'aml\'al\'ok).
\item \textbf{Tiszt\'it\'as:} A tan\'it\'o adathalmazb\'ol az ismert t\'amad\'asok elt\'avol\'it\'asa a ,,tiszta'' norm\'al profil l\'etrehoz\'as\'ahoz.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Speci\'alis jellemz\H o-kinyer\'esi m\'odszer:} A \textbf{Feature as a Counter (FaaC)} technika, amely a nyers h\'al\'ozati adatokat (timestamp, flag, port, stb.) sz\'aml\'al\'o alap\'u jellemz\H okk\'e konvert\'alja. \item \textbf{A modell t\'ipusa:} \textbf{Autoencoder-alap\'u} m\'elytanul\'asi modell. Pontosabban a \textbf{Kitsune}, amely egy \textbf{autoencoderekb\H ol \'all\'o egy\"uttest (ensemble)} haszn\'al.
\item \textbf{Vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'eny:} A modell az \textbf{RMSE (Root-Mean-Squared Error)}, azaz a n\'egyzetes k\"oz\'ephiba \'ert\'ek\'et haszn\'alja az anom\'alia m\'ert\'ek\'enek meghat\'aroz\'as\'ahoz minden egyes autoencodern\'el.
\item \textbf{Alkalmazott tan\'it\'asi algoritmus:} Egy \textbf{k\'etl\'epcs\H os iterat\'iv m\'odszertan}: az els\H o l\'ep\'esben az optim\'alis \textbf{ablakm\'eretet} keresik meg inkrement\'alis tan\'it\'assal, a m\'asodikban pedig \textbf{cs\'usz\'oablakos keres\'essel} (sliding window) azonos\'itj\'ak az ide\'alis \'es a szennyezett adatszakaszokat. A folyamat sor\'an \textbf{korai le\'all\'it\'ast (early stopping)} alkalmaznak a hat\'ekonys\'ag \'erdek\'eben.

\end{itemize}

@inproceedings{memmesheimerIncreasingDetectionRate2024,
  title = {Increasing {{Detection Rate}} for {{Imbalanced Malicious Traffic}} Using {{Generative Adversarial Networks}}},
  booktitle = {European {{Interdisciplinary Cybersecurity Conference}}},

  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {Intrusion Detection and Prevention Systems aim to detect and prevent malicious activity or policy violations. Anomaly-based models like autoencoders or neural networks have become prominent because they do not rely on pre-defined signatures. In this study, we leverage the generative abilities of a Wasserstein Generative Adversarial Network + Gradient Penalty (WGAN-GP) to create anomalies to combat class imbalance artificially. We compare its performance on the CSE-CIC-IDS2018 data set from the Canadian Institute for Cybersecurity Intrusion Detection System (CIC-IDS) with two other anomaly-based models and one discriminative model. Our model Data-Imbalanced Aware XGBoost (DIAX) excels with an F1 score of 96.90\% that shows great performance to combat class imbalances. Additionally, we conduct a detailed analysis using SHapley Additive exPlanations (SHAP) to interpret predictions of the best-performing model. Lastly, we argue that SHAP can help in the task of dimensionality reduction for classifiers.},
  isbn = {979-8-4007-1651-5},
  langid = {english},
  note = {A forr\'ask\'ent megadott cikk ("Increasing Detection Rate for Imbalanced Malicious Traffic using Generative Adversarial Networks") alapj\'an az al\'abbi v\'alaszokat adom a k\'erd\'eseire:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Alkalmas-e a megold\'as zero-day t\'amad\'asok \'eszlel\'es\'ere?} \textbf{Igen.} A cikk hangs\'ulyozza, hogy az \textbf{anom\'alia-alap\'u modellek}, mint amilyeneket a tanulm\'anyban vizsg\'altak, az\'ert v\'altak jelent\H oss\'e, mert nem el\H ore meghat\'arozott szignat\'ur\'akra t\'amaszkodnak. Ez a megk\"ozel\'it\'es lehet\H ov\'e teszi a kor\'abban ismeretlen, \'ugynevezett zero-day t\'amad\'asok azonos\'it\'as\'at a norm\'al viselked\'est\H ol val\'o elt\'er\'esek figyel\'es\'evel.
\item \textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'asi paradigm\'at haszn\'al?} A bemutatott \textbf{DIAX modell} alapvet\H oen a \textbf{fel\"ugyelt tanul\'asi (supervised learning)} paradigm\'aba sorolhat\'o, mivel c\'imk\'ezett adatokon (benignus \'es malici\'ozus forgalom) tan\'itj\'ak az XGBoost oszt\'alyoz\'ot,. Ugyanakkor a folyamat tartalmaz egy \textbf{generat\'iv} elemet is (WGAN-GP), amelyet a t\'amad\'asi mint\'ak mesters\'eges el\H o\'all\'it\'as\'ara haszn\'alnak az adathalmaz kiegyens\'ulyoz\'asa \'erdek\'eben,.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk az imbolyg\'o (imbalanced) adathalmazok kezel\'es\'evel?} \textbf{Igen.} Ez a cikk k\"ozponti t\'em\'aja. A szerz\H ok egy \textbf{Wasserstein Generative Adversarial Network + Gradient Penalty (WGAN-GP)} modellt haszn\'alnak arra, hogy mesters\'eges anom\'ali\'akat gener\'aljanak, \'es ezzel orvosolj\'ak az oszt\'aly-imbalansz probl\'em\'aj\'at,,.
\item \textbf{Kezeli-e a cikk a concept drift jelens\'eget?} A cikk \textbf{nem javasol konkr\'et technikai megold\'ast} a concept driftre, de elismeri a jelens\'eg l\'etez\'es\'et. Megjegyzik, hogy az anom\'alia-alap\'u modellek teljes\'itm\'enye az \'evek sor\'an romlik a t\'amad\'ok folyamatosan v\'altoz\'o technik\'ai miatt, ez\'ert a modellek folyamatos fejleszt\'ese \'es friss\'it\'ese kulcsfontoss\'ag\'u.
\item \textbf{Fontos szempont-e az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg (interpretability)?} \textbf{Igen}, a szerz\H ok szerint az IDS riaszt\'asok okainak meg\'ert\'ese alapvet\H o szempont. Az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eghez a \textbf{SHapley Additive exPlanations (SHAP)} technik\'at alkalmazz\'ak, amellyel elemzik az egyes jellemz\H ok (features) hat\'as\'at a modell j\'oslataira,,.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'eseket haszn\'alnak?} A cikk az al\'abbi l\'ep\'eseket sorolja fel:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Oszlopok eldob\'asa:} A Dst IP, Flow ID, Src Port, Src IP \'es Timestamp jellemz\H oket t\"or\"olt\'ek, hogy elker\"ulj\'ek a t\'ultanul\'ast (overfitting).
\item \textbf{Hi\'anyz\'o \'ert\'ekek kezel\'ese:} T\"or\"olt\'ek azokat az adatpontokat, amelyek NaN, v\'egtelen vagy negat\'iv v\'egtelen \'ert\'eket tartalmaztak.
\item \textbf{Adatfeloszt\'as:} 85\%/15\% ar\'any\'u v\'eletlenszer\H u feloszt\'ast alkalmaztak tan\'it\'o- \'es tesztk\'eszletre.
\item \textbf{Sk\'al\'az\'as:} A GAN \'es WGAN-GP modellek eset\'eben \texttt{MinMaxScaler}-t haszn\'altak a normaliz\'al\'ashoz.

\textbf{Milyen vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt (loss function) haszn\'alnak?}

\item Az XGBoost eset\'eben egy \textbf{regul\'aris objekt\'iv f\"uggv\'enyt} haszn\'alnak, amely egy tetsz\H oleges differenci\'alhat\'o konvex vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyb\H ol \'es egy komplexit\'ast b\"untet\H o regul\'aris tagb\'ol \'all.

\end{itemize}

\textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'asi algoritmust alkalmaznak?}

\begin{itemize}

\item Az XGBoost-ot \textbf{Gradient Tree Boosting} algoritmussal tan\'itj\'ak, amely addit\'iv m\'odon \'ep\'iti fel a d\"ont\'esi f\'akat,.

\end{itemize}

\end{itemize}

@article{mesadieuLeveragingDeepReinforcement2024,
  title = {Leveraging {{Deep Reinforcement Learning Technique}} for {{Intrusion Detection}} in {{SCADA Infrastructure}}},
  author = {Mesadieu, Frantzy and Torre, Damiano and Chennamaneni, Anitha},
  year = 2024,
  journal = {IEEE Access},
  volume = {12},
  pages = {63381--63399},
  issn = {2169-3536},
  doi = {10.1109/ACCESS.2024.3390722},
  urldate = {2026-03-04},
  abstract = {The prevalence of cyber-attacks perpetrated over the last two decades, including coordinated attempts to breach targeted organizations, has drastically and systematically exposed some of the more critical vulnerabilities existing in our cyber ecosystem. Particularly in Supervisory Control and Data Acquisition (SCADA) systems with targeted attacks aiming to bypass signature-based protocols, attempting to gain control over operational processes. In the past, researchers utilized deep learning and reinforcement learning algorithms to mitigate threats against industrial control systems (ICS). However, as technology evolves, these techniques become ineffective in monitoring and enhancing the cybersecurity defenses of those system against unwanted attacks. To address these concerns, we propose a deep reinforcement learning (DRL) framework for anomaly detection in the SCADA network. Our model utilizes a ``Q-network'', which allows it to achieve state-of-the-art performance in pattern recognition from complex tasks. We validated our solution on two publicly available datasets. The WUSTL-IIoT-2018 and the WUSTL-IIoT-2021, each comprised of twenty-five networking features representing benign and attack traffic. The results obtained shows that our model successfully achieved an accuracy of 99.36\% in attack detection, highlighting DRL's potential to enhance the security of critical infrastructure and laying the foundation for future research in this domain.},
  copyright = {https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-nc-nd/4.0/},
  note = {Based on the excerpts provided from the article \textbf{"Leveraging Deep Reinforcement Learning Technique for Intrusion Detection in SCADA Infrastructure,"} here are the answers to your questions:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Is the solution suitable for detecting zero-day attacks?} The article acknowledges that detecting novel or previously unseen attacks is a \textbf{significant challenge and a limitation} of the proposed model. While the authors state the system can "adapt to new threats in real-time" through continuous monitoring, they explicitly identify the detection of unknown attack patterns not present in the training dataset as a concern for future research.
\item \textbf{What training paradigm does it use, and how can it be categorized?} The model uses a \textbf{Deep Reinforcement Learning (DRL)} paradigm. It is categorized as a combination of \textbf{deep learning} and \textbf{reinforcement learning}, where an agent learns through trial and error by interacting with a predefined environment.
\item \textbf{Does the article address the handling of imbalanced datasets?} The article mentions that SCADA datasets are subject to \textbf{"data bias"} and \textbf{"sampling bias"} due to their non-stationary properties. However, it does \textbf{not} detail specific algorithmic techniques (like SMOTE or class weighting) used to handle imbalanced datasets beyond general "rigorous preprocessing".
\item \textbf{Does the article deal with the concept drift phenomenon?} \textbf{Yes.} The authors address issues stemming from \textbf{"non-stationary distributions"} and "non-stationary data". To solve this, they propose the use of a \textbf{"ReplayMemory" function} to store and sample past transitions, and they emphasize the necessity of \textbf{continuous monitoring and periodic updating} of the model with new data to adapt to emerging threats.
\item \textbf{Is interpretability considered an important aspect?} \textbf{Yes}, but the authors admit the current DRL framework \textbf{lacks interpretability}. They highlight improving explainability and interpretability as a key direction for \textbf{future research}, noting that the structural complexity makes it difficult to understand the reasoning behind the model's decisions. No specific techniques like LIME or SHAP are used in this study.
\item 
\par
\textbf{What data preprocessing steps are used?} The listed preprocessing steps include:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item Inspecting the data for \textbf{typos}.
\item Removing all \textbf{duplicates}.
\item Removing \textbf{irrelevant content}.
\item \textbf{Label mapping} and encoding.
\item Formatting data using \textbf{multi-class binary classification}.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Which datasets were used or tested in the study?} The study utilized two publicly available datasets: \textbf{WUSTL-IIoT-2018} and \textbf{WUSTL-IIoT-2021}.
\item \textbf{Is there any special feature engineering method applied?} The article mentions that the authors \textbf{"extracted features of importance"} from the datasets to fit the model's input. However, it does not describe a unique or named feature engineering algorithm; instead, the agent learns directly from the \textbf{low-dimensional feature inputs} provided by the metadata.
\item \textbf{What type of model is presented?} The article presents a \textbf{Deep Reinforcement Learning (DRL)} framework specifically using a \textbf{Deep Q-Network (DQN)}. It incorporates an \textbf{Actor-Critic algorithm} and utilizes a \textbf{Deep Neural Network (DNN)} architecture. The authors also mention the use of \textbf{Convolutional Neural Networks (CNN)} paired with Q-learning in their scheme.
\item \textbf{What loss function is used?} The model utilizes a loss function based on the \textbf{Mean Squared Error (MSE)} between the predicted Q-value and the target Q-value: \$Loss = (Q(s\_i, a\_i) - target)\textasciicircum 2\$.
\item 
\par
\textbf{What training algorithm is applied?} The training involves several components:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Deep Q-Learning (DQN)} for approximating action-value functions.
\item An \textbf{Actor-Critic algorithm} for updating policies and evaluating actions.
\item An \textbf{off-policy approach} using an \textbf{\$\textbackslash epsilon\$-greedy policy} for exploration and exploitation.
\item \textbf{Gradient descent} to minimize the loss function.

\end{enumerate}

\end{itemize}

  title = {Securing {{SDN}}: {{Hybrid}} Autoencoder-Random Forest for Intrusion Detection and Attack Mitigation},

  abstract = {Software Defined Networking (SDN) has revolutionized network administration by providing centralized management through software, enabling traffic adjustment independent of the data plane. Despite the benefits, SDN networks are prone to security threats from external sources, thus necessitating the implementation of security measures. Unfortunately, most existing efforts have been just a simple mapping of earlier solutions into the SDN environments. This paper addresses the problem of SDN security based on deep learning in a purely native SDN environment, where a Deep Learning intrusion detection module is tailored to a native SDN environment. In particular, we propose a hybrid Deep AutoEncoder with a Random Forest classifier model (DAERF) to enhance intrusion detection performance in a native SDN environment. The proposed model is incorporated into a novel adaptive framework for attack mitigation in SDN environments. The proposed framework consists of a three-layer protection mechanism for detecting and preventing attacks. It is based on entropy-based detection, hybrid machine learning in the control layer and proactive services monitoring in the application layer. Experimental results have shown that our DEARF proposed autoencoder model achieved anomaly detection rates in excess of 98\% in stand-alone mode as well as when incorporated within the framework, making it highly solution for next generation SDN networks.},

@article{minhWhenWeakestModel2025,
  title = {When the Weakest Model Sees the Threat: {{An}} Explainable Ensemble Learning System for Detecting Network Attacks},
  shorttitle = {When the Weakest Model Sees the Threat},
  author = {Minh, C{\'e}line and Vermeulen, Kevin and Lefebvre, C{\'e}dric and Owezarski, Philippe and Ritchie, William},
  year = 2025,
  month = dec,
  journal = {Computer Networks},
  volume = {273},
  pages = {111730},
  issn = {13891286},
  doi = {10.1016/j.comnet.2025.111730},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {The growing importance of network security is driven by two major challenges. First, the ever-increasing volume of network traffic, which exceeds human processing capabilities. Second, the rising frequency and sophistication of new attacks require advanced and intelligent analysis to detect. To make critical decisions, such as blocking traffic from a specific IP address, security analysts need to understand why network intrusion detection systems are raising alarms. This highlights the limitations of relying on machine learning models whose internal decision-making processes are not transparent, often referred to as ``black boxes''. The key issue is their lack of interpretability when justifying critical security actions. Consequently, this paper emphasizes the need for providing explainable machine learning solutions. To detect new attacks effectively, we focus on behavioral approaches - specifically, analyzing short time windows of aggregated traffic to identify abnormal patterns - by experimenting with various unsupervised machine learning detectors. We found that these detectors often provide complementary results: they do not always agree on the same detections, and in some cases, a globally less effective detector can be the only one capable of accurately identifying a specific attack. This underlines the importance of adopting an ensemble approach to combine the strengths and perspectives of different models. Our contributions are threefold. First, we introduce a stacking-based ensemble learning strategy that improves detection accuracy by incorporating minority reports, going beyond standard majority-voting methods. Second, we present a visual representation technique that converts anomaly scores into heatmaps, making the system's outputs more interpretable to human analysts. Third, we leverage convolutional neural networks to process these visual representations, simulating human reasoning and enhancing pattern recognition while maintaining transparency. Overall, we develop and evaluate an unsupervised, explainable system capable of detecting even previously unknown network attacks, combining the strengths of ensemble learning and visual interpretability.},
  langid = {english},
  note = {A megadott forr\'asdokumentum ("When the weakest model sees the threat: An explainable ensemble learning system for detecting network attacks") alapj\'an a v\'alaszok a k\"ovetkez\H ok:

@article{narmadhaImprovedNetworkAnomaly2025,
  title = {Improved Network Anomaly Detection System Using Optimized Autoencoder - {{LSTM}}},
  author = {Narmadha, S. and Balaji, N.V.},
  year = 2025,
  month = may,
  journal = {Expert Systems with Applications},
  volume = {273},
  pages = {126854},
  issn = {09574174},
  doi = {10.1016/j.eswa.2025.126854},
  urldate = {2026-03-04},
  abstract = {Intrusion detection (ID) gives security in network traffic or system activities monitors to detect suspicious activities, behavior, potential attacks, or unauthorized access. IDs are crucial in cybersecurity, as organizations identify and respond to threats before they cause harm. The anomaly-based detection method is a popular and challenging research area in identifying new threats. So, this study focuses on developing an efficient network anomaly-based detection approach. It can establish a baseline for normal behavior and flag deviations from this baseline as potential threats. So, it can detect new or unknown attacks that deviate from standard traffic patterns. The study's main objective is to reduce the false positive rate and improve the class imbalance issues in the data. So, an optimized Deep Learning (DL) model is developed to detect new threats and reduce the false positive rate in the present ID systems. The DL model combines the Long Short-Term Memory (LSTM) with the Autoencoder model, where the auto-encoder learns normal patterns, while LSTM handles sequential dependencies in the data. Moreover, the LSTM model performance is optimized using Particle Swarm Optimization (PSO). The performance of the DL model is evaluated with existing IDS methods and the results shows that the proposed model achieves maximum detection accuracy rate of 0.9989.},
  langid = {english},
  note = {Az al\'abbi v\'alaszokat a megadott forr\'asdokumentum (\textbf{"Improved network anomaly detection system using optimized autoencoder - LSTM"}) alapj\'an k\'esz\'itettem el:
