@article{nasreenfathimaEnhancingNetworkTraffic2024,
  title = {Enhancing {{Network Traffic Anomaly Detection}}: {{Leveraging Temporal Correlation Index}} in a {{Hybrid Framework}}},
  shorttitle = {Enhancing {{Network Traffic Anomaly Detection}}},
  author = {Nasreen Fathima, A. H. and Ibrahim, S. P. Syed and Khraisat, Ansam},
  year = 2024,
  journal = {IEEE Access},
  volume = {12},
  pages = {136805--136824},
  issn = {2169-3536},
  doi = {10.1109/ACCESS.2024.3458903},
  urldate = {2025-06-13},
  abstract = {The modern digital environment is becoming increasingly interconnected, underscoring the critical need to safeguard network infrastructures. Detecting anomalies in network traffic remains essential as cyber threats continue to evolve. Analyzing trends, patterns, and relationships in network traffic data over time poses challenges. On the other hand, traditional generative neural networks emphasize detecting network attacks but encounter difficulties due to limitations in capturing the temporal and dynamic aspects of network traffic. This paper introduces a new methodology aimed at enhancing the identification of irregularities in network traffic using a Temporal Metric-Driven GRU Embedded Generative Neural Network (TMG-GRU-VAE). This method incorporates Gated Recurrent Units (GRU) into variational autoencoders to effectively train on the temporal characteristics of network traffic in temporal sequential networks. Moreover, we present a Temporal Correlation Index (TCI) score designed for anomaly detection in Network Intrusion Detection Systems (NIDS). This innovative metric offers a sophisticated and dynamic assessment of temporal behavior within network traffic. TCI's ability to distinguish between normal and anomalous temporal patterns plays a pivotal role in mitigating false positives. Our proposed method greatly improves the detection of small changes in abnormal sequences over time, enhancing accuracy by making anomalies stand out more clearly and reducing false alarms, thereby making the system more reliable. The proposed work, validated using the CIC-IDS-2017 and CIC-IDS-2018 datasets, demonstrates a significant decrease in False Positives (FP) across all models. Notable improvements range from 7.2\% to 12.9\% for the CIC-IDS-2017 dataset and from 7.1\% to 14.1\% for the CIC-IDS-2018 dataset. This highlights its significant impact on decreasing false positive rates.},
  keywords = {Anomaly detection,Correlation,deep learning,Deep learning,generative model,Indexes,Network intrusion detection,network intrusion detection system,Network security,network traffic,security,Telecommunication traffic,temporal correlation,unknown attack,variational autoencoder},
  note = {A megadott forr\'asdokumentum alapj\'an az al\'abbi v\'alaszokat adom a k\'erd\'eseidre:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Alkalmas-e a megold\'as a zero-day t\'amad\'asok \'eszlel\'es\'ere?} \textbf{Igen}, elm\'eletileg alkalmas, mivel a rendszer egy \textbf{anom\'alia-alap\'u bet\"or\'esjelz\H o (AIDS)}, amely a norm\'al viselked\'est\H ol val\'o elt\'er\'eseket figyeli, \'igy k\'epes az ismeretlen \'es a zero-day t\'amad\'asok azonos\'it\'as\'ara is,. Ugyanakkor a szerz\H ok a konkl\'uzi\'oban megjegyzik, hogy a jelenlegi megk\"ozel\'it\'es els\H osorban az ismert t\'amad\'asi mint\'akra f\'okusz\'al, \'es el\H ofordulhat, hogy a j\"ov\H oben felbukkan\'o \'uj zero-day fenyeget\'esek ellen nem ny\'ujt teljes v\'edelmet.
\item \textbf{Milyen tanul\'asi paradigm\'at haszn\'al, \'es hogyan kategoriz\'alhat\'o?} A cikkben bemutatott megold\'as egy \textbf{fel\"ugyelet n\'elk\"uli (unsupervised)} m\'elytanul\'asi m\'odszer,,. A generat\'iv modell (Variational Autoencoder) c\'imk\'ezetlen adatokon tanulja meg a norm\'al h\'al\'ozati forgalom alapvet\H o strukt\'ur\'ait \'es mint\'azatait,.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk a kiegyens\'ulyozatlan adatk\'eszletek (imbalanced datasets) kezel\'es\'evel?} \textbf{Igen.} A szerz\H ok kifejtik, hogy a h\'al\'ozati forgalmi adatokn\'al gyakori probl\'ema az oszt\'alyok egyenl\H otlen eloszl\'asa. A megold\'asuk az\'ert alkalmaz \textbf{Variational Autoencoder (VAE)} modellt, mert az elfogulatlanul k\'epes tanulni a szekvenci\'alis adatokb\'ol, \'es a generat\'iv megk\"ozel\'it\'es seg\'it a kiegyens\'ulyozatlan oszt\'alyeloszl\'asok kezel\'es\'eben,,.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk a concept drift jelens\'eggel?} B\'ar a "concept drift" kifejez\'est nem haszn\'alj\'ak explicit m\'odon, a cikk \textbf{foglalkozik a h\'al\'ozati forgalom dinamikus v\'altoz\'as\'aval}. Megold\'ask\'ent a \textbf{Temporal Correlation Index (TCI)} m\'er\H osz\'amot javasolj\'ak, amely lehet\H ov\'e teszi a \textbf{dinamikus anom\'alia-\'eszlel\'esi k\"usz\"ob\'ert\'ekek} be\'all\'it\'as\'at, \'igy a rendszer k\'epes alkalmazkodni a h\'al\'ozati forgalom id\H obeli v\'altoz\'asaihoz,,.
\item \textbf{Fontos szempont-e az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg (interpretability)? Milyen technik\'akat haszn\'alnak?} A cikk megeml\'iti az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eget, de ink\'abb mint \textbf{j\"ov\H obeli kutat\'asi ir\'anyt}. A szerz\H ok hangs\'ulyozz\'ak, hogy a j\"ov\H oben explainable AI (XAI) modelleket kell fejleszteni, hogy jobban meg\'erts\'ek, mi\'ert jel\"ol meg a rendszer bizonyos forgalmat anom\'aliak\'ent. A jelenlegi tanulm\'anyban nem eml\'itenek olyan konkr\'et technik\'akat, mint a LIME vagy a SHAP.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'eseket alkalmaznak?} A forr\'as az al\'abbi l\'ep\'eseket sorolja fel:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Inkonzisztenci\'ak elt\'avol\'it\'asa}.
\item \textbf{Hi\'anyz\'o \'ert\'ekek kezel\'ese}.
\item \textbf{Relev\'ans jellemz\H ok kiv\'alaszt\'asa}.
\item \textbf{One-hot k\'odol\'as} a kateg\'orikus v\'altoz\'okhoz,.
\item \textbf{Min-max normaliz\'aci\'o} a numerikus jellemz\H okh\"oz,.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Mely adatk\'eszleteket haszn\'alt\'ak vagy tesztelt\'ek a tanulm\'anyban?} A kutat\'as sor\'an a \textbf{CIC-IDS-2017} \'es a \textbf{CIC-IDS-2018} benchmark adatk\'eszleteket haszn\'alt\'ak,,.
\item \textbf{Alkalmaznak-e valamilyen speci\'alis feature engineering m\'odszert?} \textbf{Igen}, a szerz\H ok bevezetik a \textbf{Temporal Correlation Index (TCI)} nev\H u \'uj m\'er\H osz\'amot,. Ez a mutat\'o az eredeti \'es a rekonstru\'alt szekvenci\'ak k\"oz\"otti id\H obeli korrel\'aci\'ot m\'eri, seg\'itve a finomabb elt\'er\'esek azonos\'it\'as\'at,. Emellett megeml\'itik a magas szint\H u jellemz\H o-lek\'epez\'est (high-level feature mapping) is a VAE-n bel\"ul.
\item \textbf{Milyen t\'ipus\'u modellt mutatnak be?} A bemutatott modell egy hibrid \textbf{m\'ely tanul\'asi (deep learning)} architekt\'ura, pontosabban egy \textbf{Gated Recurrent Unit (GRU)} egys\'egekkel kieg\'esz\'itett \textbf{Variational Autoencoder (VAE)}, melynek neve \textbf{TMG-GRU-VAE},,.
\item \textbf{Milyen vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt (loss function) haszn\'alnak?} A modell tan\'it\'asa sor\'an a \textbf{negat\'iv log-likelihood} minimaliz\'al\'as\'ara t\"orekednek, amely azt m\'eri, hogy a modell mennyire j\'ol j\'osolja meg az aktu\'alis adatpontot a m\'ultbeli megfigyel\'esek alapj\'an,,.
\item \textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'asi algoritmust alkalmaznak?} A vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'eny optimaliz\'al\'as\'ahoz gradiens-alap\'u technik\'akat, p\'eld\'aul \textbf{Stochastic Gradient Descent (SGD)} vagy \textbf{Adam} optimaliz\'al\'ot haszn\'alnak,. A tan\'it\'as t\"obb korszakon (epoch) kereszt\"ul zajlik a TensorFlow k\"onyvt\'ar seg\'its\'eg\'evel.

\end{itemize}

\par
\textbf{A cikk hozz\'af\'erhet\H os\'ege:} A v\'alaszok a forr\'ask\'ent megadott cikk kiterjedt r\'eszletein alapulnak (absztrakt, m\'odszertan, eredm\'enyek, k\"ovetkeztet\'esek), \'igy az inform\'aci\'ok a \textbf{teljes sz\"ovegb\H ol sz\'armaznak}, nem csup\'an metaadatokb\'ol.},

@article{nessAnomalyDetectionNetwork2025,
  title = {Anomaly {{Detection}} in {{Network Traffic Using Advanced Machine Learning Techniques}}},
  author = {Ness, Stephanie and Eswarakrishnan, Vishwanath and Sridharan, Harish and Shinde, Varun and Venkata Prasad Janapareddy, Naga and Dhanawat, Vineet},
  year = 2025,
  journal = {IEEE Access},
  volume = {13},
  pages = {16133--16149},
  issn = {2169-3536},
  doi = {10.1109/ACCESS.2025.3526988},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {Anomaly detection in network traffic is a critical aspect of network security, particularly in defending against the increasing sophistication of cyber threats. This study investigates the application of various machine learning models for detecting anomalies in network traffic, specifically focusing on their effectiveness in addressing challenges such as class imbalance and feature complexity. The models assessed include Isolation Forest, Naive Bayes, XGBoost, LightGBM, and SVM classification. Through comprehensive evaluation, this research explores both supervised and unsupervised approaches, comparing their performance across key metrics like accuracy, F1-score, and recall. The results reveal that while models like XGBoost and LightGBM exhibit impressive performance, with LightGBM achieving near-perfect training accuracy (1.0) and solid test accuracy (0.85), others like Isolation Forest show limitations with low accuracy. The study highlights the strengths and weaknesses of each model, providing valuable insights into their practical application for network anomaly detection. By comparing different algorithms, this research contributes to advancing the application of machine learning in network security, offering guidance on model selection and optimization for improved detection of cyber threats.},
  copyright = {https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/legalcode},
  note = {A forr\'ask\'ent megadott cikk (Ness \'es mtsai, 2025) alapj\'an a k\'erd\'eseire az al\'abbi v\'alaszok adhat\'ok:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Alkalmas-e a megold\'as zero-day t\'amad\'asok \'eszlel\'es\'ere?} \textbf{Igen}, a cikk szerint az anom\'alia-\'eszlel\'es c\'elja a szokatlan mint\'ak \'es viselked\'esek felfedez\'ese, ami proakt\'iv v\'edelmet ny\'ujt a biztons\'agi kock\'azatokkal szemben. A szerz\H ok k\"ul\"on kiemelik, hogy az \textbf{inkrement\'alis tanul\'as} (online learning) alkalmaz\'asa -- amelyet p\'eld\'aul a LightGBM t\'amogat -- d\"ont\H o fontoss\'ag\'u lenne a \textbf{zero-day anom\'ali\'ak} \'eszlel\'es\'ehez, mivel \'igy a modell a folyamatosan \'erkez\H o adatok alapj\'an k\'epes friss\"ulni.
\item \textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'asi paradigm\'at haszn\'al?} A tanulm\'any mind \textbf{fel\"ugyelt (supervised)}, mind \textbf{fel\"ugyelet n\'elk\"uli (unsupervised)} megk\"ozel\'it\'eseket vizsg\'alt \'es \"osszehasonl\'itott. Az Isolation Forest modellt fel\"ugyelet n\'elk\"uli m\'odszerk\'ent kategoriz\'alj\'ak, m\'ig a t\"obbi vizsg\'alt algoritmus (XGBoost, LightGBM, SVM, Naive Bayes) fel\"ugyelt tanul\'ast alkalmaz.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk a kiegyens\'ulyozatlan adatk\'eszletek kezel\'es\'evel?} \textbf{Igen}, a cikk kiemelten kezeli az adatok egyens\'ulyhi\'any\'at. A megold\'as a \textbf{SMOTE (Synthetic Minority Over-sampling Technique)} haszn\'alata, amellyel szintetikus mint\'akat gener\'alnak a kisebbs\'egi oszt\'alyokhoz, hogy az adatk\'eszlet oszt\'alyai k\"ozel egyenl\H o m\'eret\H uek legyenek, cs\"okkentve ezzel a modell elfogults\'ag\'at.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk a koncepci\'ov\'alt\'as (concept drift) jelens\'eg\'evel?} \textbf{Igen}, a szerz\H ok javasolj\'ak a \textbf{drift-\'eszlel\'esi m\'odszerek} (drift detection methods) integr\'al\'as\'at, hogy a rendszerek k\'epesek legyenek \'eszlelni, ha az alapul szolg\'al\'o adateloszl\'as megv\'altozik, \'es ez\'altal sz\"uks\'egess\'e v\'alik a modell \'ujratan\'it\'asa a fejl\H od\H o forgalmi mint\'akhoz val\'o alkalmazkod\'ashoz.
\item \textbf{Fontos szempont-e az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg?} \textbf{Igen}, a tanulm\'any egyik c\'elja az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg (interpretability) jav\'it\'asa. Az alkalmazott technik\'ak k\"oz\"ul az \textbf{XGBoost jellemz\H o-fontoss\'agi diagramj\'at} (feature importance plot) emelik ki, amely \'atl\'athat\'ov\'a teszi, mely jellemz\H ok befoly\'asolj\'ak a modell d\"ont\'eseit. Emellett a Naive Bayes modellt egyszer\H us\'ege miatt eleve magas \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg\H unek tartj\'ak.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'eseket alkalmaznak?} A cikk az al\'abbi l\'ep\'eseket sorolja fel:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Hi\'anyz\'o \'ert\'ekek kezel\'ese}: \'Atlaggal val\'o helyettes\'it\'es (mean substitution).
\item \textbf{Duplik\'alt bejegyz\'esek elt\'avol\'it\'asa}: Pontos egyez\'es sz\H ur\'ese (exact match filtering).
\item \textbf{Kategorikus jellemz\H ok k\'odol\'asa}: C\'imkek\'odol\'as (label encoding).
\item \textbf{Adatkiegyens\'ulyoz\'as}: SMOTE alkalmaz\'asa.
\item \textbf{Standardiz\'al\'as}: Z-score normaliz\'al\'as (0 \'atlag \'es 1 sz\'or\'as).
\item \textbf{Jellemz\H o-kinyer\'es}: Dimenzi\'ocs\"okkent\'es \textbf{PCA} (f\H okomponens-anal\'izis) seg\'its\'eg\'evel.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Mely adatk\'eszleteket haszn\'alt\'ak a vizsg\'alat sor\'an?} A kutat\'as els\H osorban a \textbf{KDDCUP'99} benchmark adatk\'eszletet haszn\'alta.
\item \textbf{Alkalmaznak-e speci\'alis jellemz\H o-m\'ern\"oki (feature engineering) m\'odszert?} Igen, a jellemz\H ok kinyer\'es\'ere \'es a dimenzi\'ocs\"okkent\'esre \textbf{Principal Component Analysis (PCA)} elj\'ar\'ast alkalmaztak, amely seg\'it felt\'arni az adatokban l\'ev\H o rejtett \"osszef\"ugg\'eseket.
\item \textbf{Milyen t\'ipus\'u modellt mutatnak be?} A tanulm\'any \textbf{klasszikus g\'epi tanul\'asi (classical machine learning)} modelleket mutat be \'es hasonl\'it \"ossze, k\"ozt\"uk az Isolation Forest, Naive Bayes, XGBoost, LightGBM, SVM, Random Forest \'es Logistic Regression algoritmusokat.
\item \textbf{Milyen vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt haszn\'alnak?} A cikk eml\'iti a \textbf{vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'eny (loss function)} haszn\'alat\'at a LightGBM \'es az XGBoost objekt\'iv f\"uggv\'enyeinek r\'eszek\'ent, de konkr\'et matematikai megnevez\'est (mint p\'eld\'aul a Cross-Entropy) nem specifik\'al az \'altal\'anos "l" jel\"ol\'esen t\'ul.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'asi algoritmust alkalmaznak?} A tan\'it\'asi folyamat r\'eszei:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Hiperparam\'eter-optimaliz\'al\'as}: Grid search (Naive Bayes, SVM) \'es random search (XGBoost, LightGBM) alkalmaz\'asa.
\item \textbf{5-sz\"or\"os keresztvalid\'aci\'o} a tan\'it\'o halmazon a t\'ultanul\'as elker\"ul\'ese \'erdek\'eben.
\item Az adatok \textbf{80:20 ar\'any\'u} feloszt\'asa tan\'it\'o \'es tesztel\H o halmazra.

\end{itemize}

\end{itemize}

\par
\textbf{A v\'alaszok alapja:} A forr\'asanyag a cikk \textbf{szinte teljes sz\"oveg\'et} (bevezet\'es, kapcsol\'od\'o munk\'ak, m\'odszertan, k\'is\'erleti eredm\'enyek, k\"ovetkeztet\'esek) tartalmazza, \'igy a fenti v\'alaszok a teljes tartalom, nem pedig csup\'an a metaadatok alapj\'an k\'esz\"ultek.},
  file = {/home/premissa/Zotero/storage/MVY5JE2W/Ness és mtsai. - 2025 - Anomaly Detection in Network Traffic Using Advanced Machine Learning Techniques.pdf}
}

@article{nieIntrusionDetectionAlgorithm2025,
  title = {Intrusion Detection Algorithm of Wireless Network Based on Network Traffic Anomaly Analysis},
  author = {Nie, Xiangqian and Xing, Jiao and Li, Qimeng and Xiao, Fan},
  year = 2025,
  month = jun,
  journal = {Egyptian Informatics Journal},
  volume = {30},
  pages = {100689},
  issn = {11108665},
  doi = {10.1016/j.eij.2025.100689},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {Due to the openness and sharing nature of wireless networks, they are vulnerable to various network attacks. To promptly identify and mitigate abnormal behaviors while ensuring normal operation and security, this paper proposes an algorithm for detecting compromised nodes in wireless networks based on network traffic anomaly analysis. In the proposed detection architecture, a network traffic data acquisition module mines and reconstructs real-time traffic data from wireless nodes, removing redundant information. The processed data is then fed into an anomaly analysis module, where abnormal traffic features are extracted and dimensionality-reduced via a stacked autoencoder to form standardized anomaly profiles. These features are analyzed by an intrusion detection module combining particle swarm optimization and support vector machine algorithms. Experimental results demonstrate that the algorithm efficiently extracts traffic anomalies, accurately detects attack duration and traffic volume changes in compromised nodes, and maintains a false detection rate below 6 \%.},
  langid = {english},
  note = {A megadott forr\'asdokumentum ("Intrusion detection algorithm of wireless network based on network traffic anomaly analysis") alapj\'an az al\'abbi v\'alaszokat adom a k\'erd\'eseire:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Alkalmas-e a megold\'as zero-day t\'amad\'asok \'eszlel\'es\'ere?} A cikk nem haszn\'alja konkr\'etan a "zero-day" kifejez\'est, de hangs\'ulyozza, hogy az algoritmus \textbf{h\'al\'ozati forgalom anom\'alia-elemz\'es\'en} alapul, amelynek c\'elja a rendellenes mint\'ak \'es biztons\'agi fenyeget\'esek korai azonos\'it\'asa,. Ez a megk\"ozel\'it\'es elm\'eletileg alkalmas ismeretlen t\'amad\'asok \'eszlel\'es\'ere az\'altal, hogy a norm\'al forgalmi \'allapott\'ol val\'o elt\'er\'eseket figyeli.
\item \textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'asi paradigm\'at haszn\'al?} A rendszer egy hibrid megk\"ozel\'it\'est alkalmaz: a jellemz\H ok kinyer\'es\'ere \'es dimenzi\'ocs\"okkent\'es\'ere \textbf{unsupervised} (fel\"ugyelet n\'elk\"uli) tanul\'ast haszn\'al egy stacked autoencoder seg\'its\'eg\'evel, m\'ig a konkr\'et oszt\'alyoz\'ast \'es \'eszlel\'est \textbf{supervised} (fel\"ugyelt) tanul\'assal v\'egzi egy SVM modell seg\'its\'eg\'evel, amely c\'imk\'ezett adatokat ig\'enyel (norm\'al vagy rendellenes),.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk az imbolyg\'o (imbalanced) adathalmazok kezel\'es\'evel?} Igen, a szerz\H ok megjegyzik, hogy a felhaszn\'alt KDD cup '99 adathalmazban kev\'es a rendellenes adat, ez\'ert a probl\'ema kezel\'es\'ere \textbf{t\"obbf\'ele szimul\'alt t\'amad\'ast vezettek be}, hogy n\"ovelj\'ek a rendellenes mint\'ak sz\'am\'at \'es valid\'alj\'ak a modellt,.
\item \textbf{Kezeli-e a cikk a concept drift jelens\'eget?} A forr\'as \textbf{nem tesz eml\'it\'est a concept drift} jelens\'egr\H ol, \'es nem javasol konkr\'et megold\'ast a h\'al\'ozati forgalom statisztikai jellemz\H oinek id\H obeli megv\'altoz\'as\'ara.
\item \textbf{Fontos szempont-e az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg (interpretability)?} A dokumentum \textbf{nem t\'er ki az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'egre}, mint kiemelt szempontra, \'es nem eml\'it olyan technik\'akat, mint a LIME vagy a SHAP. (A SMOTE egy adatkiegyenl\'it\H o technika, de az sem szerepel a sz\"ovegben).
\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'eseket haszn\'alnak?} A cikk az al\'abbi el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'eseket sorolja fel:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item Val\'os idej\H u forgalmi adatok b\'any\'aszata \'es redund\'ans inform\'aci\'ok elt\'avol\'it\'asa.
\item \textbf{Varianciaanal\'izis} az adatok sim\'it\'asa \'es folytonoss\'aga \'erdek\'eben.
\item \textbf{Grubbs-teszt} az adathalmazban l\'ev\H o kiugr\'o \'ert\'ekek (outliers) \'es redund\'ans adatok azonos\'it\'as\'ara \'es kiz\'ar\'as\'ara.
\item \textbf{S-transzform\'aci\'o spektr\'alis felbont\'as} az adatok egyenletess\'eg\'enek biztos\'it\'as\'ara \'es a feldolgoz\'asi komplexit\'as cs\"okkent\'es\'ere.
\item \textbf{Short-Time Fourier Transform (STFT)} alkalmaz\'asa a Gauss-ablak funkci\'oval,.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Mely adathalmazokat haszn\'alt\'ak vagy tesztelt\'ek a tanulm\'anyban?} A tanulm\'any a \textbf{KDD cup '99} adathalmazt haszn\'alta, kieg\'esz\'itve saj\'at szimul\'alt t\'amad\'ast\'ipusokkal (pl. Land attack, Syn flood, DDoS, IP spoofing),.
\item \textbf{Alkalmaztak-e speci\'alis feature engineering m\'odszert?} Igen, \textbf{sliding window (cs\'usz\'oablak) alap\'u jellemz\H o-kinyer\'est} alkalmaznak, amellyel 23 dimenzi\'os statisztikai jellemz\H oket (csomagsz\'am, b\'ajtsz\'am, id\H ointervallum statisztik\'ak stb.) gy\H ujtenek,. Ezt kiterjesztik \"ot id\H oablakra, \"osszesen 115 dimenzi\'os jellemz\H ok\'eszletet hozva l\'etre,.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen t\'ipus\'u modellt mutatnak be?} A bemutatott modell egy hibrid architekt\'ura:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Deep Learning:} Stacked autoencoder (SAE) a dimenzi\'ocs\"okkent\'eshez,.
\item \textbf{Classical Machine Learning:} Support Vector Machine (SVM) az oszt\'alyoz\'ashoz, Improved Particle Swarm Optimization (IPSO) algoritmussal optimaliz\'alva,.

\end{itemize}

\item \textbf{Milyen vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt (loss function) haszn\'alnak?} A stacked autoencoder eset\'eben a \textbf{rekonstrukci\'os hib\'at} (reconstruction error) haszn\'alj\'ak vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyk\'ent, amelyet a bemeneti vektor \'es a dek\'odolt vektor k\"ul\"onbs\'egek\'ent hat\'aroznak meg (\$L = \textbar x - g(f(x))\textbar\textasciicircum 2\$).
\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'asi algoritmust alkalmaznak?}
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item Az autoencoder h\'al\'ozat param\'etereit \textbf{backpropagation} (visszaterjeszt\'es) seg\'its\'eg\'evel optimaliz\'alj\'ak a rekonstrukci\'os hiba minimaliz\'al\'asa \'erdek\'eben,.
\item Az SVM oszt\'alyoz\'o eset\'eben a param\'eterek (penalty factor \$Q\$ \'es kernel function width \$\textbackslash sigma\textasciicircum 2\$) optimaliz\'al\'as\'ara az \textbf{IPSO (Improved Particle Swarm Optimization)} algoritmust alkalmazz\'ak,,.

\end{itemize}

\end{itemize}

@article{parkHybridMultistageFramework2026,
  title = {Hybrid Multi-Stage Framework for Identifying Zero-Day Attacks and Known Threats in Network Traffic},
  author = {Park, Yeon-Seo and Lim, Yeon-Sup},
  year = 2026,
  month = feb,
  journal = {Computer Networks},
  volume = {275},
  pages = {111875},
  issn = {13891286},
  doi = {10.1016/j.comnet.2025.111875},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {The rapid advance of network technologies and the proliferation of diverse network applications have led to the continuous emergence of zero-day attacks-novel, previously unseen cyber threats. Detecting such attacks remains a fundamental challenge for intrusion detection systems (IDS), as they deviate from known patterns and cannot be captured effectively by traditional signature-based or purely supervised approaches. To tackle this challenge, we propose a multi-stage detection framework that integrates unsupervised and supervised machine learning approaches, such as clustering, a variational autoencoder (VAE), and a deep neural network (DNN), to identify both zero-day and known attacks. The process begins with a benign traffic filter based on radius-aware K-means clustering, which employs a robust statistical threshold to define cluster boundaries. The remaining traffic is then analyzed by a VAE that leverages Mahalanobis distance in the latent space, relative to benign traffic, to detect zero-day threats as anomalies. Finally, a supervised DNN classifies the remaining traffic into known attack categories. Our evaluation using the CICIDS2017 and CICIDS2018 datasets demonstrates that the proposed framework achieves strong generalization to unseen attacks, low false positive rates, and robustness under realistic network conditions. These results highlight the effectiveness of combining unsupervised and supervised learning for zero-day attack detection.},
  langid = {english},
  note = {Based on the provided sources, here are the answers to your questions regarding the article \textbf{"Hybrid multi-stage framework for identifying zero-day attacks and known threats in network traffic"} by Park and Lim (2026):

\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Suitability for detecting zero-day attacks:} Yes, the solution is explicitly designed for this purpose. The framework uses a \textbf{Variational Autoencoder (VAE)} to identify statistical deviations associated with zero-day attacks as anomalies in the latent space.
\item \textbf{Training paradigm and categorization:} The article presents a \textbf{hybrid multi-stage framework}. It integrates \textbf{unsupervised learning} (radius-aware K-means clustering and VAE) with \textbf{supervised learning} (Deep Neural Network).
\item \textbf{Handling of imbalanced datasets:} Yes, the article addresses this. The authors \textbf{exclude attack classes with fewer than 700 samples} to avoid sparsity and maintain training stability. They also \textbf{group semantically similar attack types} into single categories to reduce fragmented labels. Additionally, the authors note that the small number of zero-day instances in certain scenarios makes precision metrics highly sensitive to misclassifications.
\item \textbf{Concept drift phenomenon:} The article acknowledges that supervised models struggle with \textbf{distributional shifts}. While the current framework does not implement a real-time drift correction mechanism, the authors propose \textbf{periodic retraining and adaptive hyperparameter/threshold tuning} as future work to handle dynamic traffic conditions.
\item \textbf{Interpretability:} Interpretability is considered an important aspect of the framework. No specific techniques like LIME or SHAP are mentioned in the sources.

\item \textbf{Training algorithm:} The framework uses the \textbf{AdamW optimizer} for training both the VAE and the DNN. It also incorporates \textbf{batch normalization} and \textbf{dropout regularization} to stabilize and accelerate the training process.

@article{pavithraOptimizingNetworkSecurity2025,
  title = {Optimizing Network Security: {{Weighted}} Average Ensemble of {{BPNN}} and {{RELM}} in {{EPRN-WPS}} Intrusion Detection},
  shorttitle = {Optimizing Network Security},
  author = {Pavithra, P.S. and Durgadevi, P.},
  year = 2025,
  month = mar,
  journal = {Computers \& Security},
  volume = {150},
  pages = {104289},
  issn = {01674048},
  doi = {10.1016/j.cose.2024.104289},
  urldate = {2026-03-04},
  abstract = {Intrusion Detection Systems (IDS) are crucial components of network security solutions designed to identify and reduce threats in real-time. The main function of IDS is to determine unauthorized access, anomalies, and misuse. When an anomaly is detected, the IDS alerts the network administrators or takes predefined actions to alleviate the threat. Several deep learning (DL) based techniques have been designed for effective IDS. Despite that, they face several complexities such as encrypted traffic, network complexity, less efficiency, and scalability issues. This research work designs a novel method named Ensemble Probability Regularized Network-based Waterwheel Plant Search (EPRN-WPS) algorithm for improving network security and integrity. The proposed framework integrates six phases namely, data collection, monitoring interval phase, alert preprocessing phase, alert scrubbing phase, alert correlation engine phase, and alert prioritization phase. For evaluation, the proposed framework deploys the input data from the Network Intrusion Detection Dataset (UNR-IDD). During, the monitor interval phase the model continuously monitored the network activities to generate more accurate alerts by deriving a diverse set of data over time. In the alert preprocessing phase, the relevant alerts are prioritized and unnecessary information is eliminated. Furthermore, the alert scrubbing phase is utilized to analyze and filter the alerts to reduce false positives and point out security threats. The potential threats by correlating alerts from various sources are identified in the alert correlation engine phase. For alert prioritization, the proposed technique EPRN-WPS combines a significance of Biased Probability Neural Network (BPNN), Regularized Extreme Learning Machine (RELM), and weighted average ensemble models and classifies the alerts into low, high, and medium. Moreover, the proposed framework implemented a Waterwheel plant optimization with an initial search strategy for optimizating the parameters thereby enhancing the effectiveness of the EPRN-WPS method. The proposed methodology achieves an accuracy of 98.9 \%, a sensitivity of 97.2 \%, a specificity of 97.7 \%, an F1-score of 96.3 \%, and a False Alarm Rate (FAR) of 1.4 \%. The experimental results show the effectiveness of the proposed EPRN-WPS method in intrusion detection and it ensures the integrity of the network.},

\item \textbf{Adattiszt\'it\'as (Alert scrubbing):} a redund\'ans inform\'aci\'ok t\"orl\'ese a false positive riaszt\'asok minimaliz\'al\'asa \'erdek\'eben

\end{enumerate}

\end{enumerate}

@article{prabuBichannelHybridGAN2024,
  title = {Bi-Channel Hybrid {{GAN}} Attention Based Anomaly Detection System for Multi-Domain {{SDN}} Environment},
  author = {Prabu, Saranya and Padmanabhan, Jayashree},

  urldate = {2026-03-04},
  abstract = {Software-Defined Networking (SDN) is a strategy that leads the network via software by separating its control plane from the underlying forwarding plane. In support of a global digital network, multi-domain SDN architecture emerges as a viable solution. However, the complex and ever-evolving nature of network threats in a multi-domain environment presents a significant security challenge for controllers in detecting abnormalities. Moreover, multi-domain anomaly detection poses a daunting problem due to the need to process vast amounts of data from diverse domains. Deep learning models have gained popularity for extracting high-level feature representations from massive datasets. In this work, a novel deep neural network architecture, supervised learning based LD-BiHGA (Low Dimensional Bi-channel Hybrid GAN Attention) system is designed to learn class-specific features for accurate anomaly detection. Two asymmetric GANs are employed for learning the normal and abnormal network flows separately. Then, to extract more relevant features, a bi-channel attention mechanism is added. This is the first study to introduce an innovative hybrid architecture that merges bi-channel hybrid GANs with attention models for the purpose of anomaly detection in a multi-domain SDN environment that effectively handles real-time unbalanced data. The suggested architecture demonstrates its effectiveness on three benchmark datasets, achieving an average accuracy improvement of 7.225\% on balanced datasets and 3.335\% on imbalanced datasets compared to previous intrusion detection system (IDS) architectures in the literature.},
  note = {Based on the provided excerpts from the article, here are the answers to your questions:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Is the solution suitable for detecting zero-day attacks?} \textbf{Yes.} While the term "zero-day" is not explicitly used to describe the model's performance in the excerpts, the system is defined as an \textbf{anomaly detection system} designed to handle the \textbf{"ever-evolving nature of network threats"}. It is specifically built to detect \textbf{"new assaults"} and abnormalities that traditional systems might miss.
\item \textbf{What training paradigm does it use and how can it be categorized?} The article categorizes the LD-BiHGA system as \textbf{supervised learning-based}. However, it is a hybrid architecture that leverages \textbf{unsupervised learning} for dimensionality reduction (via a denoising autoencoder) and \textbf{semi-supervised learning} for the GAN-based feature extraction.
\item \textbf{Does the article address the handling of imbalanced datasets?} \textbf{Yes.} The abstract states the system \textbf{"effectively handles real-time unbalanced data"}. It achieves this by using a \textbf{GAN (Generative Adversarial Network)}, which has the \textbf{"ability... to generate a new set of training samples"} to sustain performance even when attack data is scarce.
\item \textbf{Does the article deal with the concept drift phenomenon?} The specific term \textbf{"concept drift" is not mentioned} in the excerpts. However, the authors design the model to address the \textbf{"ever-evolving nature of network threats"} and the GAN generator is specifically used to learn \textbf{"patterns that evolve over time"}.
\item \textbf{Is interpretability considered an important aspect?} \textbf{No.} Interpretability is \textbf{not highlighted as a primary goal} or feature of the proposed model. While the authors use an attention mechanism, which is sometimes associated with interpretability in other literature, they do not apply specific techniques like LIME or SHAP to their own results in these excerpts.
\item 
\par
\textbf{What data preprocessing steps are used?} The preprocessing steps include:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{One-hot encoding} for categorical/symbolic features.
\item \textbf{Min-max normalization}.
\item \textbf{Spearman's cross-correlation} for feature selection.
\item \textbf{Denoising Autoencoder} for feature extraction (dimensionality reduction).
\item \textbf{Data Partitioning} (segregating benign and attack flows for the bi-channel model).

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Which datasets were used or tested in the study?} Three benchmark datasets were used: \textbf{KDDCUP 99}, \textbf{CICIDS2017}, and \textbf{InSDN}.
\item \textbf{Is there any special feature engineering method applied?} \textbf{Yes.} The model uses an \textbf{"Ensemble dimensionality reduction"} approach combining Spearman's correlation and a Denoising Autoencoder. It also employs a \textbf{bi-channel hybrid GAN} (LSTM generator and CNN discriminator) to extract class-specific, high-level feature representations.
\item 
\par
\textbf{What type of model is presented?} It is a \textbf{hybrid deep learning model} called \textbf{LD-BiHGA} (Low Dimensional Bi-channel Hybrid GAN Attention). It combines:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Denoising Autoencoder (DAE)}.
\item \textbf{Generative Adversarial Network (GAN)} using \textbf{LSTM} and \textbf{CNN}.
\item \textbf{Self-Attention mechanism} (LSTM-based).
\item \textbf{Fully Connected Neural Network (FCN/DNN)} for final classification.

\end{itemize}

\item 
\par
\textbf{What loss function is used?} The model utilizes two primary loss functions:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Mean Squared Error (MSE)} for the Denoising Autoencoder.
\item \textbf{Binary Cross-Entropy} for the Hybrid GAN and the final FCN classification.

\end{itemize}

\item \textbf{What training algorithm is applied?} The system uses the \textbf{Adam optimizer} for training the GAN and the classification layers. The training involves a \textbf{two-player mini-max game} for the GAN components and \textbf{k-fold cross-validation} (specifically 4 folds) for performance evaluation.

\end{itemize}

@article{presekalSpatioTemporalAdvancedPersistent2025,
  title = {Spatio-{{Temporal Advanced Persistent Threat Detection}} and {{Correlation}} for {{Cyber-Physical Power Systems Using Enhanced GC-LSTM}}},

  abstract = {Electrical power grids are vulnerable to cyber attacks, as seen in Ukraine in 2015, 2016, and 2022. These cyber attacks are classified as Advanced Persistent Threats (APTs) with potential disastrous consequences such as a total blackout. However, state-of-the-art intrusion detection systems are inadequate for APT detection owing to their stealthy nature and long-lasting persistence. Furthermore, they are ineffective as they focus on individual anomaly instances and overlook the correlation between attack instances. Therefore, this research proposes a novel method for spatio-temporal APT detection and correlation for cyber-physical power systems. It provides online situational awareness for power system operators to pinpoint system-wide anomaly locations in near real-time and preemptively mitigate APTs at an early stage before causing adverse impacts. We propose an Enhanced Graph Convolutional Long Short-Term Memory (EGC-LSTM) by using sequential and neural network filters to improve APT detection, correlation, and prediction. Control center and substation communication traffic is used to determine cyber anomalies using semi-supervised deep packet inspection and software-defined networking. Power grid circuit breaker status is used to determine physical anomalies. Cyber-physical anomalies are correlated in cyber-physical system integration matrix and EGC-LSTM. The EGC-LSTM outperforms existing state-of-the-art spatio-temporal deep learning models, achieving the lowest mean square error.},

@article{qiuMiningMultiScaleSpatialFrequency2025,
  title = {Mining {{Multi-Scale Spatial-Frequency Clues}} for {{Unsupervised Intrusion Detection}}},
  author = {Qiu, Chenyang and Nan, Guoshun and Zhang, Caiyi and Liang, Chenrui and Dai, Ruiqi and Yang, Hongchen and Liu, Shiyuan and Zhou, Zhili and Pei, Changhua and Tao, Xiaofeng},
  year = 2025,
  journal = {IEEE Transactions on Information Forensics and Security},
  volume = {20},
  pages = {11447--11461},
  issn = {1556-6013, 1556-6021},
  doi = {10.1109/TIFS.2025.3620090},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {Unsupervised network-based intrusion detection system (UNIDS) identifies suspicious traffic and alerts administrators without using any traffic labels. Existing Graph Convolutional Network (GCN)-based UNIDS approaches show great potential with collaboratively utilizing traffic features and network topologies. However, these methods suffer from an excessively high false-positive rate (FPR), e.g., 3.27\% FPR in a supervised NIDS approach, while increases dramatically to 19.37\% under the unsupervised setting. We reveal that the high FPR stems from a single-scale spatial-frequency learning paradigm, which blurs the distinction between benign and malicious traffic and misleads UNIDS systems. Therefore, we propose a Multi-scale Spatial-Frequency Intrusion Detection System (MSF-IDS) to mitigate the high FPR. Specifically, we propose multi-scale frequency encoders, thereby differentiating abnormal feature patterns. Then we propose NAPH as a spatial encoder, mining intrinsic abnormal topology patterns by tracking tens of thousands of evolving traffic nodes. To the best of our knowledge, NAPH takes the first step toward differentiable persistent homology analysis over dynamic network data. We also develop an executable application for NAPH to provide easy-access visualization insights. Finally, a self-supervised representation augmentor and an intrusion detector are proposed to refine and highlight the attack-specific information. Equipped with MSF-IDS, administrators effectively identify the unknown attack traffic, freeing security staff from labor-intensive engineering. Extensive experiments demonstrate the superiority of MSF-IDS, including binary classification, multi-classification, online intrusion detection, and visualized discussions. Our codes, datasets, and an executable application are available at https://github.com/qcydm/MSF-IDS.},
  copyright = {https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/Xplorehelp/downloads/license-information/IEEE.html},
  annotation = {Access to Full Paper is not available},

\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Alkalmass\'ag zero-day t\'amad\'asok \'eszlel\'es\'ere:} Igen, a cikk szerint a megold\'as alkalmas az \textbf{ismeretlen t\'amad\'asi forgalom (unknown attack traffic)} azonos\'it\'as\'ara, ami lehet\H ov\'e teszi a rendszergazd\'ak sz\'am\'ara az \'uj t\'ipus\'u fenyeget\'esek elleni v\'edekez\'est.

\item \textbf{\'Ertelmezhet\H os\'eg (interpretability):} Igen, az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg megjelenik a munk\'aban: a szerz\H ok kifejlesztettek egy \textbf{futtathat\'o alkalmaz\'ast a NAPH-hoz}, amely \textbf{vizualiz\'aci\'os betekint\'est (visualization insights)} ny\'ujt a felhaszn\'al\'oknak. \item \textbf{Multi-scale frequency encoding:} t\"obb l\'ept\'ek\H u frekvencia-k\'odol\'as a jellemz\H omint\'ak megk\"ul\"onb\"oztet\'es\'ere.
\item \textbf{Persistent homology analysis:} perzisztens homol\'ogia elemz\'es a dinamikus h\'al\'ozati adatokon a topol\'ogiai mint\'ak kinyer\'es\'ehez.
\item \textbf{Self-supervised representation augmentation:} \"onfel\"ugyelt reprezent\'aci\'o-er\H os\'it\'es a t\'amad\'as-specifikus inform\'aci\'ok kiemel\'es\'ere.

@article{rabbiComparativeAnalysisMachine2025,
  title = {A Comparative Analysis of Machine Learning Techniques for Detecting Probing Attack with {{SHAP}} Algorithm},
  author = {Rabbi, Fazla and Ibne Hossain, Niamat Ullah and Das, Saikat},
  year = 2025,
  month = may,
  journal = {Expert Systems with Applications},
  volume = {271},

  issn = {09574174},
  doi = {10.1016/j.eswa.2025.126718},
  urldate = {2026-03-04},
  abstract = {Internet-based network safety has transformed into a major global issue because of the rising dependency of people, businesses, and countries. Therefore, it is vitally important for individuals to use an intrusion detection system (IDS) that may protect computer networks from potential threats and data leakage. It is gradually improving with the growth of machine learning (ML) methods. In this research, we present an intrusion detection method utilizing several ML algorithms to detect probe attacks using the NSL-KDD dataset. This attack targets the potential weak point of the network to get an idea about the structure and vulnerabilities. Therefore, the objective of this study is to build a best-performed ML model that provides the lowest possible false positive rate, the lowest run time, and the highest possible F1 score. To that end, different ML models have been developed, such as Neural Network (NN), Random Forest (RF), K-Nearest Neighbor (KNN), Bagging Classifier, and Extreme Gradient Boosting Classifier (XGBoost). Furthermore, cross-validation, sampling methods, and hyperparameter tuning were conducted on those ML models to improve their efficiency. Moreover, a SHAP algorithm has been conducted to interpret the prediction of the ML models and figure out the most influential features that affect cyber-attack detection. We performed a comparative analysis among all ML models that we built, and it shows the XGBoost model is the best-performing model that outperformed all other models with a 92.93\% F1 score, the lowest 2.35\% false positive rate, and with a minimum runtime of 13 s. Furthermore, our feature importance study shows that the ``src\_bytes'' or source bytes feature, which offers information on the number of bytes an attacker sends to each port during the scanning phase, has the greatest influence on identifying probing attacks. Compared to existing research on probe attack detection, our proposed model demonstrates an excellent example in terms of fast and accurate anomaly detection with negligible false positives. Additionally, it outperforms traditional probe attack detection in terms of computational efficiency and handling diverse network scenarios in the presence of high traffic volumes and dynamic environments.},

  abstract = {Network Intrusion Detection Systems (NIDS) are critical in ensuring the security of connected computer systems by actively detecting and preventing unauthorized activities and malicious attacks. Machine learning based NIDS models leverage algorithms that learn from historical network traffic data to identify patterns and anomalies to capture complex relationships. The primary objective of this research is to generate tags and descriptions for the packets that are difficult to classify by the NIDS. Most NIDS datasets that are publicly available have focused on flow data, offering aggregated information about network connections, and have played a crucial role in enabling researchers and network security professionals to design and develop flow-based NIDS solutions. While flow records provide valuable information for detecting network-level anomalies and attacks, they do not consider packet-level information and payload content. In this research, we propose a packet-level approach for NIDS that leverages the flow information with the packet header fields and payload. To facilitate this research, we have curated a comprehensive Packet-level dataset constructed by extracting the network packet captures from two widely used flow-level datasets, namely CIC-IDS2017 and UNSW-NB15. Recent advancements in natural language processing have demonstrated the effectiveness of Transformer-based models in handling sequence data with tasks such as token classification and text generation. We have adapted this technology to NIDS to extract key features and characteristics of the header and payload in the context of various attacks. Unlike traditional classification methods that assign predefined labels to network packets, this method focuses on generating tags based on the packet signature that explains the packet content and potential risks through high variability and explainability based on cosine semantic similarity scores of embeddings. The tags and descriptions offer network security professionals a tool to comprehend suspicious packets with an unfamiliar or potentially malicious signature, assess their nature, and help make informed decisions promptly. For the purposes of open science, all the code related to this paper is shared publicly.},

\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Is the solution presented in the article suitable for detecting zero-day attacks?} The solution is primarily designed for \textbf{explainability} rather than detection itself,. While it is intended to function within an \textbf{Open-World Recognition (OWR)} framework that handles novel attacks, the specific module proposed generates tags and descriptions for packets that a NIDS has already identified as \textbf{unknown or difficult to classify},,. It is suitable for describing "unknown activity" that the system was not trained to recognize,.
\item \textbf{What training paradigm does it use, and how can it be categorized?} The article utilizes a \textbf{supervised learning} paradigm by fine-tuning pre-trained transformer models (BERT and Falcon) on large, labeled network intrusion datasets,,.
\item \textbf{Does the article address the handling of imbalanced datasets? If yes, how?} \textbf{Yes.} The authors address class imbalance by applying \textbf{oversampling} to minority classes and \textbf{undersampling} to majority classes until each class in the training dataset contains exactly \textbf{100,000 packets}.
\item \textbf{Does the article deal with the concept drift phenomenon? \par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Natural Language Tag Generation}: Generating 10 descriptive tags per packet,.
\item \textbf{Text Generation (Falcon-7B)}: Producing human-readable sentences explaining packet headers, payloads, and tags,.
\item \textbf{Semantic Cluster Analysis}: Grouping packets based on \textbf{BERT embeddings} to identify patterns,,.
\item \textbf{Cosine Semantic Similarity}: Used to measure the relevance of generated tags,.

\end{itemize}

\textbf{What data preprocessing steps are used?} The preprocessing steps include:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Extraction} of packet-level information from PCAP files using the \textbf{Payload-Byte tool},.
\item \textbf{Conversion} of hexadecimal byte values to \textbf{decimal (0-255)}.
\item \textbf{Filtering} to include only \textbf{TCP/IP packets}.
\item \textbf{Selection} of packets containing a \textbf{payload}, excluding connection control packets.
\item \textbf{Removal of specific headers} (e.g., source/destination IPs, TCP sequence numbers, checksums) to focus on tag generation.
\item \textbf{Merging} of datasets and \textbf{dropping duplicate records}.
\item \textbf{Tokenization} using the \textbf{BERT tokenizer},.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Is there any special feature engineering method applied?} The authors applied \textbf{n-gram word extraction} (up to 10-grams) from their network security corpus,. They also converted payloads to \textbf{readable ASCII} and extracted English words to provide additional context for the text generation model.
\item \textbf{What type of model is presented?} The article presents \textbf{Transformer-based models}, specifically \textbf{distilbert-base-cased (BERT)} for embedding and tag generation and \textbf{Falcon-7B} for text generation,,. It also mentions using \textbf{Random Forest} for payload feature importance analysis.
\item \textbf{What loss function is used?} The article reports reaching specific \textbf{training loss values} (e.g., 0.03385 for BERT and 0.7297 for Falcon), but it \textbf{does not explicitly name the specific mathematical loss function} (such as Cross-Entropy) used during training,.
\item \textbf{What training algorithm is applied?} The training involves \textbf{batch processing} and fine-tuning for \textbf{three epochs}. For the Falcon model, the authors used \textbf{Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning (PEFT)} combined with \textbf{Quantized Low-Rank Adapters (QLoRA)}, employing \textbf{4-bit NormalFloat (NF4)} data types and \textbf{double quantization}.

\end{itemize}

@article{ramuAttackClassificationNetwork2024,
  title = {Attack Classification in Network Intrusion Detection System Based on Optimization Strategy and Deep Learning Methodology},
  author = {Ramu, Ch.Kodanda and Rao, T. Srinivasa and Rao, E. Uma Shankar},
  year = 2024,
  month = feb,
  journal = {Multimedia Tools and Applications},

  issn = {1573-7721},

  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {In recent times, network-based applications have rapidly grown in the field of information and communication technology(ICT), which enables individuals and organizations to connect and share their sensitive information seamlessly. The security of these network-based applications is imperative to avoid cyber-attacks during the exchange of sensitive information. The identification of anomalies in network events can be highly challenging due to the complex nature of traffic flows. To solve the challenges, network intrusion detection system (NIDS) technology is used; any network can profit from this system because it can monitor traffic and detect any irregularities. The existing NIDS systems driven by machine learning models do not provide sufficient ability to handle heterogeneous data and realize performance degradation while detecting some types of attacks. Therefore, this paper proposes an innovative meta-heuristic optimization and deep learning-based methodology for improving the performance of NIDS systems. Initially, the raw captured traffic data is fed into the pre-processing phase to attain data standardization and data balancing. Further, an extended Pelican Optimization algorithm (Ex-Pel) is employed to select the set of features from the pre-processed data optimally. Finally, the Self-Attention Assisted Weighted Auto Encoder (SAttn\_WAE) is executed to detect the attacks precisely through the set of optimal features. The execution of the proposed methodology is carried out in the Python platform, and performance evaluation is done using accuracy, precision, recall, FAR, and F1-score metrics. The proposed model achieved an accuracy of 99.23\%, precision of 99.78\%, FAR of 0.67\%, recall of 99.13\%, and F1-score of 99.32\%, which is comparatively better than existing models.},

\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Fontos szempont-e az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg?} A forr\'asok \textbf{nem t\'argyalj\'ak} az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eget vagy magyar\'azhat\'os\'agot (pl. LIME haszn\'alat\'at). B\'ar a SMOTE-ot eml\'iti a sz\"oveg, azt az adatok kiegyens\'ulyoz\'as\'ara, nem pedig az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg jav\'it\'as\'ara haszn\'alj\'ak.

\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{One-hot k\'odol\'as} (kategorikus adatok numerikuss\'a alak\'it\'asa).
\item \textbf{Null \'ert\'ekek kezel\'ese}.
\item \textbf{Szimbolikus adatok Boolean form\'atumba} alak\'it\'asa.
\item \textbf{Z-score normaliz\'al\'as} (standardiz\'al\'as: 0 \'atlag \'es 1 sz\'or\'as).
\item \textbf{Adatkiegyens\'ulyoz\'as} (SMOTE \'es RUS haszn\'alat\'aval).

\item \textbf{Mely adatk\'eszleteket haszn\'alt\'ak a vizsg\'alat sor\'an?} A kutat\'as sor\'an a \textbf{KDD CUP 99}, az \textbf{NSL-KDD} \'es az \textbf{UNSW-NB15} adatk\'eszleteket haszn\'alt\'ak tesztel\'esre \'es valid\'al\'asra.

\item \textbf{Milyen t\'ipus\'u modellt mutatnak be?} A bemutatott modell egy \textbf{m\'ely tanul\'asi} architekt\'ura, pontosabban egy \textbf{Self-Attention Assisted Weighted Auto Encoder (SAttn\_WAE)}, azaz \"onfigyelemmel t\'amogatott s\'ulyozott autoenk\'oder.
\item \textbf{Milyen vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt (loss function) haszn\'alnak?} A modell els\H osorban az \textbf{\'atlagos n\'egyzetes hib\'at (Mean Square Error - MSE)} haszn\'alja a rekonstrukci\'os hiba m\'er\'es\'ere. Ezt kieg\'esz\'itik a \textbf{Kullback--Leibler (KL) divergenci\'aval}, amely regul\'aci\'os tagk\'ent szolg\'al a ritka (sparse) k\'odol\'as el\'er\'es\'ehez, valamint s\'ulycsillap\'it\'asi elemekkel az overfitting elker\"ul\'ese \'erdek\'eben.
\item \textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'asi algoritmust alkalmaznak?} A tan\'it\'as sor\'an \textbf{moh\'o r\'etegenk\'enti el\H otan\'it\'ast (greedy layer-wise pre-training)} alkalmaznak, amelyet egy \textbf{hibatov\'abb\'it\'asi (error feed-forward) alap\'u finomhangol\'as} k\"ovet az optim\'alis param\'eterek el\'er\'es\'eig.

\end{itemize}

  journal = {IEEE Access},
  volume = {13},
  pages = {139141--139154},
  issn = {2169-3536},
  doi = {10.1109/ACCESS.2025.3596179},
  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {The widespread adoption of encryption in computer networks has made detecting malicious traffic, especially at network perimeters, increasingly challenging. As packet contents are concealed, traditional monitoring techniques such as Deep Packet Inspection (DPI) become ineffective. Consequently, researchers have started employing data-driven methods based on Machine and Deep Learning (ML \& DL) to identify malicious behavior even from encrypted traffic, typically within Anomaly-based Network Intrusion Detection Systems (A-NIDS). Existing approaches rely heavily on supervised learning, which requires large volumes of labeled benign and malicious traffic. Generating these labels is time-consuming, error-prone, and often requires expert knowledge. In this paper, we propose a semi-supervised learning framework that leverages Self-Supervised Learning (SSL) to learn discriminative representations from unlabeled network traffic. We design a novel pretext task that predicts important masked features, enabling the model to capture meaningful structure in the data. The learned representations are fine-tuned with minimal labeled data using a Custom-Autoencoder (Custom-AE) classifier. Experimental results show that the representation learned from our proposed pretext task outperforms the best competing method in terms of accuracy by 3.41\% on UNSW-NB15 (NB15) and 1.53\% on CSE-CIC-IDS2018 (CSE18) when evaluated using linear probing. When fine-tuned with the Custom-AE on only 100 benign and 10 malicious samples, it achieves 83.51\% (NB15) and 87.43\% (CSE18) accuracy, representing gains of 4.55\% and 5.08\% over the initial features, respectively. This demonstrates stronger suitability for label-scarce real-world scenarios compared to existing approaches.},

  note = {A v\'alaszok a megadott forr\'asdokumentum ("Self-Supervised Learning Meets Custom Autoencoder Classifier") r\'eszletei alapj\'an k\'esz\"ultek. A forr\'as a cikk jelent\H os r\'eszeit (absztrakt, bevezet\'es, m\'odszertan, k\'is\'erletek) tartalmazza, \'igy a v\'alaszok ezen \textbf{sz\"ovegr\'eszleteken alapulnak}.
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Alkalmas-e a megold\'as zero-day t\'amad\'asok \'eszlel\'es\'ere?} Igen. A cikk kifejti, hogy az anom\'alia-alap\'u h\'al\'ozatbiztons\'agi rendszerek (A-NIDS) -- szemben a szignat\'ura-alap\'u rendszerekkel -- k\'epesek a \textbf{kor\'abban nem l\'atott (previously unseen) t\'amad\'asok} azonos\'it\'as\'ara a viselked\'esi mint\'azatok elemz\'ese r\'ev\'en.
\item \textbf{Milyen tanul\'asi paradigm\'at haszn\'al?} A keretrendszer \textbf{f\'elig fel\"ugyelt tanul\'ast (semi-supervised learning)} alkalmaz. El\H osz\"or egy \textbf{\"onfelt\'ar\'o tanul\'asi (self-supervised learning - SSL)} f\'azist haszn\'al a reprezent\'aci\'ok kinyer\'es\'ere, majd ezt egy minim\'alis mennyis\'eg\H u c\'imk\'ezett adatot ig\'enyl\H o finomhangol\'asi szakasz k\"oveti.
\item \textbf{Kezeli-e az imbolans (kiegyens\'ulyozatlan) adatk\'eszleteket?} Igen, a cikk foglalkozik ezzel a k\'erd\'essel. A szerz\H ok a k\'is\'erleti be\'all\'it\'asokn\'al \textbf{r\'etegzett v\'eletlenszer\H u feloszt\'ast (stratified random split)} alkalmaztak a CSE18 adatk\'eszleten, hogy \textbf{kiegyens\'ulyozott tan\'it\'o- \'es tesztk\'eszleteket} hozzanak l\'etre. Emellett a m\'odszert kifejezetten a val\'os vil\'agra jellemz\H o "c\'imkeszeg\'eny" forgat\'ok\"onyvekre tervezt\'ek.
\item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk a concept drift jelens\'eggel?} A megadott forr\'asr\'eszletek \textbf{nem tesznek eml\'it\'est} a concept drift jelens\'egr\H ol.
\item \textbf{Fontos szempont-e az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg (interpretability)?} Az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg \textbf{nem k\"ozponti eleme} a javasolt modellnek. A szerz\H ok megeml\'itik az \'ertelmezhet\H o TabNet modellt mint \"osszehasonl\'it\'asi alapot, illetve megjegyzik, hogy a fa-alap\'u modellek (pl. A LIME-ot nem eml\'itik, a SMOTE-ot pedig csak a kapcsol\'od\'o munk\'ak k\"oz\"ott sorolj\'ak fel
\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'eseket haszn\'alnak?} A k\"ovetkez\H o l\'ep\'eseket alkalmazz\'ak:
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item Kategorikus adatok \'atalak\'it\'asa \textbf{One-hot k\'odol\'assal}
\item \textbf{Normaliz\'al\'as (Min-max sk\'al\'az\'as)} a 0-1 tartom\'anyba
\item .\textbf{Pearson-f\'ele korrel\'aci\'os egy\"utthat\'o} kisz\'am\'it\'asa a jellemz\H ok k\"oz\"otti kapcsolatok elemz\'es\'ehez
\item A \textbf{z\'er\'o varianci\'aj\'u jellemz\H ok} (melyek nem hordoznak inform\'aci\'ot) azonos\'it\'asa \'es elt\'avol\'it\'asa

\end{enumerate}

@article{renROENUniversalDynamic2025,
  title = {{{ROEN}}: {{Universal}} Dynamic Topology-Adaptive Evolution Model for Multi-Modal Mixed Network Traffic Detection},
  shorttitle = {{{ROEN}}},
  author = {Ren, Linghao and Wang, Sijia and Zhong, Shengwei and Li, Yiyuan and Tang, Bo},
  year = 2025,

  abstract = {Modern network traffic detection systems face significant challenges in accurately classifying sophisticated cyber attacks. Traditional approaches relying on static traffic features (e.g., port numbers and packet sizes) prove inadequate for capturing the dynamic topological evolution inherent in Advanced Persistent Threats (APTs) and complex intrusions. This limitation stems from overlooking temporal correlations and structural dynamics within network traffic flows. Our investigation identifies this oversight as the primary cause of suboptimal performance in multi-modal traffic recognition, hybrid attack detection, and analysis with incomplete or anomalous data. To address this critical gap, we propose a novel dynamic topology-based method that quantifies evolving network structures through traffic pattern distribution transformations. Departing from traditional attention-based anomaly detection paradigms, our streamlined architecture introduces a dual-thread framework with multi-level feature fusion. This innovative design effectively integrates explicit statistical features with implicit dynamic topology information, achieving improved intrusion detection accuracy while reducing computational complexity. By modeling intrinsic interactions between statistical and topological characteristics, our method reveals latent intrusion patterns through three key innovations: (1) quantitative modeling of network topological dynamics, (2) a lightweight dual-thread architecture for efficient feature fusion, and (3) robust detection mechanisms under data scarcity. To our knowledge, this represents the first universal network intrusion detection framework that efficiently combines dynamic topological analysis with conventional statistical features. Extensive benchmark evaluations demonstrate state-of-the-art performance with significant improvements in AUC (5.8\%) and macro-averaged AUC (7.2\%) over existing methods, while maintaining a 23\% lower computational overhead. Our solution establishes a foundation for next-generation intrusion detection systems, providing a generalizable and resource-efficient approach to counter evolving cyber threats. The code and dataset are available at https://github.com/vjkgll/ROEN.git.},
  langid = {english},
  note = {Az al\'abbi v\'alaszok a megadott forr\'asdokumentum ("ROEN: Universal dynamic topology-adaptive evolution model for multi-modal mixed network traffic detection") tartalma \'es metaadatai alapj\'an k\'esz\"ultek. \begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Alkalmas-e a megold\'as zero-day t\'amad\'asok \'eszlel\'es\'ere?} Igen. A szerz\H ok a k\"ovetkeztet\'esben kifejezetten eml\'itik, hogy a j\"ov\H obeli munka r\'esze lesz a ROEN teljes\'itm\'eny\'enek tov\'abbi vizsg\'alata specifikusan \textbf{zero-day t\'amad\'asok} eset\'en
\item \textbf{Milyen tanul\'asi paradigm\'at haszn\'al?} A modell \textbf{fel\"ugyelt tanul\'ast (supervised learning)} alkalmaz. A k\'is\'erleti r\'eszben le\'irj\'ak, hogy az adatok 80\%-\'at haszn\'alt\'ak tan\'it\'asra (training set), \'es a modellt \textbf{t\"obboszt\'alyos oszt\'alyoz\'asi feladatra} (multi-class classification) k\'esz\'itett\'ek fel c\'imk\'ezett benchmark adatk\'eszletek seg\'its\'eg\'evel
\item \textbf{Kezeli-e az imbolans (kiegyens\'ulyozatlan) adatk\'eszleteket?} Igen. A cikk foglalkozik a "s\'ulyos minta-imbalansz" (severe sample imbalance) probl\'em\'aj\'aval. \item \textbf{Foglalkozik-e a cikk a concept drift jelens\'eggel?} Igen. A ROEN architekt\'ura \textbf{"concept drift rezisztenci\'aval"} rendelkezik, amit Lyapunov-stabilit\'aselemz\'essel garant\'alnak. A k\'is\'erletek sor\'an k\"ul\"on vizsg\'alt\'ak a modell stabilit\'as\'at 120 \'or\'anyi folyamatos concept drift ut\'an, \'es azt tal\'alt\'ak, hogy a teljes\'itm\'enycs\"okken\'es minim\'alis ({$<$}2\%) maradt
\item \textbf{Fontos szempont-e az \'ertelmezhet\H os\'eg (interpretability)?} Igen, a cikk tartalmaz egy dedik\'alt \textbf{SHAP-alap\'u interpret\'alhat\'os\'agi modult} (Dual-Shapley interpretability framework), amely SHAP-\'ert\'ek dekompoz\'ici\'o seg\'its\'eg\'evel sz\'amszer\H us\'iti a jellemz\H ok k\"oz\"otti interakci\'okat
\item 
\par
\textbf{Milyen adat-el\H ok\'esz\'it\'esi l\'ep\'eseket haszn\'alnak?}
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{H\'al\'ozati interakci\'os gr\'af (}Gt\hspace{0pt}\textbf{) \'ep\'it\'ese:} Az IP-c\'imek k\"oz\"otti interakci\'ok id\H oir\'any\'itott gr\'aff\'a alak\'it\'asa
\item \textbf{Id\H oablak alap\'u monitoroz\'as:} A forgalom figyel\'ese specifikus id\H oablakokban
\item J\textbf{ellemz\H ok sz\H ur\'ese \'es dimenzi\'ocs\"okkent\'ese:} MLP alkalmaz\'asa a zaj cs\"okkent\'es\'ere \'es a dimenzi\'osz\'am korai kontroll\'al\'as\'ara
\item \textbf{Normaliz\'al\'as:} A gr\'af szomsz\'eds\'agi \'es foksz\'am-m\'atrixainak normaliz\'al\'asa az inform\'aci\'oterjed\'es stabiliz\'al\'as\'ahoz.

\end{enumerate}

Emellett egy \textbf{du\'alsz\'al\'u rekurz\'iv f\'uzi\'os (DTRF)} mechanizmust haszn\'alnak az explicit statisztikai \'es implicit topol\'ogiai jellemz\H ok integr\'al\'as\'ara
\item \textbf{Milyen t\'ipus\'u modellt mutatnak be?} A modell neve \textbf{ROEN} (Recursive Optimal Embedding Network), amely egy \textbf{m\'elytanul\'asi (Deep Learning)} architekt\'ura. Ez egy \textbf{du\'alsz\'al\'u} keretrendszer, amely \"otv\"ozi a \textbf{Gr\'af Neur\'alis H\'al\'ozatokat (GNN)} a topol\'ogiai strukt\'ura kinyer\'es\'ehez \'es a \textbf{Rekurrens Neur\'alis H\'al\'ozatokat (RNN)} a dinamikus mint\'azatok \'es id\H obeli f\"ugg\H os\'egek k\"ovet\'es\'ehez
\item \textbf{Milyen vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt (loss function) haszn\'alnak?} Mivel a feladat t\"obboszt\'alyos oszt\'alyoz\'as, a modell \textbf{keresztentr\'opia vesztes\'egf\"uggv\'enyt (cross-entropy loss function)} haszn\'al.
\item \textbf{Milyen tan\'it\'asi algoritmust alkalmaznak?} \textbf{Gradiens-alap\'u tan\'it\'ast} alkalmaznak. A tanul\'asi r\'at\'at egys\'egesen \textbf{0,01}-re \'all\'itott\'ak be, a batch size pedig \textbf{1} volt az id\H obeli koherencia biztos\'it\'asa \'erdek\'eben. A modell stabilit\'as\'at Lyapunov-stabilit\'asi felt\'etelekkel optimaliz\'alj\'ak a tan\'it\'as sor\'an.

\end{itemize}},

@article{rezaeiFedLLMGuardFederatedLarge2025,
  title = {{{FedLLMGuard}}: {{A}} Federated Large Language Model for Anomaly Detection in {{5G}} Networks},
  shorttitle = {{{FedLLMGuard}}},
  author = {Rezaei, Hadiseh and Taheri, Rahim and Shojafar, Mohammad},
  year = 2025,

  urldate = {2026-03-03},
  abstract = {The rise of 5G networks has introduced new security challenges, particularly in detecting and mitigating dynamic traffic anomalies, adversarial threats, and large-scale cyberattacks. Traditional Machine learning-based Intrusion Detection Systems (IDS) suffer from high latency, poor adaptability, and privacy risks due to centralized data aggregation. To address these issues, we propose FedLLMGuard as a novel framework integrating Federated Learning (FL) with Large Language Models (LLM) for real-time privacy-preserving and adaptive anomaly detection in 5G networks. Our method uses FL for decentralized learning while utilizing LLM for contextual traffic analysis and interoperability. Moreover, our framework introduces the CorruptNet adversarial attack, a novel poisoning strategy targeting FL-based anomaly detection, ensuring robustness evaluation under adversarial conditions. We evaluate our model against three methods -- Random Forest, LSTM, and PSO Autoencoder LSTM -- using three benchmark datasets: TII-SSRC-23, CICDDoS2019, and NF-UNSW-NB15. Experimental results demonstrate that FedLLMGuard outperformed all models, whether subjected to the CorruptNet adversarial attack or not. Under the CorruptNet attack, it achieves an accuracy of 98.64\%, a false positive rate of only 2.16\%, and ultra-low detection latency (0.0113s). These results underscore FedLLMGuard's capacity to detect threats rapidly, mitigate attacks effectively, and sustain high accuracy while ensuring data privacy, making it a scalable and resource-efficient security solution for 5G networks.},
  langid = {english},
  note = {Based on the sources provided, here are the answers to your questions regarding the \textbf{FedLLMGuard} framework:
\par
\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Zero-day attacks:} The solution is designed to handle \textbf{evolving and novel threats} that traditional systems struggle to identify. The authors specifically utilized the \textbf{NF-UNSW-NB15 dataset}, which explicitly includes \textbf{zero-day exploits}, to evaluate the model's adaptability to previously unseen cyber threats.
\item \textbf{Training paradigm:} The model uses a \textbf{Federated Learning (FL)} paradigm. It is categorized as \textbf{supervised learning}, as it trains on labeled datasets (e.g., TII-SSRC-23, CICDDoS2019) and utilizes a \textbf{cross-entropy loss function} based on ground-truth labels.
\item \textbf{Imbalanced datasets:} \textbf{Yes}, the article addresses this. To handle class imbalance, the authors compute \textbf{dynamic class weights} before training, which are then applied to the \textbf{weighted cross-entropy loss function}.
\item \textbf{Concept drift:} The article acknowledges that traditional models cannot handle the \textbf{non-stationary nature of 5G traffic}, but it does not explicitly propose a specific technical solution labeled as "concept drift" correction for FedLLMGuard. Instead, it focuses on \textbf{real-time adaptability} to evolving threats through its LLM-based contextual analysis.
\item \textbf{Interpretability:} \textbf{Yes}, interpretability is a key aspect. The framework leverages \textbf{LLM-based contextual analysis} to interpret the semantic meaning behind network logs, which provides better \textbf{contextual insights} and explainability than numerical-only features. No specific techniques like LIME or SHAP are mentioned for this model.
\item 
\par
\textbf{Data preprocessing steps:}
\par
\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Data cleaning}: General cleaning of raw traffic logs.
\item \textbf{Temporal normalization}: Converting timestamps into numerical formats.
\item \textbf{Label encoding}: Applied to categorical features such as protocol types and IP addresses.
\item \textbf{Min-Max normalization}: Used to scale numerical attributes like packet size and flow duration.
\item \textbf{BERT-based tokenization}: Converting structured log data into \textbf{text embeddings} for the LLM.

\end{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Datasets used:} Three benchmark datasets were used: \textbf{TII-SSRC-23}, \textbf{CICDDoS2019}, and \textbf{NF-UNSW-NB15}.
\item \textbf{Feature engineering:} The authors apply a dual approach using \textbf{flow-based features} (e.g., entropy, flow duration, SYN/ACK flags) and \textbf{text-based features} (semantic embeddings generated via Tiny-BERT tokenization).
\item \textbf{Model type:} The architecture is a hybrid \textbf{Federated Learning} and \textbf{Large Language Model (LLM)} framework. Specifically, it utilizes \textbf{Tiny-BERT}, which is a \textbf{Transformer-based} lightweight model.
\item \textbf{Loss function:} The framework uses a \textbf{weighted Cross-Entropy loss}.
\item \textbf{Training algorithm:} The central server uses the \textbf{Federated Averaging (FedAvg)} algorithm for global aggregation. Local clients perform \textbf{gradient descent} using a \textbf{soft prompt tuning} strategy, updating only the soft prompts and the final transformer layers to remain resource-efficient.

\end{itemize}

@article{rookardUnsupervisedMachineLearning2025,
  title = {Unsupervised {{Machine Learning}} for {{Cybersecurity Anomaly Detection}} in {{Traditional}} and {{Software-Defined Networking Environments}}},

  abstract = {Cybersecurity has become a field of increasing importance within the past years, with the National Academy of Engineering most recently designating securing cyberspace as one of the fourteen Grand Challenges in Engineering in the 21st Century. Henceforth, it is imperative to design a robust anomaly detection and response approach that can identify and mitigate anomalous Internet traffic. In this study, we present several unsupervised/semi-supervised machine learning models to combat prolific anomalous data on a computer network. Specifically, we employ five unsupervised machine learning models, including a Generative Adversarial Network (GAN), Deep Belief Network (DBN), Restricted Boltzmann Machine (RBM), One-Class Support Vector Machine (OCSVM), and Isolation Forest (I-Forest). We use these models separately and, when applicable, combined together to examine their anomaly detection performance on three prominent traditional networking datasets, namely the KDD-Cup 99, NSL-KDD, and CIC-IDS2017 dataset, and implement these models within a software-defined networking and industrial Internet-of-Things environment using the DNP3 intrusion detection dataset. Furthermore, we investigate the generalizability of the models across the two datasets. Our results suggest I-Forest and DBN overall perform better than other models in traditional and software-defined networking environments; our GAN manages to outperform some benchmark models on the CIC-IDS2017 dataset.},
